# Places

Everything people come to Japan for, as things to do: the famous sights, the snowy side, the hot springs, the far north and the islands. Each one says how it fits our trip as it is now: on our route, a day out from a stop, a change to the route, or a trip of its own.

Areas are in order of how they fit our route: on it, a day out from it, would mean a change, another trip. The fit is worked out from the route and the journey times.

## Tokyo

**Fit:** On our route — Stop 1 · 4 nights · 20–24 Dec

**Prefecture:** Tokyo

### Tags

- food
- museums
- classics
- gentle

### Known for

- **The food**: Tonkatsu in a bathhouse, tempura since 1923, soba since 1884, the depachika food halls. · Place: Maisen Aoyama
- **Museums and gardens**: Nezu, Mori, the Edo-Tokyo Museum, the Edo-period gardens open on all our dates. · Place: Nezu Museum
- **Neighbourhoods**: Asakusa's temple lanes, Meiji Jingu's forest, Harajuku, Ginza, Shibuya at night. · Place: Meiji Jingu
- **Fuji from a tower**: On a clear December morning the mountain stands over the city from any high deck.

### Window

- Christmas illuminations run until 25 Dec: the arrival week is the lights week.
- Museums close 29 Dec–3 Jan; the Tokyo National Museum's main building is also shut 22–25 Dec, so it waits for 7–8 Jan.
- 1 Jan is the only dead day; department stores reopen on 2 Jan, Takashimaya on the 3rd.
- 9 Jan opens a three-day weekend, so the last night stays within 90 minutes of the airport.

**Feel:** Big, clean, quiet for its size, and easier than it looks: trains every three minutes, English on every sign, and a neighbourhood feel once you step off the main streets. December is dry, bright and cold, with the lights on until Christmas.

**Getting there:** Haneda is 30–40 minutes from the centre; Narita about an hour. Every base on the route is 1.5–4 hours away by train. Lifts everywhere; the metro's priority seats work with the maternity mark.

**For Fernanda:** The easiest base of the trip: flat, lifts, a hospital with English-speaking obstetrics (St Luke's) ten minutes from the centre.

**Region:** tokyo-fuji

**Do line:** Food, museums, gardens and old neighbourhoods

### Aliases

- Shibuya
- Shinjuku
- Asakusa
- Ginza
- Harajuku
- Tsukiji
- Ueno
- Roppongi
- Meiji Jingu

### Verdict

**Headline:** The big, calm city where the trip begins and ends.

**Text:** Tokyo is huge but easy, with trains every few minutes, English on every sign, old gardens, great museums, and food from tonkatsu to tempura. We arrive in the week of the Christmas lights, and come back at the end for the museums that were shut in December and a single act of kabuki.

### How long to stay and what is on

**Best:** 4 nights at the start and 2 at the end; 5 if Tokyo is a single stay

#### Date notes

- 20–25 Dec: the Christmas illuminations; most end on the 25th, Marunouchi runs on into February.
- Mon 21 Dec, Mon 28 Dec and Mon 4 Jan: most museums close, so these are the Tsukiji, Hama-rikyu and Asakusa days.
- 21–28 Dec: the Metropolitan Art Museum (the Musée d'Orsay show) is shut; 22–25 Dec the National Museum's main building is mostly shut, and the whole museum closes 26–31 Dec.
- 27 Dec–2 Jan: the Ghibli Museum is closed; tickets go on sale on the 10th of the month before.
- 29 Dec–1 Jan: the Edo-period city gardens close; Shinjuku Gyoen stays shut to 3 Jan.
- 29–31 Dec: Comiket fills Odaiba and the Toyosu area in the mornings.
- 31 Dec: the Zojo-ji bell at midnight under Tokyo Tower; most restaurants shut, so book a hotel dinner.
- 1 Jan: the one dead day, with department stores closed. 1–3 Jan Meiji Jingu and Senso-ji take millions: dawn, or not at all.
- 2 Jan: the Imperial Palace New Year greeting, the Ekiden start and the department-store sales; the Kabuki-za New Year programme opens. Takashimaya reopens on the 3rd.
- 31 Dec–4 Jan: the Toyosu market is shut and Tsukiji mostly; the first tuna auction is Tue 5 Jan.
- Fri 8 Jan: the Ueno museums are open to 20:00.
- 9 Jan opens a three-day weekend; the January sumo starts on the 10th, the day after we fly.

#### Nights

##### 1

**Why:** A flight night: pack, sleep, fly. It works at the very end, within easy reach of Haneda or Narita on 9 Jan; at the start it means moving on jet-lagged the next morning.

**Longer:** A second night buys one real Tokyo day: the Ueno museums in January, or Tsukiji and Hama-rikyu in December.

**Shorter:** A single night is the least Tokyo can be, with no slack if a train or the weather slips.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** No idle risk, and no room for anything but the airport.

##### 2

**Why:** One full day plus an arrival or packing evening. At the end of the trip that is the Musée d'Orsay show and the National Museum on 7–8 Jan; at the start it is one classic day, too little for the four first-timers.

**Longer:** A third night buys a second full day: Asakusa and the Edo-Tokyo Museum, or Kamakura.

**Shorter:** One night is a flight night, with no museums.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** Full but calm: one big thing a day and a last good dinner.

##### 3

**Why:** Two full days: two of the three classic days (Tsukiji and Hama-rikyu; Meiji Jingu, Harajuku and the Nezu Museum; Asakusa and the Edo-Tokyo Museum), or a generous end of trip with the Ueno museums and a shopping day.

**Longer:** A fourth night gives the first-timers the third classic day and lets jet lag land on a free morning.

**Shorter:** Two nights is one full day: fine at the end, too little at the start.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** Two big days and no spare one; a stay from 20 Dec makes Mon 21 the market and gardens day.

##### 4

**Why:** Arrival plus three full days: all three classic days, the free Fuji view from the Metropolitan Government towers, and the Christmas lights if it falls before the 25th. The right first stay for jet lag and four first-timers.

**Longer:** A fifth night buys a Kamakura or Nikko day, or a slow day for Fernanda and the parents.

**Shorter:** Three nights loses one classic day for the first-timers, and jet lag lands on a sightseeing day.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** No idle risk: Tokyo fills every day, indoors or out, with a rest after lunch.

##### 5

**Why:** The three classic days plus one more: a day trip to Kamakura, Nikko or the snow at Echigo-Yuzawa, or a teamLab Borderless and Mori Art Museum day. Over New Year it is the city with the most still open: Senso-ji, the towers, teamLab and the food halls from 2 Jan.

**Longer:** A sixth night is a second day trip or a proper rest day.

**Shorter:** Four nights drops the day trip but keeps all three classic days.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** Comfortable, with one easy day built in.

##### 6

**Why:** Enough for three classic days, two day trips and a rest, or for a December stay and a January stay together. If it covers 29 Dec–3 Jan, museums and the city gardens shut and the big shrines are crushed, so those days lean on Mori, teamLab, the towers and the food halls.

**Longer:** A seventh night is another day trip or a slow neighbourhood day; Tokyo never runs out.

**Shorter:** Five nights loses one day trip or the rest day.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** Easy to fill; the risk is a trip that feels like one city, not idle days.

##### 7

**Why:** A week: the classic days, Kamakura and Nikko, the Ghibli Museum or a sumo stable morning, and real rest. If it spans New Year, 1 Jan is a dead day and 29 Dec–3 Jan leans on shrines at dawn, towers and teamLab.

**Longer:** An eighth night only makes sense as a base for more day trips, at the cost of somewhere new.

**Shorter:** Six nights loses one day trip or rest day.

**Idle:** some

**Note:** Plenty to do, but Pedro and Fernanda know the city; a week here is a week less somewhere new.

### Places to visit (40)

- **Tsukiji Outer Market** (market, main) — Street food and knife shops; pairs with Hama-rikyu and Ginza on museum-closed Mondays
  - Hours: Mornings
  - For Fernanda: moderate, Crowded, standing
  - **Famous for:** Tokyo's old fish-market district, now a street-food and kitchenware market of about 400 shops.
  - **Expect:** The wholesale market moved to Toyosu in 2018, but the outer lanes kept the grilled scallops, tamagoyaki, knife shops and tea stalls. Walk, snack and shop for about 90 minutes. It is busiest from 9 to 11 and packed on weekends and the last days of December, when Tokyo shops for New Year. Many shops close Sundays and Wednesdays. Cooked skewers and egg rolls are fine for her; skip the raw bowls.
  - **Reputation:** classic
  - **Links:**
  -   - **Official site**: Url: https://www.tsukiji.or.jp/english/
  -   - **japan-guide**: Url: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e3021.html
- **Toyosu tuna auction tour** (experience, main) — The auction floor at 5am with a guide
  - Hours: About 05:00, 3 h; market closed Wed, Sun, holidays
  - For Fernanda: moderate, Very early, cold, standing
  - Price: From US$139
  - Booking: Weeks ahead
  - Source: https://www.headout.com/walking-tours/tokyo-toyosu-tuna-auction-observation-walking-tour-with-breakfast-reservation-e-48830/
  - **Famous for:** Watching the tuna auction at the world's biggest fish market from a glass gallery before dawn.
  - **Expect:** The auction runs from about 5:30 to 6:30 on the fourth floor of a very clean, very cold building. You stand behind glass and watch the bidders' hand signs; a guide explains who is buying what, and most tours end with breakfast at the market's sushi or tempura counters. You need to leave the hotel around 4:30. The market is closed Wednesdays, Sundays and over New Year.
  - **Reputation:** real
  - **Links:**

- **Koishikawa Korakuen** (garden, short) — Wintersweet in January
  - Hours: 9:00-17:00
  - Closed: {"start":"2026-12-29","end":"2027-01-01","note":"Metropolitan gardens closed","source":null}

  - Price: ¥300

The humpback bridges and uneven stones are the only awkward part, and they can be skipped. Closed 29 December to 1 January.

  - Hours: 9:00-16:00

- **Meiji Jingu** (sight, main) — Shrine in a forest; pair with Harajuku
  - Hours: Sunrise to sunset
  - For Fernanda: moderate, Long gravel path
  - Source: https://www.gotokyo.org/en/spot/ev110/index.html
  - **Famous for:** Tokyo's grandest Shinto shrine, inside a 100,000-tree forest next to Harajuku.
  - **Expect:** From the Harajuku gate it is a ten-minute walk on wide gravel under tall trees to the main courtyard, where you can watch offerings and often a wedding procession on weekends. Free; the inner garden costs 500 yen. Allow an hour, then step out into Omotesando. Early January is the busiest week of the year here, with millions of New Year visitors through 3 January; by 7 January it has calmed but is still lively. Go early.

- **Nezu Museum** (museum, short) — Tea-culture art and a hillside garden; winter tea gathering display to 17 Jan
  - Hours: 10:00-17:00
  - Closed weekdays: Mon
  - For Fernanda: moderate, Garden has slopes
  - Booking: Timed online booking required
  - Source: https://www.nezu-muse.or.jp/en/index.html
  - **Famous for:** A private collection of East Asian art in a Kengo Kuma building, with a hillside garden of teahouses.
  - **Expect:** Small, calm and well lit: one or two galleries of lacquer, bronzes and tea utensils, then a wooded garden with stone lanterns, ponds and four teahouses. About 90 minutes with a coffee in the glass-walled cafe. The winter tea-gathering display runs to 17 January. Timed tickets must be booked online, usually 1,500 yen. The garden paths slope and have steps; the galleries are level. Closed Mondays and over New Year.

- **Edo-Tokyo Museum** (museum, main) — Reopened March 2026: life-size old Tokyo
  - Hours: 9:30-17:30
  - Closed weekdays: Mon
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Price: ¥800, ¥400 over 65
  - Source: https://www.edo-tokyo-museum.or.jp/en/
  - **Famous for:** Life-size reconstructions of old Edo and Tokyo, including a full-scale half of Nihonbashi bridge.
  - **Expect:** Reopened in 2026 after a four-year renovation. You cross the wooden bridge into a hall of models: a kabuki theatre front, merchant houses, a Meiji newspaper office, postwar apartments. Two to three hours go easily, with lifts between floors and plenty of seating. Entry about 800 yen, cheaper over 65. Closed Mondays. It sits next to the sumo stadium in Ryogoku, so pair it with the Hokusai Museum or a chanko lunch.
  - **Reputation:** classic
  - **Links:**

- **Sumida Hokusai Museum** (museum, short) — Hokusai prints next to the Edo-Tokyo Museum
  - Hours: 9:30-17:30
  - Closed weekdays: Mon
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Source: https://hokusai-museum.jp/?lang=en
  - **Famous for:** Hokusai's woodblock prints in a mirrored aluminium building in the district where he was born.
  - **Expect:** A small museum: one permanent floor with touch screens, a life-size model of the artist at work and rotating originals, plus a temporary show. The Great Wave is usually shown as a high-quality facsimile with originals rotated in for conservation. An hour is plenty; 400 yen for the permanent exhibition. Closed Mondays. A seven-minute walk from the Edo-Tokyo Museum, so do both in one afternoon.

- **teamLab Planets** (museum, short) — Immersive art walked barefoot through water
  - For Fernanda: high, teamLab says pregnant women aren't permitted in some areas
  - Source: https://www.teamlab.art/e/planets/
  - **Famous for:** Barefoot digital art: wading through knee-deep water with koi projected around your legs.
  - **Expect:** You leave shoes and socks in a locker, borrow shorts and walk through water, mirror rooms and a garden of hanging orchids. About 90 minutes, with timed tickets of about 3,800 yen. One of the most photographed places in Tokyo, and most people say it lives up to it. teamLab's own notice says pregnant visitors are not permitted in some areas, and wet floors are slippery, so this one is for the others; Borderless is the dry alternative.
  - **Reputation:** both
  - **Links:**
  -   - **Official site**: Url: https://www.teamlab.art/e/planets/
  -   - **japan-guide: Toyosu**: Url: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e3013.html
- **Ghibli Museum** (museum, short) — Studio Ghibli's museum in Mitaka
  - Closed weekdays: Tue
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Booking: Lawson tickets on the 10th of the previous month

  - **Famous for:** Studio Ghibli's own museum in Mitaka, designed by Miyazaki, with an exclusive short film.
  - **Expect:** Small, hand-made and deliberately maze-like: original drawings, a room on how animation works, a cat bus for children, a short film in a tiny cinema and the robot soldier on the roof (stairs only). No photos inside. Two hours is typical. Tickets go on sale on the 10th of the previous month and sell out within minutes, so set an alarm. Closed Tuesdays. Twenty minutes by train from Shinjuku, then a walk through Inokashira Park.

  - **Expect:** A lift goes straight to the 45th floor; there is a cafe and souvenir counter and views across the whole city. Go between 9:30 and 11 on a cold, clear morning for Fuji, or after dark for the lights. Twenty to thirty minutes is enough. The two decks close on different weekdays and both close over New Year, so check which one is open. Nothing to climb, nothing to pay; a ten-minute walk from Shinjuku station's west exit.

  - **Expect:** An hour by train from Shinjuku, then a six-minute cable car to the halfway point. A paved path with a few flights of steps leads about a kilometre to Yakuo-in temple; the summit is another 20 minutes up. On clear December days Fuji stands behind the ridges, and around 16 to 26 December the sun sets exactly on its peak, drawing big crowds. Allow half a day. She can do the cable car and temple and turn back.

## Kanazawa

**Fit:** On our route — Stop 3 · 3 nights · 27–30 Dec

**Prefecture:** Ishikawa

### Tags

- snow
- food
- museums
- classics
- gentle

### Known for

- **Kenroku-en**: One of the three great gardens, its pines roped against the snow; free from 6 am. · Place: Kenroku-en
- **Crab**: The female kobako crab is in season only in November and December; Omicho market is the place. · Place: Omicho Market
- **Samurai and geisha districts**: Nagamachi's earthen walls, Higashi Chaya's teahouses, a gold-leaf workshop. · Place: Higashi Chaya and Shima teahouse
- **The 21st Century Museum**: The swimming pool you walk under, open to 20:00 on Fridays and Saturdays. · Place: 21st Century Museum of Contemporary Art

### Window

- Intermittent snow in late December, often bare ground with cold; the garden's snow ropes are up regardless.
- Museums close Mon 28 Dec and from 29 Dec; the Nomura house closes 26–27 Dec. Everything we want fits 24–27 Dec.
- Omicho market is open and at its fullest 28–30 Dec, then closes 1–3 Jan.
- Kenroku-en is free and open all night on 31 Dec, if anyone is still there.

**Feel:** A small, walkable city that kept its old districts and its food culture when the bombs missed it. Grey skies, wet snow, warm oden counters, and a modern museum in the middle of it all. The slow middle of the trip.

**Getting there:** Two and a half hours from Tokyo on the Hokuriku shinkansen, all reserved seats; book the moment sales open a month ahead. The Nohi bus continues to Shirakawa-go and Takayama.

**For Fernanda:** Flat, taxis everywhere, a university hospital in town. The garden paths can be icy on a cold morning; cleats help.

**Region:** alps

**Do line:** Gardens, geisha streets, gold leaf and crab

### Aliases

- Kenroku-en
- Kenrokuen
- Higashi Chaya
- Omicho
- Ishikawa

### Verdict

**Headline:** A small old city of gardens, teahouses and crab.

**Text:** The war's bombs missed Kanazawa, so its samurai lanes, geisha teahouses and food culture survived, with Kenroku-en, one of Japan's three great gardens, at the centre. In late December the pines are roped against the snow and the small kobako crab is in its last week. Three days: the garden and the 21st Century Museum on arrival, Shirakawa-go as a day trip on Monday 28 Dec, when the museums close, then the market, Higashi Chaya and the samurai lanes.

### How long to stay and what is on

**Best:** 3 nights with Shirakawa-go as a day trip, 2 without

#### Date notes

- Sat 26 Dec, 13:00: the geiko dance afternoon in a Higashi Chaya teahouse (up to ten people, so seven fits).
- 26–27 Dec and 1–2 Jan: the Nomura samurai house is closed, so go on 24 or 25 Dec.
- Mon 28 Dec: museums close; from 29 Dec the 21st Century Museum is shut to 1 Jan and the D.T. Suzuki and Prefectural museums to 3 Jan.
- 27 Dec–3 Jan: the castle park is lit in the evenings. 31 Dec–3 Jan Kenroku-en is free, and open all night on New Year's Eve.
- 28–31 Dec: Omicho market at its fullest; 1–4 Jan only 20–40 of its shops open; fully back Tue 5 Jan.
- End of December: the last of the kobako crab, the small female crab; cooked, not raw, for Fernanda.
- Mon 4 Jan: museums close again for the Monday.

#### Nights

##### 1

**Why:** An evening and a morning: Higashi Chaya at dusk and Kenroku-en first thing (free from 6:00), then on. A stop between Tokyo and the mountains, not a stay; the museums and the market need a day of their own.

**Longer:** A second night gives one full day: Kenroku-en, the castle park and the 21st Century Museum.

**Shorter:** A single night is a dinner of oden and a garden walk at dawn.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** Rushed, after a 2.5-hour train; Fernanda would probably skip the evening.

##### 2

**Why:** One full day: Kenroku-en with its snow ropes, the castle park and the 21st Century Museum, with oden or crab at night and Higashi Chaya on the arrival afternoon.

**Longer:** A third night adds a gold-leaf workshop, Omicho market and the samurai house.

**Shorter:** One night is a dawn garden walk and little else.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** Tight: the museums or the geisha district get squeezed.

##### 3

**Why:** Two full days: Kenroku-en and the museums on one, Higashi Chaya, gold leaf and the Nomura samurai house on the other, with lunch at Omicho. The length most guidebooks give it.

**Longer:** A fourth night adds the D.T. Suzuki Museum, the Museum of Art, the Sat 26 Dec geiko afternoon or a Kaga Onsen half day, and a slow day.

**Shorter:** Two nights loses the museums or the geisha district.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** Full, not idle.

##### 4

**Why:** Three full days at an easy pace: the garden, the museums, the teahouses, a wagashi class and the market, in a rental house with a washing machine as the mid-trip reset. It has to finish by 28 Dec, when the museums close.

**Longer:** A fifth night is mostly rest: the market, the castle park, a Kaga Onsen afternoon.

**Shorter:** Three nights squeezes the museums into one long day and loses the slow day.

**Idle:** some

**Note:** Day four is light by design: the market, a workshop, laundry.

##### 5

**Why:** Four full days: everything above plus a Kaga Onsen half day or the Noto coast, two hours each way and still rebuilding. Only worth it before 28 Dec; over 29 Dec–3 Jan the museums, the samurai house and most of the market are shut.

**Longer:** A sixth night would be a day with nothing new.

**Shorter:** Four nights keeps all of the city and loses the spare day.

**Idle:** real

**Note:** Two light days in a row: fine as a rest, dull for João and Carolina.

### Places to visit (21)

- **Kenroku-en** (garden, main) — One of Japan's three great gardens; snow ropes on the pines
  - Hours: 8:00-17:00; free 6:00 to opening Nov-Feb
  - For Fernanda: moderate, Gentle slopes, icy
  - Source: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e4200.html
  - **Famous for:** One of Japan's three great gardens, famous in winter for the rope cones that protect its pines from snow.
  - **Expect:** A strolling garden of ponds, streams, bridges and the two-legged Kotoji lantern that is on every poster. Ninety minutes at an easy pace. The yukitsuri ropes go up from November, and with snow on them the garden is at its best. Entry 320 yen, and free from 6:00 until opening, when it is nearly empty. Paths are gravel with gentle slopes and get icy; take it slowly after a freeze. Open every day of the year.
  - **Reputation:** classic

- **Kanazawa Castle Park** (sight, short) — Rebuilt turrets; evening light-up 28 Dec - 4 Jan (confirm)
  - Hours: Grounds 8:00-17:00
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Source: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e4202.html
  - **Famous for:** The rebuilt white turrets and long storehouse of the Maeda lords' castle, across the road from Kenroku-en.
  - **Expect:** Free grounds with wide lawns, lead-tiled roofs and the Gyokusen'inmaru garden below the walls, which is lit with music on winter evenings including the year-end holiday. Forty-five minutes outside; the reconstructed turrets cost 320 yen and show the joinery inside. The site is large and level with one gentle climb from the Kenroku-en side. Best combined with the garden in one morning. Grounds open 8:00 to 17:00.

## Shirakawa-go

**Fit:** On our route — A day out from Kanazawa, Mon 28 Dec

**Prefecture:** Gifu

### Tags

- snow
- classics

### Known for

- **The thatched village**: Steep gassho-zukuri farmhouses under a metre of snow, a World Heritage site. · Place: Shirakawa-go (Ogimachi)
- **The viewpoint**: The whole village from the Shiroyama lookout, a short shuttle up.

### Window

- Snow on the ground is near-certain by 25 Dec.
- The famous light-up nights are 11, 17, 24 and 31 Jan 2027, lottery only, all after we leave.
- Nohi buses thin out 29 Dec–3 Jan; we pass through on the 28th.
- No farmhouse sleeps seven over New Year, and the inns have steep stairs and shared baths.

**Feel:** A postcard that turns out to be a real village: smoke from the irori, tourists in the lanes until the last bus, then silence and snow.

**Getting there:** Fifty minutes from Takayama, seventy-five from Kanazawa, by Nohi bus with reserved seats booked 30 days out. Our bus stops here for two and a half hours on the way to Takayama.

**For Fernanda:** Flat lanes but packed snow; the viewpoint shuttle avoids the climb. A stop, not a night.

**Region:** alps

**Do line:** Thatched farmhouses in the snow

### Aliases

- Shirakawa
- Gassho-zukuri
- Ogimachi

### Verdict

**Headline:** Thatched farmhouses under snow, a day out from Kanazawa.

**Text:** Steep thatched farmhouses in a World Heritage village, with smoke from the hearths and snow almost certain by our date. A van with a driver, or the Nohi bus, takes about 75 minutes each way from Kanazawa, with a shuttle up to the viewpoint. No farmhouse can sleep seven over New Year, so it is a day out, not a night.

### How long to stay and what is on

**Best:** No night: a day trip from Kanazawa or a stop on the bus to Takayama, or 1 night in a farmhouse between 20 and 27 Dec

#### Date notes

- From about 25 Dec: snow on the ground is near-certain.
- 20–27 Dec: the only realistic dates for a farmhouse night for a group; New Year is long sold out, and no farmhouse sleeps seven then.
- 29 Dec–3 Jan: Nohi buses run a holiday timetable with some stops skipped; 1–3 Jan the village is very busy.
- Around 31 Dec–1 Jan: the Wada House has closed in past years; ask the tourist office.
- The light-up nights are 11, 17, 24 and 31 Jan 2027, all after we leave.

#### Nights

##### 1

**Why:** A night under thatch: dinner by the irori hearth, futons, and the village silent and snowy after the last coaches leave around 16:00. The farmhouse inns have shared baths and ladder-steep stairs, so Fernanda should ask for a ground-floor room.

**Longer:** A second night buys a quiet morning and the bus to Gokayama's Ainokura, with a tenth of the crowds.

**Shorter:** A single night is the keeper: the empty village at dusk and again at dawn.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** Full: an afternoon, the evening and a dawn walk.

##### 2

**Why:** The night plus a whole second day: the Wada House, the viewpoint shuttle, then Gokayama by the limited bus, or simply the snow. Most of the village is seen in half a day.

**Longer:** A third night would have nothing new; the village is a few lanes.

**Shorter:** One night keeps the empty village at dusk and dawn.

**Idle:** some

**Note:** Day two is slow and cold, with few places to sit indoors.

##### 3

**Why:** Too long for a village of a few lanes, with shared bathrooms, no doctor and buses that thin out over New Year. It only works as a snowed-in retreat.

**Longer:** A fourth night adds nothing.

**Shorter:** Two nights still gives the village, Gokayama and a quiet second evening.

**Idle:** real

**Note:** Two idle days in a cold farmhouse; not one for Fernanda at 30 weeks.

##### 4

**Why:** Not sensible: after a day the village repeats itself, and the towns with more to do, Takayama and Kanazawa, are an hour or more away by bus.

**Longer:** Nothing to add.

**Shorter:** Three nights is already more than the village holds.

**Idle:** real

**Note:** Three empty days; base in Takayama or Kanazawa instead.

##### 5

**Why:** Not sensible: Shirakawa-go is at its best as a stop on the bus or a single night, and every extra day is a cold one with little to do.

**Longer:** Nothing to add.

**Shorter:** Four nights is already far too many.

**Idle:** real

**Note:** Mostly idle; spend these nights in Takayama or Kanazawa.

### Places to visit (2)

- **Shirakawa-go (Ogimachi)** (sight, main) — Snow-covered thatched farmhouses (UNESCO)
  - Hours: Wada House 9:00-17:00
  - For Fernanda: moderate, Icy paths; ladder stairs inside houses
  - Source: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e5951.html
  - **Famous for:** A World Heritage village of steep thatched farmhouses, at its most famous under deep snow.
  - **Expect:** Ogimachi is a working village of 59 gassho-zukuri houses, a few of them open as museums; Wada House (400 yen) shows the silkworm lofts up ladder-like stairs. Two to three hours covers the village, the deck and lunch of soba or Hida beef. Snow is likely in late December and the paths are icy and uneven: waterproof boots and slip-on spikes for her. Buses from Kanazawa need reservations and can be delayed by snow. Expect crowds at midday.
  - **Reputation:** both
  - **Links:**
  -   - **Official site**: Url: https://shirakawa-go.gr.jp/en/
  -   - **japan-guide**: Url: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e5951.html
  -   - **Nohi Bus timetable**: Url: https://www.nouhibus.co.jp/route_bus/shirakawago_en/
- **Shiroyama observation deck** (viewpoint, short) — Classic view; take the shuttle (10:00-14:40, none 12-13)
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Source: https://shirakawa-go.gr.jp/en/faq/5/
  - **Famous for:** The postcard view of the whole village and its thatched roofs from the hill above.
  - **Expect:** From the Shiroyama deck you look straight down on the farmhouses in the valley; with snow it is the photo everyone comes for, and in person it is as good. A shuttle bus runs from near Wada House every 20 minutes between 10:00 and 14:40 (none from 12:00 to 13:00) for a small fare; the walking trail up is often closed in snow. Fifteen minutes up there is enough, and it is cold and windy, so dress for it.
  - **Reputation:** both
  - **Links:**

## Kyoto

**Fit:** On our route — Stop 4 · 6 nights · 30 Dec – 5 Jan

**Prefecture:** Kyoto

### Tags

- food
- museums
- classics
- gentle

### Known for

- **The temples**: Kiyomizu-dera and the Higashiyama lanes, Fushimi Inari at dawn, Arashiyama's bamboo. · Place: Kiyomizu-dera and Higashiyama lanes
- **New Year rituals**: The Okera-mairi flame at Yasaka on the 31st, the calligraphy festival at Kitano on 2 Jan, Okina Noh at Yasaka on the 3rd.
- **Kaiseki and yudofu**: Simmered tofu in a temple garden, kaiseki at lunch for a third of the dinner price. · Place: Okutan Nanzen-ji
- **The National Museum**: Its New Year show opens on 2 Jan. · Place: Kyoto National Museum

### Window

- Most restaurants close 1–3 Jan; department-store food halls reopen 2 Jan.
- Hatsumode at Fushimi Inari draws 2.7 million people 1–3 Jan: go at dawn or after 15:00, or not at all.
- The National Museum and most sights reopen 2 Jan; Nara on Mon 4 Jan when Kyoto's museums close again.
- Kyoto's accommodation tax rose in March 2026; it shows at checkout.

**Feel:** Grey stone, bare maples, early dusk and lantern light; the city at its quietest and most itself in the first days of January, once the hatsumode crowds thin.

**Getting there:** About 3.5 h from Takayama via Nagoya, 2¼ h from Kanazawa via Tsuruga, 2 h 15 from Tokyo on the Tokaido shinkansen. Nara is 35–45 minutes.

**For Fernanda:** Flat except the temple approaches; taxis for the hills. Two university hospitals with 24-hour obstetrics.

**Region:** kansai

**Do line:** Temples, gardens and New Year rituals

### Aliases

- Fushimi Inari
- Arashiyama
- Gion
- Kiyomizu-dera
- Ginkaku-ji
- Philosopher's Path
- Nanzen-ji
- Nishiki market

### Verdict

**Headline:** The old capital, for New Year's Eve and after.

**Text:** On New Year's Eve Higashiyama is lantern light and crowds: the Okera fire at Yasaka Shrine from about 19:30, temple bells you can ring, and a walk home through the old lanes. New Year's Day is slow on purpose. Then the National Museum's New Year show, and easy days out to Nara, Uji, Ohara or Himeji while the rest of Japan travels home. It is a hub, so six nights never means an empty day.

### How long to stay and what is on

**Best:** 4 nights (5 at a gentle pace)

#### Date notes

- 21 Dec: Toji's last temple market of the year; 25 Dec: Kitano Tenmangu's.
- Sun 27 Dec, 14:00: the free trial ringing of the Chion-in bell.
- 28–30 Dec: Nishiki market at its busiest; 1–3 Jan most stalls are shut.
- 29–31 Dec: Nijo Castle is closed, and 1–3 Jan only its gardens open. 29 Dec–1 Jan the National Museum is shut; 29–30 Dec Himeji Castle too.
- 31 Dec: the Chion-in bell from about 22:40 (watch only, booked online from 1 Dec) and the Okera fire at Yasaka; restaurants close.
- 1–3 Jan: Fushimi Inari draws about 2.7 million: before 7:00, after 15:00, or not at all. Most restaurants close on the 1st; hotel restaurants are the safe bet.
- 2 Jan: Kitano's first-calligraphy festival. 2–3 Jan: the National Museum's New Year exhibition. 3 Jan: Okina Noh and the court-dress card game at Yasaka, and the Kyocera Museum's only open day.
- 29 Dec to about 5 Jan: Gion Corner and the geiko world are on holiday; the Imperial Palace and Katsura stay shut to 4 Jan.
- Mon 4 Jan: museums close again, which makes it the Nara day.
- 8–12 Jan: Toka Ebisu at Kyoto's Ebisu shrine.

#### Nights

##### 1

**Why:** An evening and one morning: Kiyomizu-dera and the Higashiyama lanes at opening, or Fushimi Inari at dawn for whoever wants it. Arriving on 1 Jan, late in the day with restaurants shut, one night is almost nothing.

**Longer:** A second night gives one full temple day.

**Shorter:** A single night is a glimpse; Kyoto wants two or more.

**Idle:** some

**Note:** A stopover; nothing will feel done.

##### 2

**Why:** One full day: Higashiyama and Kiyomizu-dera, Gion at dusk. In December everything is open; on 1–3 Jan the temples are, but museums only from the 2nd and many restaurants are shut.

**Longer:** A third night adds Arashiyama's bamboo and Tenryu-ji, or the National Museum.

**Shorter:** One night is a single morning.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** Full, and the first-timers will leave wanting more.

##### 3

**Why:** Two full days: Higashiyama on one, Arashiyama on the other, with Gion at dusk and Fushimi Inari at dawn for the early risers. Starting on 1 Jan, that afternoon counts for nothing.

**Longer:** A fourth night brings back Nara or the National Museum.

**Shorter:** Two nights loses Arashiyama or Higashiyama.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** Two big days; Nara drops out.

##### 4

**Why:** Three big days plus Nara: Higashiyama, Arashiyama, the National Museum and the New Year rites on 2–3 Jan, and Nara on Mon 4 Jan when Kyoto's museums close. What the main route does from 1 to 5 Jan.

**Longer:** A fifth night buys an easy day for Ohara or Kibune, often snowy, or a second temple dawn.

**Shorter:** Three nights loses Nara or Arashiyama.

**Idle:** some

**Note:** Starting on 1 Jan, that day is a quiet arrival with restaurants shut; expected, and the only such day.

##### 5

**Why:** Four days plus a gentle one: Ohara's moss garden or Kibune, Nanzen-ji and the Philosopher's Path, or a day out to Himeji (open from 31 Dec) or Osaka's bunraku from about 3 Jan.

**Longer:** A sixth night adds Uji's tea houses or an Osaka day.

**Shorter:** Four nights keeps the big days and loses the easy one.

**Idle:** some

**Note:** One easy day built in; comfortable for Fernanda and the parents.

##### 6

**Why:** The city plus day trips: Nara, Osaka, Himeji or Uji, one a day, with a rest. If it spans New Year's Eve, the Chion-in bell (watched, booked, cold) or the Okera fire at Yasaka, with dinner at a hotel or in a rented house because restaurants close.

**Longer:** A seventh night is another day trip or a rest.

**Shorter:** Five nights drops one day trip.

**Idle:** some

**Note:** Full if you use the trains; slow over 29 Dec–3 Jan if you stay in the city.

##### 7

**Why:** A week only works as a base for Kansai: Nara, Osaka, Himeji, Uji and a day in the hills. Across 29 Dec–3 Jan half of it is shrine visits and closed museums, with Fushimi Inari taking millions.

**Longer:** An eighth night is too many with the city half shut.

**Shorter:** Six nights loses a day trip.

**Idle:** some

**Note:** Pedro and Fernanda know the city; a week here is a week less somewhere new.

### Places to visit (30)

- **Kiyomizu-dera and Higashiyama lanes** (sight, main) — The classic first Kyoto morning
  - For Fernanda: moderate, Slopes and stone steps
  - **Famous for:** Kiyomizu-dera's wooden stage over the hillside and the preserved sloping lanes that lead up to it.
  - **Expect:** Start at the temple when it opens at 6:00 and you will have the stage and the city view almost to yourselves; by 10 the lanes of Ninenzaka and Sannenzaka are shoulder to shoulder. Entry 500 yen. Two to three hours including shops and a tea stop. It is all uphill on the way in, with stone steps that are slippery when wet or icy, so slow going for her. Very crowded 1 to 3 January.

- **Arashiyama bamboo grove and Tenryu-ji** (sight, main) — Go before 9:00
  - For Fernanda: moderate
  - **Famous for:** The towering bamboo path and Tenryu-ji's 14th-century pond garden, Kyoto's most photographed corner.
  - **Expect:** The bamboo grove is a 500-metre path, free and open all hours, and it is magical only when empty: arrive before 8:30 or it is a slow shuffle behind phones. Then Tenryu-ji's garden (500 yen), laid out in 1339 against the hills, which is the real reason to come. Two to three hours with the river and Togetsukyo bridge. Flat and paved. Shops open around 10.

- **Fushimi Inari at dawn** (sight, main) — Torii tunnels; best at dawn, before the crowds
  - For Fernanda: high, Stairs; turn back at Yotsutsuji
  - **Famous for:** Thousands of orange torii gates forming tunnels up a wooded mountain, open day and night.
  - **Expect:** Free and never closed, so go at first light, when the lower tunnels are empty. The walk to the Yotsutsuji viewpoint takes 30 to 45 minutes on paved steps and is enough; the full loop is two to three hours. From 1 to 3 January this is the busiest shrine in western Japan, so pick 4 or 5 January and be there by 7:00. Steady stairs: she can turn back at Yotsutsuji or stop at the main hall.

- **Himeji Castle** (sight, day_trip) — Japan's finest original castle
  - Closed: {"start":"2026-12-29","end":"2026-12-30","note":"Closed","source":null}

  - **Famous for:** Japan's largest and finest original castle, the white 1609 keep that survived war and earthquakes.

Inside the keep you climb six floors of steep wooden stairs in socks and queue on busy days; entry 1,000 yen. Three hours with the grounds and the Koko-en garden next door. The grounds, baileys and garden are flat and worth the trip on their own, so she can skip the keep. Open from 1 January; crowded on the first three days.

It was open over New Year last year, which makes it the safe choice for 1 or 2 January. Book online or by phone (075-342-5527) and ask for a cooked-only course for her; hotel kitchens handle this easily. Ninety minutes.

## Nara

**Fit:** On our route — A day out from Kyoto, 1 Jan and 4 Jan

**Prefecture:** Nara

### Tags

- classics
- gentle

### Known for

- **Todai-ji**: The Great Buddha in the world's largest wooden hall; free from midnight on 1 Jan with the face window open. · Place: Todai-ji
- **The deer**: They wander the park and bow for crackers. · Place: Kasuga Taisha
- **Naramachi**: Old merchant lanes, kamameshi rice in an iron pot, fresh-pounded mochi.

### Window

- Todai-ji is open every day; the park is at its emptiest on a weekday in January.
- Kamameshi Shizuka closes Tuesdays, which is why Nara is Monday 4 Jan.
- Most Nara museums close Mondays, so this is a temple-and-park day.

**Feel:** A park the size of a town, with temples at its edges and deer in the middle. Slow, flat, and a relief after Kyoto's crowds.

**Getting there:** Forty-five minutes from Kyoto on the Kintetsu or JR line.

**For Fernanda:** Flat paths, benches everywhere. The one long walk is from the station to Todai-ji; a bus covers it.

**Region:** kansai

**Do line:** The Great Buddha, deer and old lanes

### Aliases

- Todai-ji
- Todaiji
- Nara deer
- Kasuga Taisha
- Naramachi

### Verdict

**Headline:** The Great Buddha and the deer, a day from Kyoto.

**Text:** A park the size of a town, with deer in the middle and temples at its edges, among them Todai-ji, whose Great Buddha sits in the world's largest wooden hall. It is slow and flat, 35 minutes from Kyoto by reserved Kintetsu train, and we go on Monday 4 Jan. Whoever is up early can also see the Great Buddha on New Year's morning (to check). Another route gives Nara two nights of its own.

**Route:** kyoto-nara-hakone

### How long to stay and what is on

**Best:** No night (a day trip from Kyoto), or 1 night for New Year's Eve at Todai-ji

#### Date notes

- 28 Dec–1 Jan: the Nara National Museum is shut; open 2–3 Jan, shut again Mon 4 Jan.
- 31 Dec: Todai-ji hands out numbered tickets to ring its bell from about 22:30 (queue from 21:30); Kasuga Taisha stays open all night.
- 1 Jan, 00:00–8:00: the Great Buddha hall is free, with the window above his face open.
- 1–3 Jan: big but orderly hatsumode crowds at Kasuga and Todai-ji.
- Tuesdays: Kamameshi Shizuka is closed. Isuien garden is shut until mid-January.

#### Nights

##### 1

**Why:** An evening and a quiet early morning in the park before the day-trippers arrive. On 31 Dec it is the place to ring a temple bell yourselves, with the Great Buddha hall open free from midnight.

**Longer:** A second night adds Horyu-ji's ancient wooden temples or Naramachi at leisure.

**Shorter:** A single night buys the dawn park; without it Nara is an easy day trip, 35–45 minutes from Kyoto.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** Calm; the park is at its best at 8:00. The midnight bell means standing in the cold.

##### 2

**Why:** Two unhurried days: Todai-ji, Kasuga Taisha and the deer on one, Horyu-ji or Naramachi and the National Museum (open 2–3 Jan) on the other. Flat paths and benches everywhere.

**Longer:** A third night would be Kyoto or Osaka as day trips.

**Shorter:** One night keeps the dawn park.

**Idle:** some

**Note:** Gentle and flat, but quiet in the evenings.

##### 3

**Why:** Nara as a calm base for Kansai: the park, Horyu-ji, and Kyoto or Osaka each 35–50 minutes away. Easy for Fernanda; wasted if Kyoto is also on the route.

**Longer:** A fourth night is another day trip.

**Shorter:** Two nights covers Nara itself.

**Idle:** some

**Note:** Nara fills two days; the third is a train out.

##### 4

**Why:** Too long for Nara itself; it only works as a quiet base for day trips into Kyoto and Osaka.

**Longer:** Nothing to add.

**Shorter:** Three nights is already a base, not a visit.

**Idle:** real

**Note:** Two days of trains or two idle days.

##### 5

**Why:** Not sensible: Kyoto or Osaka would be the better base for the same day trips.

**Longer:** Nothing to add.

**Shorter:** Four nights is already long.

**Idle:** real

**Note:** Mostly idle in Nara itself.

### Places to visit (7)

- **Todai-ji** (sight, main) — The Great Buddha hall
  - Hours: 8:00-17:00
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Source: https://www.todaiji.or.jp/en/?p=8117
  - **Famous for:** The 15-metre bronze Great Buddha inside one of the largest wooden buildings in the world.
  - **Expect:** Walk through the deer park and the Nandaimon gate with its two giant guardian statues to the hall, which still stops people in the doorway. Entry 800 yen; an hour, more with the museum. Children crawl through the pillar hole said to equal the Buddha's nostril. Flat gravel; the deer are pushy around anyone holding crackers, so she should watch rather than feed. Open 8:00 to 17:00 and busy from mid-morning.
  - **Reputation:** classic
  - **Links:**
  -   - **Official site**: Url: https://www.todaiji.or.jp/en/
  -   - **japan-guide**: Url: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e4100.html
  -   - **japan-guide: Nara Park**: Url: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e4103.html
- **Kasuga Taisha** (sight, short) — Shrine of lanterns
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Source: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e4102.html
  - **Famous for:** Nara's great vermilion shrine, approached through a forest lined with 3,000 stone and bronze lanterns.
  - **Expect:** A gentle 20-minute walk uphill from Todai-ji under old trees and moss-covered lanterns, or a bus to the shrine stop to save the climb. The outer areas are free; the inner precinct (700 yen) has corridors hung with bronze lanterns and a dark room where some are lit all year. An hour. The lanterns are all lit only in February and August. Open 7:00 to 17:00; busy with New Year visitors in the first week of January.
  - **Reputation:** classic
  - **Links:**
  -   - **Official site**: Url: https://www.kasugataisha.or.jp/

- **Naramachi** (sight, short) — Merchant lanes
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Source: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e4108.html
  - **Famous for:** Nara's old merchant quarter of lattice-fronted townhouses, small temples and craft shops.
  - **Expect:** A flat grid of quiet lanes south of the shopping arcade: a restored merchant house you can enter for free, Gango-ji temple (500 yen), cafes in old houses and the red cloth monkeys hanging outside doors. One to two hours, best after the park when you want somewhere calm. Nakatanidou's pounded mochi, near the arcade, is the snack. Most shops open from about 10 to 17; some close on 4 January.
  - **Reputation:** real
  - **Links:**

- **Horyu-ji** (sight, main) — The oldest wooden buildings in the world, from about 700, with a five-storey pagoda and a treasure hall; 30 minutes by JR from Nara
  - Hours: 8:00-16:30 from November to February
  - For Fernanda: low, Flat gravel between the halls; a long site, so take it in two halves
  - Price: About ¥1,500
  - Source: https://www.gltjp.com/en/article/item/20472/
- **Nara National Museum** (museum, short) — Japan's finest Buddhist sculpture, next to the deer park; it reopens on 2 Jan
  - Hours: 9:30-17:00; closed Mondays and 28 Dec-1 Jan
  - Closed: {"start":"2026-12-28","end":"2027-01-01","note":"Closed for New Year","source":"https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e4109.html"}
  - Closed weekdays: Mon
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Source: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e4109.html
- **Kofuku-ji and its National Treasure Museum** (museum, short) — The temple by the park whose small museum holds the three-faced Ashura statue; the pagoda is under repair until 2034
  - Hours: Museum 9:00-17:00
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Price: About ¥900
  - Source: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e4101.html
- **Kamameshi Shizuka** (food, meal) — Rice cooked in an iron pot with chicken or vegetables, near the park; all cooked
  - Hours: Lunch and early dinner; closed Tuesdays
  - Closed weekdays: Tue
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Source: https://narashikanko.or.jp/en/spot/cuisine/shiduka-koen

## Kawaguchiko

**Fit:** On our route — Stop 5 · 2 nights · 5–7 Jan

**Prefecture:** Yamanashi

### Tags

- fuji
- onsen
- gentle

### Known for

- **Fuji straight across the lake**: Nothing in the way: from the north shore, the room, the bath, the boat. · Place: Oishi Park
- **The ropeway and the lake boat**: Three minutes up to the Kachi Kachi viewpoint; twenty minutes on the water. · Place: Mt Fuji Panoramic Ropeway
- **Chureito Pagoda**: The red pagoda with Fuji behind; 398 steps, for whoever wants the climb. · Place: Chureito Pagoda
- **Hoto noodles**: Yamanashi's thick noodle stew in an iron pot, all cooked. · Place: Hoto Fudou (Higashi-Koiji)

### Window

- Late December to early January is Fuji's clearest season, snow-capped most mornings.
- The first-sunrise rush fills the lakeside inns on 31 Dec; 5–7 Jan is open at the hotels.
- The winter fireworks start 23 Jan and the Diamond Fuji weeks are in February, both after us.
- Fewer indoor options than Hakone on a cloudy day: the kimono museum, the music forest.

**Feel:** Flat lakeside paths, a mountain that fills the sky, and a quieter resort than Hakone. The postcard view, for real.

**Getting there:** From Kyoto about 4 h 15 via Mishima and the Fujikyu bus; to Shinjuku 2 h on the Fuji Excursion train. An hour more than Hakone from the west.

**For Fernanda:** Flat shoreline, lifts and beds in the lake hotels; the pagoda steps are optional. The lake is at 833 m, which is no concern on its own. Fujiyoshida Municipal Hospital, a regional perinatal centre that takes obstetric walk-ins, is nearby.

**Region:** tokyo-fuji

**Do line:** Fuji across the lake, ropeway, boat, hoto

### Aliases

- Mount Fuji
- Mt Fuji
- Fuji
- Fujisan
- Fuji Five Lakes
- Lake Kawaguchi
- Chureito Pagoda

### Verdict

**Headline:** Fuji fills the sky, straight across the lake.

**Text:** The famous view of Fuji with nothing in the way, from the hotel, the lakeside path and the ropeway, with hoto noodle stew for lunch. Early January is the mountain's clearest season, and the lake is new for all seven of us. The move from Kyoto is about 4 h 15, the longest of the trip, and on a cloudy day there is less to do here than in Hakone, which is why we have two dawns to catch it clear. The nearest obstetric emergency care is to check before we book.

### How long to stay and what is on

**Best:** 2 nights (3 for a third chance at Fuji)

#### Date notes

- Late December to early January is Fuji's clearest season, snow-capped most mornings.
- 31 Dec: first-sunrise visitors fill the lakeside inns; Fuji-Q Highland runs a countdown with fireworks.
- 29 Dec–3 Jan: buses from Shinjuku sell out and the lake road jams; 1–3 Jan Chureito and Oshino Hakkai are busy.
- The Itchiku Kubota Art Museum's January closing days differ between sources; check.
- The winter fireworks start 23 Jan and Diamond Fuji at Lake Yamanaka is February, both after we leave.

#### Nights

##### 1

**Why:** One afternoon on the shore and one dawn for Fuji, if it is clear. After a long move, such as about four hours from Kyoto, the lake becomes a stopover.

**Longer:** A second night gives the ropeway and the lake boat, and a second dawn.

**Shorter:** A single night gives one dawn and no ropeway day.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** All arriving and leaving; Fuji gets one chance.

##### 2

**Why:** One full day for the Panoramic Ropeway, the boat, Oishi Park's flat shore and a hoto lunch, and two dawns for Fuji straight across the water.

**Longer:** A third night is a third chance at Fuji and an easy day at Oshino Hakkai or Lake Yamanaka, each about 30 minutes by bus.

**Shorter:** One night gives one dawn and no ropeway day.

**Idle:** some

**Note:** Clear days are full; a cloudy day has less indoors than Hakone.

##### 3

**Why:** The lake plus Oshino Hakkai's spring ponds or Lake Yamanaka, Chureito Pagoda at dawn for the climbers (398 steps, not for Fernanda), and three dawns for Fuji.

**Longer:** A fourth night has little more to see.

**Shorter:** Two nights keeps the lake and the ropeway.

**Idle:** some

**Note:** Good if clouds threaten; otherwise one easy day.

##### 4

**Why:** Only as a Fuji retreat in a house with a view, or with a day out to Hakone, about two hours by bus. Over 29 Dec–3 Jan the lake road jams.

**Longer:** A fifth night adds nothing.

**Shorter:** Three nights already holds every sight and three dawns.

**Idle:** real

**Note:** Two quiet days, more if it clouds over.

##### 5

**Why:** Too long: the lake has two days of sights and a cloudy spell would leave little indoors.

**Longer:** Nothing to add.

**Shorter:** Four nights is already long.

**Idle:** real

**Note:** Mostly idle unless it is a rest week.

### Places to visit (10)

- **Oishi Park** (viewpoint, short) — A flat, free lakeside promenade with Fuji straight across the water; the easiest Fuji view of the trip for her
  - Hours: Always open
  - For Fernanda: low, Flat paths along the north shore
  - Source: https://en.kawaguchiko.net/park-en/oishi-park
  - **Famous for:** A flat lakeside park on Kawaguchiko's north shore with Fuji straight across the water.
  - **Expect:** A free promenade along the shore with flower beds, a cafe and a shop selling blueberry soft-serve; Fuji fills the view with nothing in the way. Thirty to forty-five minutes, or longer on a bench. Go early in the morning for the calmest water and clearest mountain; cloud builds by midday even in January. Always open. The easiest Fuji view of the trip for her: paved, level, with parking and toilets. Reached by the sightseeing bus red line.
  - **Reputation:** both
  - **Links:**
  -   - **Official site**: Url: https://en.kawaguchiko.net/park-en/oishi-park
  -   - **japan-guide: Lake Kawaguchiko**: Url: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e6906.html
- **Mt Fuji Panoramic Ropeway** (nature, main) — Three minutes up to the Kachi Kachi viewpoint over the lake and Fuji; the big thing of the day, with the lake cruise
  - Hours: 9:00-17:00 in winter; stops in strong wind
  - For Fernanda: low, Seated; a few steps at the top deck
  - Source: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e6905.html
  - **Famous for:** A three-minute cable car to a viewing deck above the lake, with Fuji in full from top to bottom.
  - **Expect:** The ropeway, long known as Kachi Kachi Yama, lifts you to 1,075 metres, where a deck, a small shrine and a cafe face the mountain; a folk tale about a rabbit and a raccoon dog decorates everything. About an hour including the queue; return ticket around 1,000 yen. Open 9:00 to 17:00 in winter and stops in strong wind. Seated in the cabin, with a few steps on the deck. Pairs with the boat from the same shore.
  - **Reputation:** classic
  - **Links:**
  -   - **Official site**: Url: https://www.mtfujiropeway.jp/
  -   - **japan-guide**: Url: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e6916.html
- **Lake Kawaguchi sightseeing boat** (nature, short) — Twenty minutes on the lake with the Fuji view from the water
  - Hours: 9:00-16:30
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Source: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e6900.html
  - **Famous for:** A 20-minute sightseeing boat on Lake Kawaguchi with Fuji rising behind the north shore.
  - **Expect:** Boats leave from the pier near the ropeway roughly every 30 minutes, 9:00 to 16:30, for about 1,000 yen. You sit on an open upper deck or in the warm cabin while the boat makes a loop; the Fuji view from the water is wide and unobstructed. It is short, so combine it with the ropeway and lunch nearby. Calm water, easy boarding, no stairs required if you stay below.
  - **Reputation:** classic
  - **Links:**
  -   - **Official site (Fujigoko Kisen)**: Url: https://www.fujigokokisen.jp/
  -   - **japan-guide: Lake Kawaguchiko**: Url: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e6906.html
- **Chureito Pagoda** (viewpoint, short) — The postcard shot: the red pagoda with Fuji behind. 398 steps, so not for her
  - Hours: Always open
  - For Fernanda: high, 398 steps; she stays below or at the shrine
  - Source: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e6917.html
  - **Famous for:** The red five-storey pagoda with Fuji behind it: the most reproduced photograph of Japan.
  - **Expect:** The pagoda is a 1963 war memorial in Arakurayama Sengen Park, and the famous angle is from a platform behind it, up 398 steps from the shrine. The climb takes 15 to 20 minutes and the view is exactly the postcard; in winter there are no blossoms but far smaller crowds. Free, always open. The steps are steep and can be icy, so she stays at the shrine below. Ten minutes on foot from Shimoyoshida station.
  - **Reputation:** photo
  - **Links:**
  -   - **japan-guide**: Url: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e6917.html
  -   - **Fujiyoshida tourism (official)**: Url: https://fujiyoshida.net/en/
- **Itchiku Kubota Art Museum** (museum, short) — Kimono painted as Fuji landscapes, in a Gaudi-like building; the cloudy-day option
  - Hours: 10:00-16:30; January closing days differ between sources, check
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Source: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e6927.html
  - **Famous for:** Kimono dyed as landscapes of Fuji and the seasons, in a Gaudi-like building on the north shore.
  - **Expect:** Itchiku Kubota revived a lost 16th-century dyeing technique and spent his life on a series of kimono that hang together as one panorama. The main hall is a pyramid of cypress beams; outside are gardens, a waterfall and a cafe. An hour; entry 1,300 yen; open 10:00 to 16:30. The best thing to do here on a cloudy day. Lifts and short paths. January closing days differ between sources, so check before going.
  - **Reputation:** real
  - **Links:**
  -   - **Official site**: Url: https://www.itchiku-museum.com/
  -   - **japan-guide**: Url: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e6912.html
- **Lakeside footbaths and the Kawaguchiko Music Forest** (experience, short) — Free footbaths on the north shore and a small European music-box museum; gentle afternoons
  - Hours: Music Forest 10:00-17:00
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Source: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e6900.html
  - **Famous for:** Free hot-spring footbaths along the north shore and a small European music-box museum with Fuji views.
  - **Expect:** The Music Forest is a cluster of European-style buildings with a rose garden, dancing fountains, automatons and hourly concerts on a giant fairground organ; 1,800 yen and about 90 minutes, mostly indoors. Open 10:00 to 17:00. Nearby, a few footbaths on the lakeside let you sit with your feet in hot water and the mountain in front of you; bring a small towel. A gentle afternoon that suits her completely.
  - **Reputation:** real
  - **Links:**

- **Hoto Fudou (Higashi-Koiji)** (food, meal) — Hoto, the thick flat noodle stew of Yamanashi, in a big white dome; about 300 seats, so six is easy
  - Hours: 11:00-20:00
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Price: ¥1,320, cash
  - Source: https://livejapan.com/en/in-tokyo/in-pref-fuji_mountain/in-mount_fuji/article-a0005559/
  - **Famous for:** Hoto, Yamanashi's thick flat noodles stewed with pumpkin and vegetables in miso, in a white dome.
  - **Expect:** One main dish, about 1,320 yen, served boiling in an iron pot big enough to be a meal on its own; add horse sashimi or pickles if you like. The Higashi-Koiji branch is a cloud-shaped concrete building with about 300 seats, so six sit together with no booking. Cash only. Open 11:00 to 20:00. Fully cooked and vegetable-heavy, a good warm lunch for her. A short taxi or bus from the station.

Everything simmered, so fine for her.

## Yudanaka, Shibu Onsen and the snow monkeys

**Fit:** On our route — Stop 2 · 3 nights · 24–27 Dec

**Prefecture:** Nagano

### Tags

- snow
- onsen

### Known for

- **The snow monkeys**: Wild macaques in a steaming pool in a snowy gorge, a 30–40 minute forest walk in.
- **Shibu Onsen**: A lantern-lit street with nine old public baths and a key to all of them.
- **Zenko-ji**: Nagano's great temple, open all night on New Year's Eve.
- **Nozawa**: Thirteen free village baths and a ski day for anyone who wants one.

### Window

- Snow likely but 'probably white, not guaranteed deep' by Christmas; the monkeys are there regardless.
- The park is open every day; the small Shibu inns sell out for New Year a year ahead.
- Nozawa's fire festival is 15 Jan, after we leave.

**Feel:** A steaming gorge, a lantern street, and a monkey staring at you from the water. Probably the most memorable single sight within reach, if the snow comes.

**Getting there:** Shinkansen to Nagano (80 min from Tokyo), then 40 minutes by bus or the Nagano Electric Railway. From Kanazawa, Nagano is an hour.

**For Fernanda:** The forest path is packed snow and can be icy: cleats and a slow pace, or she waits at the ryokan. The six-room village inns have stairs; the larger hotels have lifts.

**Region:** alps

**Do line:** Snow monkeys and a lantern-lit onsen street

### Aliases

- Snow monkeys
- Jigokudani
- Snow Monkey Park
- Shibu Onsen
- Nozawa

### Verdict

**Headline:** Wild monkeys bathing in a steaming, snowy gorge.

**Text:** Wild monkeys sit in a hot pool at the end of a forest walk of about 1.6 km on packed snow, and Shibu Onsen below is a lantern-lit lane of nine old public baths. We spend Christmas Eve at a ryokan here, with Zenko-ji on the way in. On the third day whoever wants snow skis at Shiga Kogen, 30 minutes by bus, while Fernanda and the parents see Obuse's Hokusai paintings. Snow can be thin before Christmas. The path can be icy and the public baths run very hot, so Fernanda may prefer the footbaths and the inn's own bath.

### How long to stay and what is on

**Best:** 2 nights before Christmas, or 3 with a ski or Obuse day

#### Date notes

- Snow by Christmas is likely but not guaranteed deep; the monkeys come down regardless.
- Every day, 9:00–16:00 in winter: the monkey park, busiest 29 Dec–3 Jan.
- 31 Dec: Zenko-ji's midnight bell (the first 108 in line ring it) and New Year's Day hatsumode, crowded but calmer than Tokyo or Kyoto.
- New Year: the small Shibu inns sold out a year ahead.
- 27 Dec–3 Jan: Nozawa night skiing; the fire festival is 15 Jan, after we leave.

#### Nights

##### 1

**Why:** The monkeys in the morning and a night on Shibu Onsen's lantern street. After a late arrival the monkeys become a rushed morning, and the forest path is 1.6 km each way on packed snow.

**Longer:** A second night gives an unhurried monkey morning and an evening of bath-hopping.

**Shorter:** A single night is the most rushed way to see them.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** Full; Fernanda may wait at the inn if the path is icy.

##### 2

**Why:** One morning for the snow monkeys in their steaming pool, one evening walking Shibu's nine old bathhouses in yukata, and a ryokan dinner. Obuse's Hokusai ceiling or Zenko-ji fit on the way in or out.

**Longer:** A third night buys Nozawa's village baths, Zenko-ji at dawn, or a second bath day.

**Shorter:** One night makes the monkeys a rushed morning after a late arrival.

**Idle:** some

**Note:** Day two is the bath street and a slow lunch. Lovely in snow, quiet without it.

##### 3

**Why:** A split day: skiing or snow at Shiga Kogen, 30 minutes by bus, for whoever wants it, while Fernanda and the parents go to Obuse for Hokusai and chestnut sweets. Or Zenko-ji's dawn service, Nozawa Onsen's village baths, or Togakushi's cedar avenue in the snow.

**Longer:** A fourth night only works as a ski stay.

**Shorter:** Two nights covers the monkeys and Shibu.

**Idle:** some

**Note:** Good for five of us; Fernanda has a quiet day at the inn.

##### 4

**Why:** Only with skiing or a split base, Nagano city plus Shibu plus Nozawa; Shibu's small inns and single lane repeat themselves.

**Longer:** A fifth night adds nothing for the non-skiers.

**Shorter:** Three nights already covers the area.

**Idle:** real

**Note:** Two idle days for anyone not skiing.

##### 5

**Why:** Too long unless it is a ski week; Nozawa wants five to seven nights over New Year, but that is a different trip.

**Longer:** Nothing to add.

**Shorter:** Four nights is already long.

**Idle:** real

**Note:** Mostly idle for Fernanda and the parents.

### Places to visit (1)

- **Shibu Onsen's nine public baths** (onsen, short) — Guests of Shibu's inns get a key to nine tiny old bathhouses along the lane; collect a stamp at each in your yukata
  - Hours: Bathhouses open about 6:00-22:00 for inn guests
  - For Fernanda: high, The water runs very hot; she enjoys the walk and the stamps and bathes at the inn
  - Source: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e6029.html

## Nagano

**Fit:** On our route — A day out from Yudanaka, 24 Dec – 26 Dec

**Prefecture:** Nagano

### Tags

- snow
- classics
- museums

### Known for

- **Zenko-ji**: A seventh-century temple with a dawn service and a pitch-dark passage under the hall, walked with one hand on the wall.
- **The snow monkeys**: Wild macaques in a steaming pool, 45 minutes away by train or bus and then a forest walk.
- **Matsumoto Castle**: Japan's black original castle, 50 minutes away on the Shinano express.
- **Obuse**: A small town of chestnut sweets, with a museum of Hokusai's late work.

### Window

- The city sees light snow; the monkey trail and the ski villages are white.
- Zenko-ji is packed from New Year's Eve to 3 Jan. Before 29 Dec or after 4 Jan it is calm.
- Matsumoto Castle closes for the year end from 29 Dec.

**Feel:** A plain, friendly temple city at the foot of snowy hills. The monkeys, the castle and the ski villages are each under an hour away.

**Getting there:** 80–100 minutes from Tokyo and about an hour from Kanazawa on the Hokuriku shinkansen.

**For Fernanda:** Flat around the station and the temple, and a station hotel with a lift. The monkey trail is packed snow and ice, so she may prefer to wait in Shibu Onsen with the foot baths. Nagano Red Cross Hospital has a perinatal centre.

**Region:** alps

**Do line:** A great temple, snow monkeys and a castle

### Aliases

- Zenko-ji
- Zenkoji
- Obuse

### Verdict

**Headline:** A friendly temple city at the foot of snowy hills.

**Text:** Zenko-ji is a seventh-century temple with a flat approach and a pitch-dark passage under the hall, walked with one hand on the wall. We stop here on the way to Yudanaka, days before the New Year crowds. The passage is optional, and Fernanda may prefer the hall and the approach.

### How long to stay and what is on

**Best:** 3 nights

#### Date notes

- Every day, including 31 Dec and 1 Jan: the snow monkey park is open. It is busiest 29 Dec–3 Jan.
- From 29 Dec: Matsumoto Castle closes for the year end. Sources differ on whether it reopens on 1 Jan or 4 Jan.
- 31 Dec into 1 Jan: Zenko-ji stays open all night and is packed, and the first-visit crowds are large until 3 Jan.
- Every morning at dawn: Zenko-ji's morning service is open to anyone.
- Obuse's Hokusai Museum: check its year-end closing days.
- 15 Jan: Nozawa's fire festival, after we leave.

#### Nights

##### 1

**Why:** Zenko-ji and the dark passage in the afternoon, dinner on the temple approach, and the snow monkeys the next morning before moving on. Kanazawa is an hour away, Tokyo 80–100 minutes.

**Longer:** A second night gives the monkeys a full morning and Shibu's lantern street in the afternoon.

**Shorter:** With no night, Nagano can be a stop between Tokyo and Kanazawa, with the bags in lockers.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** A busy stopover.

##### 2

**Why:** Zenko-ji on arrival, then the snow monkeys at Jigokudani for the walkers. Fernanda can take the foot baths and a private bath in Shibu Onsen and meet them for lunch.

**Longer:** A third night adds Matsumoto Castle or Obuse.

**Shorter:** One night squeezes the monkeys into a morning.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** Two good days.

##### 3

**Why:** Zenko-ji, the snow monkeys, and a day out to Matsumoto's black castle (50 minutes) or Obuse's Hokusai museum and chestnut sweets. A flat city with trains in every direction and a Red Cross hospital.

**Longer:** A fourth night adds a snow day at Nozawa or Togakushi's cedar avenue.

**Shorter:** Two nights drops Matsumoto or the monkeys.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** Every day has a clear plan.

##### 4

**Why:** Three days plus a snow day at Nozawa or Togakushi: sledding, a ski lesson for João and Carolina, the wooden village baths. Fernanda may prefer a slow day at the hotel or in Obuse.

**Longer:** A fifth night is a second snow day.

**Shorter:** Three nights keeps the main days out.

**Idle:** some

**Note:** One easier day.

##### 5

**Why:** A base for a Nagano week: the monkeys, Matsumoto, Obuse, Nozawa and Togakushi. Across 29 Dec–3 Jan it means Zenko-ji crowds and a closed castle.

**Longer:** A sixth night is too many for a station hotel.

**Shorter:** Four nights already holds a snow day.

**Idle:** some

**Note:** Full if you use the trains, slow if you do not.

### Places to visit (8)

- **Zenko-ji and the o-kaidan passage** (nature, short) — A seventh-century temple with a dawn service and a pitch-dark tunnel walked with one hand on the wall
  - Hours: Open all night 31 Dec into 1 Jan and very crowded then; go before 29 Dec or after 4 Jan
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Source: https://www.zenkoji.jp/annual/
- **Jigokudani snow monkeys** (onsen, main) — Wild macaques bathing in a hot spring in the snow
  - Hours: Open every day including 31 Dec and 1 Jan; busiest 29 Dec–3 Jan
  - For Fernanda: high
  - Source: https://www.snowmonkeyresorts.com/smr/snowmonkeypark/winter/
- **Shibu Onsen lane and foot baths** (onsen, main) — Lantern-lit onsen street near the monkey trail, where she can wait warm and easy
  - Hours: Open
  - For Fernanda: moderate
  - Source: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e6029.html
- **Matsumoto Castle day trip** (sight, main) — Japan's black original castle, 50 minutes away on the Shinano express
  - Hours: Closed 29–31 Dec; special opening 1–3 Jan
  - For Fernanda: moderate
  - Source: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e6051.html
- **Obuse and the Hokusai Museum** (museum, short) — A small town of chestnut sweets with a museum of Hokusai's late work, 10 minutes' walk from the station
  - Hours: Check year-end closing days
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Source: https://www.go-nagano.net/en/trip-idea/3-days-of-naganos-winter-highlights
- **Nozawa Onsen snow day** (onsen, main) — Sledding, ski lessons and wooden village bathhouses, 40 minutes by shinkansen and bus
  - Hours: Lifts and baths open; busiest 29 Dec–3 Jan
  - For Fernanda: high
  - Source: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e6028.html
- **Togakushi Chusha shrine and soba** (sight, main) — A mountain shrine among giant cedars, an hour up the Birdline by bus, with Togakushi soba served in bamboo baskets
  - Hours: Chusha and Hokosha open in winter; the upper Okusha and its cedar avenue close in deep snow (from early January) and the path past the Zuishinmon gate is shut
  - For Fernanda: moderate, Snowy steps at the Chusha; cleats help
  - Source: https://togakushi-21.jp/news/54485/
- **Matsumoto City Museum of Art (Kusama Yayoi)** (museum, short) — Kusama Yayoi's home-town museum, with her dotted flowers outside and a room of her work inside, 12 minutes' walk from Matsumoto station
  - Hours: Usually 9:00–17:00; closed Mondays and over the New Year (check the 2026 dates)

## Hakone

**Fit:** A day out — A day out from Tokyo, about 1 h 30 each way

**Prefecture:** Kanagawa

### Tags

- onsen
- fuji
- museums
- gentle

### Known for

- **Fuji over the lake**: From the ropeway, the pirate boats, the Narukawa museum lounge and the shrine gate in the water. · Place: Hakone loop: ropeway, Owakudani, Lake Ashi boat
- **Ryokan with private baths**: An open-air bath in every room at Gora Hanaougi, Kitanokaze Saryo or Hakone Ginyu.
- **Five art museums**: The Open-Air Museum's sculpture park and Picasso pavilion, Pola's Impressionists, Okada's footbath café. · Place: Hakone Open-Air Museum
- **Owakudani**: The steaming volcanic valley on the ropeway, black eggs and all. · Place: Hakone loop: ropeway, Owakudani, Lake Ashi boat

### Window

- Fuji shows fully on about six January mornings in ten; dawn and late afternoon are best.
- The Hakone Ekiden relay closes the roads on 2–3 Jan and the inns sold out for those nights a year ago; 5–7 Jan is clear.
- Onshi-Hakone Park reopens 4 Jan; Okada Museum closes 31 Dec and 1 Jan only.
- Hatsuhana soba closes Wednesdays, so it is the arrival lunch on Tue 5 Jan.

**Feel:** Steam, pines, a lake with a red torii, and a dozen ways to move: ropeway, cable car, boat, mountain railway. Eighty minutes from Shinjuku and a different century.

**Getting there:** From Kyoto about 3 h 15: Hikari to Odawara (the Nozomi does not stop), then the Hakone Tozan line. To Shinjuku 85 minutes on the Romancecar.

**For Fernanda:** The ropeway crosses volcanic gas; the operator bars some conditions, so ask her doctor. Everything else is seated. A private bath makes the onsen easy.

**Region:** tokyo-fuji

**Do line:** Art museums, ryokan baths and Fuji views

### Aliases

- Lake Ashi
- Owakudani
- Odawara
- Gora

### Verdict

**Headline:** Steam, art museums and Fuji over the hills.

**Text:** Steam rising between pines, a lake with a red shrine gate, and a mountain railway, cable car, ropeway and boat. Five art museums fill a cloudy day, and the best ryokan have an outdoor bath in every room. From Kyoto it is about 3 hours, which keeps every move under four. We chose the lake because there Fuji stands straight across the water and it is new for all seven of us; Pedro and Fernanda have already been to Hakone.

**Route:** kyoto-new-year-hakone

### How long to stay and what is on

**Best:** 2 nights (3 if the weather looks uncertain)

#### Date notes

- 19 Dec–3 Jan: Onshi-Hakone Park, the classic Fuji-over-the-lake view, is closed; it reopens 4 Jan.
- 31 Dec–1 Jan: the Okada Museum is closed; the Open-Air Museum is open every day.
- 1–3 Jan: hatsumode crowds at Hakone Shrine. 2–3 Jan: the Ekiden relay closes Route 1 and every inn sold out a year ago.
- Wednesdays (23 and 30 Dec, 6 Jan): Hatsuhana soba is closed.
- Mid-January: the ropeway's annual inspection begins, after we leave.

#### Nights

##### 1

**Why:** An afternoon arrival, the ryokan bath and kaiseki, and one morning for Fuji before leaving. No time for the loop.

**Longer:** A second night gives the full loop day and a second dawn for Fuji.

**Shorter:** A single night makes the ryokan a stopover and gives Fuji one chance.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** Arriving and leaving, with one good evening.

##### 2

**Why:** One full day for the loop by mountain railway, cable car, the ropeway over Owakudani and the boat, plus a museum, and two dawns for Fuji (clear on about six January mornings in ten). The ropeway crosses volcanic gas, so Fernanda checks with her doctor.

**Longer:** A third night is a second chance at Fuji and a second museum day.

**Shorter:** One night gives one morning for Fuji and no loop.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** Two full mornings and one full day; on a cloudy day the museums fill it.

##### 3

**Why:** The loop plus a museum day: the Open-Air Museum, the Pola's Impressionists or the Okada, and a yosegi marquetry workshop, with three dawns for Fuji.

**Longer:** A fourth night is a third museum or a slow day at the inn.

**Shorter:** Two nights keeps the loop and one museum.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** Five museums mean a cloudy day is never wasted.

##### 4

**Why:** Long, but Hakone can carry it: the loop, two museum days and a lazy ryokan day, with Lake Ashi's red torii at Hakone Shrine. Over 2–3 Jan the Ekiden closes the roads and rooms are gone.

**Longer:** A fifth night is a rest day at the inn.

**Shorter:** Three nights covers everything at an easy pace.

**Idle:** some

**Note:** One slow day; pleasant in a ryokan with a private bath.

##### 5

**Why:** Too long unless rest is the point: by day five you are repeating the loop. A good ryokan can make it a happy retreat for Fernanda and the parents.

**Longer:** Nothing to add.

**Shorter:** Four nights already holds the museums and a rest day.

**Idle:** real

**Note:** Two slow days; João and Carolina would want to move on.

### Places to visit (9)

- **Hakone Open-Air Museum** (museum, main) — Sculpture park and Picasso pavilion
  - Hours: 9:00-17:00 every day
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Source: https://www.hakone-oam.or.jp/en/
  - **Famous for:** A hillside sculpture park with Henry Moore, Miro, a stained-glass tower and a whole Picasso pavilion.
  - **Expect:** You wander lawns and terraces between large outdoor sculptures, with the hills behind; children climb inside a few of them. The Picasso building holds 300 works and there is a hot-spring footbath for tired legs. Two hours; entry 2,000 yen; open 9:00 to 17:00 every day of the year. The paths slope and have steps, and the spiral staircase inside the glass tower is optional. Next to Chokoku-no-mori station on the mountain railway.

  - Hours: 9:00-17:00
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Source: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e5214.html
  - **Famous for:** A glass-and-steel museum in a beech forest, with Monet, Renoir, Cezanne and Picasso from a cosmetics fortune.
  - **Expect:** A strong Impressionist collection in calm, well-lit rooms, plus a short forest trail with sculptures outside. Ninety minutes to two hours; entry 2,200 yen; open 9:00 to 17:00 with occasional closures between shows. The building is all lifts and ramps, with a good restaurant. Reached by a bus from Gora or Sengokuhara, which is the main effort. Rarely crowded on winter weekdays.

- **Okada Museum of Art** (museum, short) — Asian art and a footbath cafe

  - For Fernanda: low
  - Source: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e5220.html
  - **Famous for:** Five floors of Chinese, Korean and Japanese art, with a cafe where you sit in a hot-spring footbath.
  - **Expect:** A private collection of screens, ceramics and Buddhist art, beautifully displayed and with no photography allowed; phones go in a locker. Two hours, more if you linger in the footbath cafe (500 yen) facing a huge mural. Entry 2,800 yen, the highest in Hakone, and worth it for art lovers. Lifts throughout. Closed 31 December and 1 January. In Kowakudani, on the bus route between Gora and the lake.
  - **Reputation:** real
  - **Links:**

- **Narukawa Art Museum** (museum, short) — Lounge over Fuji and the lake
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Source: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e5221.html
  - **Famous for:** Modern Japanese-style paintings and a lounge with the best indoor view of Fuji over Lake Ashi.
  - **Expect:** A small museum of nihonga, delicate paintings on silk and paper, that most people visit for the panorama lounge: floor-to-ceiling glass facing the lake, the red torii of Hakone Shrine and Fuji behind. Forty-five minutes to an hour; entry 1,500 yen; cafe with the same view. By the Moto-Hakone pier, so it fits after the boat. Lifts, warm and a good place for her to wait while others climb Onshi park.
  - **Reputation:** real

- **Hakone loop: ropeway, Owakudani, Lake Ashi boat** (nature, main) — Volcanic valley and Fuji views
  - Hours: Ropeway 9:00-16:15 in winter

  - Source: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e5223.html
  - **Famous for:** The circuit of mountain railway, cable car, ropeway over a steaming volcanic valley and a boat across Lake Ashi.
  - **Expect:** A full day in sequence: train to Gora, cable car to Sounzan, ropeway over Owakudani's sulphur vents (eat a black egg) to Togendai, then the pirate-style boat 30 minutes to Moto-Hakone. All seated; the Hakone Free Pass covers it. Fuji shows from the ropeway and boat on clear mornings. The ropeway stops in strong wind, and the operator bars people with asthma or heart problems because of the gas; she should ask her doctor. Ropeway 9:00 to 16:15 in winter.

The view everyone photographs is from the lookout building at the top, reached by about 200 stone steps; it is the picture, and in person it is a good one on a clear morning. Thirty to forty-five minutes. Open 9:00 to 16:30 and closed 19 December to 3 January, so open on 5 and 6 January. The steps are the issue for her; the lakeside path at the bottom is flat.

Thirty to sixty minutes, seated, from about 1,000 to 2,000 yen, and you keep the result. The shops also sell the famous puzzle boxes. Reserve for six. Good for a wet afternoon and no strain for her.
