## Okayama, Kurashiki and Naoshima

**Fit:** A day out — A day out from Kyoto, about 1 h each way

**Prefecture:** Okayama & Kagawa

### Tags

- museums
- food

### Known for

- **Naoshima**: The Chichu Art Museum, Benesse House and the yellow pumpkin on an island in the Inland Sea.
- **Korakuen**: One of the three great gardens; cranes released on 1 Jan, free that day.
- **Kurashiki**: A canal quarter of white-walled warehouses and the Ohara Museum's El Greco.
- **Himeji**: Japan's finest original castle, 50 minutes away; closed 29–30 Dec.

### Window

- Chichu is closed Mondays and needs timed tickets a month ahead; Teshima is shut Tue–Thu in winter.
- Himeji closes exactly when a Kyoto group would go (29–30 Dec); 4 or 5 Jan works.
- Korakuen's New Year festival runs 1–3 Jan with koto music and the cranes.

**Feel:** Mild Inland Sea light, flat towns, art in concrete and a garden built for walking. The art-and-gardens alternative to Fuji.

**Getting there:** An hour from Kyoto on the Sanyo shinkansen; the Naoshima ferry is 20 minutes from Uno. Three hours twenty back to Tokyo.

**For Fernanda:** Flat towns; island walking in a cold sea wind. Himeji's keep is ladder-steep, so she stays in the grounds.

**Region:** west

**Do line:** Art islands, a great garden and Himeji

### Aliases

- Naoshima
- Kurashiki
- Korakuen
- Chichu Art Museum
- Benesse House
- Teshima
- Setouchi

### Verdict

**Headline:** Art islands and a great garden instead of Fuji.

**Text:** Naoshima's island museums, among them the Chichu Art Museum, Kurashiki's canal quarter of white-walled warehouses, and Korakuen, one of Japan's three great gardens, with Himeji Castle on the way. It is mild by the Inland Sea and an hour from Kyoto, so Korakuen and Kurashiki make a day out; Naoshima's islands need a night, and staying would cost the Fuji lake.

**Route:** art-not-fuji

### How long to stay and what is on

**Best:** 2 nights (3 for Naoshima and Kurashiki both)

#### Date notes

- Mondays (21 and 28 Dec, 4 Jan): Chichu and most of Naoshima are closed; Chichu needs timed tickets about a month ahead.
- Tuesdays (22 and 29 Dec, 5 Jan): the Teshima Art Museum is closed; last year it was also shut 31 Dec–2 Jan, and the island buses stopped.
- 31 Dec–1 Jan: Naoshima's museums have closed in recent years; the 2026–27 dates come out in autumn.
- 29–30 Dec: Himeji Castle is closed; 29–31 Dec Okayama Castle too.
- 1–3 Jan: Korakuen's New Year festival with koto music and the red-crowned cranes, free on 1 Jan; Ritsurin in Takamatsu is free on 1 Jan too.
- The Ohara Museum in Kurashiki: sources differ on its year-end closure (around 23–27 Dec or 28–31 Dec); on 1 Jan only the main gallery opens.
- From 12 Jan: Chichu closes for maintenance, after we leave.

#### Nights

##### 1

**Why:** Himeji Castle on the way in (shut 29–30 Dec) and Korakuen's garden, then on. Naoshima does not fit in one night.

**Longer:** A second night gives Naoshima: Chichu, Benesse House and the pumpkins, on a day the museums are open.

**Shorter:** A single night is a castle and a garden.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** Full, but it misses the reason for coming west.

##### 2

**Why:** Himeji or Korakuen and Kurashiki's canal on one day, a full day on Naoshima for the Chichu Art Museum on the other. Flat towns suit Fernanda; the islands mean a cold sea wind.

**Longer:** A third night adds Kurashiki properly, or Teshima if it is open.

**Shorter:** One night loses Naoshima, which is the point.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** No idle risk if the ferry runs.

##### 3

**Why:** Naoshima, Kurashiki with the Ohara Museum, and Korakuen at an easy pace, or Teshima's water-drop museum on an open day. Over 1–3 Jan, Korakuen has its New Year festival.

**Longer:** A fourth night buys Takamatsu's Ritsurin garden or Hiroshima as a day out.

**Shorter:** Two nights drops Kurashiki or Teshima.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** Three good days.

##### 4

**Why:** Room for Ritsurin in Takamatsu, a night on Naoshima itself, or Hiroshima and Miyajima as a day trip. Over 31 Dec–2 Jan the island museums are likely shut.

**Longer:** A fifth night is a rest day.

**Shorter:** Three nights covers the core.

**Idle:** some

**Note:** One slow day.

##### 5

**Why:** Long for Okayama itself; it works as a Setouchi base with Hiroshima, Ritsurin and two island days. Okayama is three hours twenty from Tokyo, so it is a long way home either way.

**Longer:** A sixth night adds nothing new.

**Shorter:** Four nights covers everything at an easy pace.

**Idle:** some

**Note:** One or two slow days.

### Places to visit (8)

- **Okayama Korakuen** (garden, main) — One of Japan's three great gardens: lawns, ponds and tea houses under the black castle; its New Year festival runs 1–3 Jan
  - Hours: Open every day; free on 1 Jan. 1–3 Jan: koto music and red-crowned cranes let out on the lawns
  - For Fernanda: low, Wide flat paths and benches; the little hill is optional
  - Source: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e5701.html
- **Okayama Castle** (sight, short) — The black "crow castle" over the river, rebuilt inside as a museum, across a footbridge from Korakuen
  - Hours: 9:00–17:30; closed 29–31 Dec
  - Closed: {"start":"2026-12-29","end":"2026-12-31","note":"Closed","source":"https://okayama-castle.jp/guide-en"}
  - For Fernanda: moderate, Stairs inside; there is a lift to some floors
  - Price: About ¥400 adults
  - Source: https://okayama-castle.jp/guide-en
- **Kurashiki canal quarter** (sight, main) — White-walled rice warehouses along a willow canal, now cafés, craft and denim shops; lovely at dusk
  - Hours: Streets always open; shops quieter 1 Jan
  - For Fernanda: low, Flat and compact; small bridges
  - Source: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e5751.html
- **Ohara Museum of Art** (museum, main) — Japan's first museum of Western art (El Greco, Monet, Gauguin), reopened in April 2026 after renovation
  - Hours: Closed Mondays; shorter winter hours. Year-end closure about 28–31 Dec, and only the main building on 1 Jan; 2026 dates to confirm
  - Closed: {"start":"2026-12-28","end":"2026-12-31","note":"Year-end closure (sources differ)","source":"https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e5754.html"}
  - Closed weekdays: Mon
  - For Fernanda: low, Flat galleries with seats
  - Source: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e5754.html
- **Chichu Art Museum, Naoshima** (museum, main) — Monet's water lilies, Turrell and De Maria in Tadao Ando's underground galleries lit by the sky
  - Hours: Closed Mondays; timed tickets about a month ahead. Closed for maintenance from 12 Jan 2027; New Year days to confirm
  - Closed: {"start":"2027-01-12","end":"2027-03-19","note":"Maintenance closure","source":"https://benesse-artsite.jp/en/news/20260630-3559.html"}
  - Closed weekdays: Mon
  - For Fernanda: moderate, Ramps and some stairs; cool and dim inside; the island means a ferry and buses in the sea wind
  - Booking: Timed online tickets, released about a month ahead
  - Source: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e5477.html
- **Benesse House Museum and the Yellow Pumpkin** (museum, short) — The hotel-museum above the sea and Kusama's spotted pumpkin on the pier, with outdoor art along the shore
  - Hours: Open daily 8:00–21:00 in normal times; the pumpkin is outdoors
  - For Fernanda: low, A shuttle bus links the sights; the shore path is flat
  - Source: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e5478.html
- **Teshima Art Museum** (museum, main) — A single concrete shell in the rice terraces where drops of water run and pool across the floor; very quiet
  - Hours: Dec–Feb closed Tuesday to Thursday; last New Year it also shut 31 Dec–2 Jan. Timed tickets
  - Closed weekdays: Tue, Wed, Thu
  - For Fernanda: moderate, Two ferries and an island bus; shoes off inside, on a cold floor
  - Booking: Timed online tickets
  - Source: https://benesse-artsite.jp/en/news/20260630-3559.html
- **Ritsurin garden, Takamatsu** (garden, main) — A huge stroll garden of sculpted pines below a wooded hill, with a tea house on the water; an hour from Okayama
  - Hours: Open every day; free on 1 Jan
  - For Fernanda: low, Flat gravel paths, long but easy; boats on the pond some days
  - Source: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e5401.html

## Nikko

**Fit:** A day out — A day out from Tokyo, about 2 h each way

**Prefecture:** Tochigi

### Tags

- snow
- museums
- classics

### Known for

- **Toshogu**: Lacquered shrines in a cedar forest, sometimes under snow.
- **Kanmangafuchi**: A riverside walk past a row of stone Jizo statues.
- **Lake Chuzenji and Kegon Falls**: The falls half-frozen, reached by lift.

### Window

- Open through New Year and packed 1–3 Jan for hatsumode; 21–23 Dec or 8 Jan are the days.
- Two hours from Tokyo on the Tobu limited express.

**Feel:** Cold, grand, and a lot of stairs: hundreds of stone steps between the shrines, worse with ice.

**Getting there:** Tobu Spacia from Asakusa, about two hours; reserved seats 30 days out.

**For Fernanda:** She stays at gate level; the climb to the tomb and the shrine stairs are for the others.

**Region:** tokyo-fuji

**Do line:** Lacquered shrines in a cedar forest

### Aliases

- Toshogu
- Kegon Falls
- Lake Chuzenji

### Verdict

**Headline:** Lacquered shrines in a cedar forest, sometimes under snow.

**Text:** Toshogu's shrines stand among tall cedars, a riverside path passes a long row of stone Jizo statues, and Kegon Falls hangs half-frozen, reached by a lift. It is a long day with hundreds of stone steps, icy in winter, so it suits whoever likes a climb; Fernanda may prefer to stay at gate level.

### How long to stay and what is on

**Best:** 1–2 nights, or a day trip from Tokyo on 21–23 Dec or 8 Jan

#### Date notes

- 31 Dec night–3 Jan: Toshogu opens around midnight for hatsumode and is packed 1–3 Jan; the roads jam.
- 31 Dec–2 Jan: half the town's restaurants close, including most of the yuba lunches.
- 21–23 Dec, or after 4 Jan: the calm days for the shrines.
- Wednesdays: Edo Wonderland is closed; it opens over New Year with short hours.
- From late December, once the snow settles: guided snowshoes on Senjogahara.

#### Nights

##### 1

**Why:** Toshogu at opening (9:00) before the tour buses, Kanmangafuchi's flat river path in the afternoon, and a Kinugawa ryokan with a family bath. The shrines are about 200 stone steps to the main gate, so Fernanda stays at gate level.

**Longer:** A second night buys Lake Chuzenji, the half-frozen Kegon Falls (a lift down, no stairs) and Yumoto's sulphur baths.

**Shorter:** A single night means the shrines without rushing for the train; without it, Nikko is a two-hour day trip from Asakusa.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** A full day and a good bath.

##### 2

**Why:** The shrines one day; the lake, Kegon Falls and Yumoto's milky baths in snow at 1,500 m the next, though the bus up has 48 hairpin bends. A big Kinugawa ryokan takes seven easily.

**Longer:** A third night adds a snowshoe on Senjogahara for the fit, or Edo Wonderland on a cold day.

**Shorter:** One night keeps the shrines and loses the lake.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** Two good days.

##### 3

**Why:** Shrines, the lake and a third day: a Senjogahara snowshoe for the parents and João and Carolina while Fernanda soaks at Yumoto, or Edo Wonderland's shows.

**Longer:** A fourth night is too many; move on to Aizu or back to Tokyo.

**Shorter:** Two nights covers the shrines and the lake.

**Idle:** some

**Note:** One easy day.

##### 4

**Why:** Too long for this group: the shrines and the lake take two days, and Kinugawa town is faded.

**Longer:** Nothing to add.

**Shorter:** Three nights already holds a snow day.

**Idle:** real

**Note:** Two idle days.

##### 5

**Why:** Not sensible: Nikko is two days at most, and its New Year is crowds at the shrines.

**Longer:** Nothing to add.

**Shorter:** Four nights is already too many.

**Idle:** real

**Note:** Mostly idle.

### Places to visit (1)

- **Nikko Toshogu and the Shinkyo bridge** (sight, day_trip) — The shogun's lacquered, gilded shrine in a cedar forest, often in snow, and the red bridge over the river at the town edge
  - Hours: 9:00-16:00 from November to March, last entry 30 minutes before
  - For Fernanda: moderate, Stone steps and icy paths; the 200-odd steps up to the tomb are optional
  - Price: About ¥1,600
  - Source: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e3801.html

## Kamakura

**Fit:** A day out — A day out from Tokyo, about 1 h each way

**Prefecture:** Kanagawa

### Tags

- classics
- gentle

### Known for

- **The Great Buddha**: Thirteen metres of bronze in the open air since 1252.
- **Temples and the sea**: Hase-dera's hydrangea slopes, Hokoku-ji's bamboo, the Enoden tram along the coast.

### Window

- An easy day from Tokyo on 21–23 Dec or 8 Jan; avoid 1–3 Jan when Tsurugaoka Hachimangu draws two million.
- Enoshima beach is the gentlest first-sunrise spot if anyone wants one.

**Feel:** Seaside, small-town, temple-dotted; an hour from Tokyo and a day well spent.

**Getting there:** About an hour on the JR Yokosuka line.

**For Fernanda:** Flat along the coast; the hill temples have stairs.

**Region:** tokyo-fuji

**Do line:** The Great Buddha, temples and the sea

### Aliases

- Great Buddha
- Daibutsu
- Enoshima
- Hase-dera
- Enoden

### Verdict

**Headline:** The Great Buddha, temples and the sea.

**Text:** A seaside town of temples, where a thirteen-metre bronze Buddha has sat in the open air since 1252. There is a bamboo grove at Hokoku-ji and a small tram along the coast. The coast is flat and the hill temples have stairs, and it fits a Tokyo day before Christmas or on 8 January.

### How long to stay and what is on

**Best:** No night (a day trip from Tokyo), or 1–2 nights at the end, an hour from Haneda

#### Date notes

- 1–3 Jan: Tsurugaoka Hachimangu draws about 2.5 million for hatsumode, with crowd control on the trains and streets. Avoid.
- 1 Jan, about 6:50: first sunrise from Enoshima's beach, the gentlest one near Tokyo.
- 21–23 Dec or 7–8 Jan: the calm days.
- Every day, 8:00–17:00 in winter: the Great Buddha and Hase-dera.
- 9 Jan: about an hour to Haneda, so it can be the last night.

#### Nights

##### 1

**Why:** The Great Buddha, Hase-dera and the bamboo garden at Hokoku-ji, the sea at dusk, and a quiet morning before the day-trippers. About an hour from Haneda, so it can be the last night.

**Longer:** A second night adds Enoshima and the north Kamakura Zen temples.

**Shorter:** A single night buys an evening by the sea; without it Kamakura is an easy day trip from Tokyo.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** Flat along the coast; the hill temples have stairs.

##### 2

**Why:** Two unhurried days: the Great Buddha and Hase-dera on one, Engaku-ji and Kencho-ji in north Kamakura and Enoshima on the other. Lodging for seven is mostly small hotels and rental houses.

**Longer:** A third night buys a Yokohama day, or Fuji from the Enoshima shore at dawn.

**Shorter:** One night keeps the main sights.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** Two calm days; a seaside pause before the flight.

##### 3

**Why:** Kamakura, Enoshima and a day in Yokohama for Chinatown and the Red Brick warehouses, close to Haneda for the 9 Jan flight.

**Longer:** A fourth night has little more.

**Shorter:** Two nights is Kamakura itself.

**Idle:** some

**Note:** One slow day.

##### 4

**Why:** Too long for a seaside temple town; Tokyo is an hour away and a better base for more.

**Longer:** Nothing to add.

**Shorter:** Three nights already includes Yokohama.

**Idle:** real

**Note:** Two idle days.

##### 5

**Why:** Not sensible; across 1–3 Jan it would mean the biggest hatsumode crush near Tokyo on the doorstep.

**Longer:** Nothing to add.

**Shorter:** Four nights is already long.

**Idle:** real

**Note:** Mostly idle.

### Places to visit (11)

- **Great Buddha of Kamakura (Kotoku-in)** (sight, short) — A 13-metre bronze Buddha sitting in the open air since the 1250s; you can step inside him for ¥50
  - Hours: 8:00-17:00 from October to March
  - For Fernanda: low, Flat; the inside is a short steep stair
  - Price: ¥300
  - Source: https://kotoku-in.jp/en/

  - For Fernanda: moderate, The lower garden is flat; the terrace and cave are up steps

- **Enoden line to Shichirigahama** (nature, short) — The little coastal railway along the beaches; at Shichirigahama Fuji stands over the sea at sunset on clear days
  - Hours: Trains every 12 minutes or so
  - For Fernanda: low, Seated; the beach is a short walk from the station
  - Source: https://www.enoden.co.jp/en/
- **Tsurugaoka Hachimangu** (sight, main) — Kamakura's great shrine at the end of the cherry-lined Dankazura avenue, with ponds and a main hall up a broad flight of steps
  - Hours: Open daily; open all night 31 Dec–2 Jan and packed 1–3 Jan (about 2.5 million visitors), with crowd control on the streets
  - For Fernanda: moderate, Main hall up a long flight of steps

- **Hokoku-ji bamboo garden** (garden, short) — A small Zen temple with a grove of 2,000 moso bamboo and matcha in a tea pavilion inside it

  - Closed: {"start":"2026-12-29","end":"2027-01-03","note":"Closed for New Year","source":"https://rurubu.jp/andmore/spot/80013695/koyo"}
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Price: About ¥400, tea extra
  - Source: https://rurubu.jp/andmore/spot/80013695/koyo
- **Enoshima** (nature, main) — A small island reached by a flat bridge, with outdoor escalators up the hill, a garden, a lighthouse tower and shrines
  - Hours: Always open; the winter lights on the tower ran 17:00–20:00 last season
  - For Fernanda: moderate, Escalators for the climb; steps beyond them

- **Enoshima Aquarium (Enosui)** (sight, short) — A bright aquarium on the beach by Enoshima, with a big Sagami Bay tank, jellyfish and a dolphin show
  - Hours: Open every day; New Year hours usually 9:00–17:00
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Source: https://report.iko-yo.net/articles/16458
- **Kencho-ji** (sight, short) — Japan's oldest Zen training monastery, in a valley in north Kamakura; on New Year's Eve anyone in the queue may ring its bell
  - Hours: 8:30–16:30; the New Year's Eve bell from about 23:45, queue before midnight
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Source: https://rurubu.jp/andmore/spot/20003422
- **Sankeien garden, Yokohama** (garden, main) — A large landscape garden with historic houses and a three-storey pagoda brought from Kyoto and Kamakura
  - Hours: 9:00–17:00; closed about 26–31 Dec in recent years; open from 1 Jan, with the Kakushokaku house shown 1–3 Jan
  - Closed: {"start":"2026-12-26","end":"2026-12-31","note":"Year-end closure in recent years; check","source":"https://www.welcome.city.yokohama.jp/eventinfo/ev_detail.php?bid=yw4848"}
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Price: ¥900
  - Source: https://www.welcome.city.yokohama.jp/eventinfo/ev_detail.php?bid=yw4848
- **Yokohama Chinatown** (food, short) — Japan's biggest Chinatown: steamed buns, soup dumplings and big round tables for seven
  - Hours: Open daily, including New Year
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Source: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e3201.html
- **Shichirigahama beach** (nature, short) — A long beach between Kamakura and Enoshima, with Fuji across the bay on clear winter evenings
  - Hours: Always open; sunset about 16:45 in early January
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Source: https://www.princehotels.com/kamakura/

## Echigo-Yuzawa

**Fit:** A day out — A day out from Tokyo, about 1 h 15 each way

**Prefecture:** Niigata

### Tags

- snow

### Known for

- **Snow Country**: Kawabata's novel was set here; the snow is deep by Christmas.
- **The sake hall**: A hundred local sakes to taste inside the station, and a sake bath.
- **GALA Yuzawa**: A ski resort with its own shinkansen station, for anyone who wants a ski day.

### Window

- Deep snow on the ground; 75 minutes from Tokyo on the Joetsu shinkansen.
- A day trip that changes no nights.

**Feel:** Step off the bullet train into a metre of snow. Nothing to see but the snow itself, which is the point.

**Getting there:** Joetsu shinkansen from Tokyo, 75 minutes.

**For Fernanda:** Easy if you stay near the station; the sake bath is warm and shallow.

**Region:** tokyo-fuji

**Do line:** Deep snow and a station sake hall

### Aliases

- Yuzawa
- GALA Yuzawa
- Niigata
- Snow Country

### Verdict

**Headline:** Step off the bullet train into deep snow.

**Text:** This is the town of Kawabata's novel Snow Country, where the snow is deep by Christmas and you can taste a hundred local sakes inside the station. There is nothing to see but the snow itself, which is the point.

### How long to stay and what is on

**Best:** No night (a day trip from Tokyo), or 1 night

#### Date notes

- GALA's opening date is announced in late autumn, usually mid to late December.
- 29 Dec–3 Jan: GALA is very busy, and Joetsu shinkansen seats are full, worst on 30 Dec and 3 Jan.
- Over New Year: the Ponshukan sake hall and its sake bath stay open.

#### Nights

##### 1

**Why:** Step off the shinkansen 75 minutes from Tokyo into a metre of snow: GALA's slopes from the station for the skiers, the Ponshukan's sake wall and warm sake bath for the rest, and a ryokan bath at night. Fernanda has an easy day near the station.

**Longer:** A second night buys Takaragawa's giant riverside baths for the parents (icy steps) or a full ski day.

**Shorter:** A single night is the snow, the sake hall and a warm bath; it also works as a day trip.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** Easy and white; the town itself is 1980s ski concrete.

##### 2

**Why:** A ski day for João and Carolina, Takaragawa or a snowy walk for the parents, and two bath evenings. Little else in town.

**Longer:** A third night is another ski day.

**Shorter:** One night is the snow and the sake hall.

**Idle:** some

**Note:** Day two is snow and baths; fine if that is the wish.

##### 3

**Why:** Only for skiers: three days of slopes, with little for Fernanda beyond the station and the inn.

**Longer:** Nothing to add.

**Shorter:** Two nights already holds the snow and the baths.

**Idle:** real

**Note:** Two idle days for the non-skiers.

##### 4

**Why:** Not sensible for this group: nothing to see but the snow itself.

**Longer:** Nothing to add.

**Shorter:** Three nights is already a ski stay.

**Idle:** real

**Note:** Mostly idle.

##### 5

**Why:** Not sensible for this group: a ski week, not a winter trip for seven.

**Longer:** Nothing to add.

**Shorter:** Four nights is already too many.

**Idle:** real

**Note:** Mostly idle.

### Places to visit (1)

- **GALA Yuzawa and the station sake hall** (nature, day_trip) — A ski area with its own shinkansen station: the gondola goes straight up into deep snow, and the Ponshukan hall in Echigo-Yuzawa station pours tastings of 100 Niigata sakes
  - Hours: Season opened 20 Dec in 2025; 2026 opening date to confirm
  - For Fernanda: moderate, Seated gondola and a warm lodge at the top; snow underfoot outside
  - Source: https://shinkansen-ticket.com/blogs/news/gala-yuzawa-opens-for-the-2025-2026-winter-season-on-december-20

## Hiroshima and Miyajima

**Fit:** A day out — A day out from Kyoto, about 1 h 45 each way

**Prefecture:** Hiroshima

### Tags

- classics
- food

### Known for

- **The Peace Park**: The dome, the museum, the cenotaph; open on 1 Jan, the museum closed 30–31 Dec.
- **Miyajima**: The floating torii, the deer, grilled oysters on the waterfront.
- **Okonomiyaki**: The Hiroshima layered version, in a building of forty stalls.

### Window

- Mild and calm; the Peace Park is open every day, the museum closed 30–31 Dec.
- Itsukushima is open from midnight on 1 Jan.
- Oyster season, but the oyster festival is in February.

**Feel:** A rebuilt city with a heavy centre and a light coast; the island at sunset makes the trip.

**Getting there:** 1 h 45 from Kyoto on the Sanyo shinkansen; four hours to Tokyo, or four to Hakone in the fuller alternative.

**For Fernanda:** Flat; Mt Misen's stairs are for the others. A long westward leg either way.

**Region:** west

**Do line:** The Peace Park, okonomiyaki and Miyajima

### Aliases

- Peace Park
- Atomic Bomb Dome
- Okonomiyaki

### Verdict

**Headline:** The Peace Park and the floating shrine gate.

**Text:** Hiroshima is a rebuilt city with an important, heavy history at its centre, and across the water Miyajima has the shrine gate that floats at high tide, deer and grilled oysters. The Peace Park is 1 h 45 from Kyoto by shinkansen, a long but easy day out. Miyajima as well needs a night there.

**Route:** golden-route-winter

### How long to stay and what is on

**Best:** 2 nights

#### Date notes

- 30–31 Dec: the Peace Memorial Museum is closed; the park is always open.
- 31 Dec, about 18:00: the Chinka-sai fire festival in Miyajima town. 1 Jan: Itsukushima opens at midnight and is crowded 1–3 Jan.
- To about 3 Jan: the Dreamination lights on Peace Boulevard.
- About 31 Dec–3 Jan: the oyster huts close; the oyster festival is in February.
- The way home is about four hours by shinkansen to Tokyo.

#### Nights

##### 1

**Why:** The Peace Park and museum on arrival and Miyajima the next morning, or the other way round; both in one day is long, and Hiroshima is about four hours from Tokyo.

**Longer:** A second night gives each its own day.

**Shorter:** A single night means the Peace Park and Miyajima on the same day.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** Long and rewarding, then a long train.

##### 2

**Why:** One day for the Peace Park and museum, one for Miyajima's shrine and floating torii (check the tide) with grilled oysters. Mild, calm weather; Mt Misen's rocky steps are for the others.

**Longer:** A third night buys Iwakuni's wooden bridge, or a night on Miyajima itself after the day visitors leave.

**Shorter:** One night puts both in one long day.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** Two full days, both good.

##### 3

**Why:** Add a night on Miyajima, Iwakuni, or Onomichi's temple hillside by ropeway, or the ferry to Dogo Onsen in Matsuyama.

**Longer:** A fourth night is a long way west for this trip.

**Shorter:** Two nights covers the city and the island.

**Idle:** some

**Note:** One gentle day.

##### 4

**Why:** Hiroshima as a western base with Okayama, Onomichi and Iwakuni as day trips; good, but the trip home stays four hours.

**Longer:** A fifth night adds nothing new.

**Shorter:** Three nights already holds a day out.

**Idle:** some

**Note:** One or two slow days.

##### 5

**Why:** Too long for the city and the island; only as a base for a Setouchi week.

**Longer:** Nothing to add.

**Shorter:** Four nights is already generous.

**Idle:** real

**Note:** Two idle days.

### Places to visit (7)

- **Peace Memorial Park and the A-Bomb Dome** (sight, short) — The ruined dome, the cenotaph, the children's monument with its paper cranes, along the river
  - Hours: Always open
  - For Fernanda: low, Flat paths; benches by the river
  - Source: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e3400.html
- **Peace Memorial Museum** (museum, main) — The story of 6 August 1945 through objects and survivors' words; hard, and essential
  - Hours: Dec–Feb 7:30–18:00; closed 30–31 Dec. Buy web tickets ahead; queues build from mid-morning
  - Closed: {"start":"2026-12-30","end":"2026-12-31","note":"Closed","source":"https://hpmmuseum.jp/?lang=eng"}
  - For Fernanda: low, Flat with lifts, but emotionally heavy; benches are few, so a break halfway helps
  - Price: ¥200 adults
  - Booking: Web tickets advised; early and evening slots need booking
  - Source: https://hpmmuseum.jp/?lang=eng
- **Okonomimura** (food, meal) — Three floors of small okonomiyaki counters: noodles, cabbage and egg layered on the griddle in front of you
  - Hours: Lunch to late; each stall sets its own New Year days
  - For Fernanda: low, Cooked through; counter stools, and a lift
  - Source: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e3406.html
- **Shukkeien garden** (garden, short) — A small landscape garden of bridges, tea houses and a carp pond, a quiet counterweight to the Peace Park
  - Hours: 9:00–17:00 in winter; closed 29–31 Dec
  - Closed: {"start":"2026-12-29","end":"2026-12-31","note":"Closed","source":"https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e3403.html"}
  - For Fernanda: low, Flat paths; one arched bridge
  - Source: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e3403.html
- **Dreamination lights** (event, short) — Peace Boulevard and the city centre lit with big light sculptures, mid-November to early January
  - Hours: Usually 17:30/18:00–22:30 nightly to about 3 Jan; 2026 dates to confirm
  - For Fernanda: low, Flat, free and open
  - Source: https://en.jal.japantravel.com/hiroshima/hiroshima-dreamination/2973
- **Hiroshima Castle grounds and Gokoku shrine** (sight, short) — The moats and Ninomaru gate of the rebuilt castle; the keep itself closed in March 2026 for earthquake safety
  - Hours: Grounds always open and lit in the evening. Gokoku shrine, inside, is the city's busiest first-visit shrine 1–3 Jan
  - For Fernanda: low, Flat; the keep is closed, so there are no stairs to face
  - Source: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e3402.html
- **Kintai bridge, Iwakuni** (sight, main) — Five wooden arches over a clear river, rebuilt to the 1673 design, with Kikko park and the castle hill behind
  - Hours: The bridge is open round the clock (small fee); about 50 min from Hiroshima by JR and bus
  - For Fernanda: moderate, The arches are steep steps up and down; the riverside paths alongside are flat
  - Price: About ¥310 return
  - Source: https://www.japan.travel/en/spot/2191/

## Osaka

**Fit:** A day out — A day out from Kyoto, about 30 min each way

**Prefecture:** Osaka

### Tags

- food

### Known for

- **Street food**: Kuromon market, takoyaki, kushikatsu, the Dotonbori lights.
- **Bunraku**: The New Year puppet-theatre programme from about 3 Jan, fully seated.
- **USJ countdown**: For João and Carolina, if they want a loud New Year's Eve.

### Window

- Kuromon stalls peak 29–31 Dec then half close 1–3 Jan.
- Fifteen minutes from Kyoto by shinkansen; Pedro and Fernanda know it.

**Feel:** Louder, funnier, hungrier than Kyoto. An evening, not a base.

**Getting there:** 15 minutes from Kyoto by shinkansen, 30 by local train.

**For Fernanda:** Flat and busy; the bunraku theatre is the gentle option.

**Region:** kansai

**Do line:** Street food, bright lights and puppet theatre

### Aliases

- Dotonbori
- Kuromon
- Universal Studios
- USJ
- Bunraku

### Verdict

**Headline:** Louder, funnier and hungrier than Kyoto.

**Text:** Takoyaki, kushikatsu, the Kuromon market and the Dotonbori lights. From about 3 January the bunraku puppet theatre has its New Year programme, all seated, a good evening for anyone who wants to sit down. Pedro and Fernanda know Osaka already, so it is an evening out, not a stop.

### How long to stay and what is on

**Best:** No night (an evening from Kyoto), or 1–2 nights

#### Date notes

- 28–30 Dec: Kuromon market at its busiest; 1–4 Jan most stalls close; fully back 5 Jan.
- 28 Dec–1 Jan: the castle tower is closed; it reopens 2 Jan.
- 31 Dec: the USJ countdown runs from 19:00 to 21:00 on 1 Jan (separate ticket); the Umeda Sky deck stays open to 01:00.
- 1–2 Jan: the Hankyu and Hanshin department stores close; the big Dotonbori chains stay open.
- From about 3 Jan: the bunraku New Year performance, fully seated.
- 9 Jan: the eve of Toka Ebisu at Imamiya Ebisu, loud and crowded.

#### Nights

##### 1

**Why:** A night in Namba: Dotonbori's lights and street food, takoyaki and okonomiyaki (cooked, which suits Fernanda), with a bunraku matinee or the Kaiyukan aquarium. It is 15 minutes from Kyoto by shinkansen.

**Longer:** A second night buys a full day: the castle park, the Kaiyukan and Shinsekai.

**Shorter:** A single night is the evening on Dotonbori; an evening out from Kyoto covers most of it.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** Loud, funny and flat; Pedro and Fernanda know it.

##### 2

**Why:** One full day and two evenings: Kuromon market, the castle, the Kaiyukan (open every day), the Umeda Sky deck. Apartment hotels for seven are easy even at New Year.

**Longer:** A third night adds Kobe or Himeji.

**Shorter:** One night is the evening on Dotonbori.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** Full of food; fine for everyone.

##### 3

**Why:** Osaka as a Kansai base: Nara, Kobe's beef and Himeji each a short train away, and Kyoto 15 minutes off. On 31 Dec USJ's countdown is there for whoever wants it.

**Longer:** A fourth night is another day trip.

**Shorter:** Two nights covers the city.

**Idle:** some

**Note:** Good food, less beauty than Kyoto.

##### 4

**Why:** Works only as a base for day trips; the city itself is two days, and over 1–3 Jan the markets and independent restaurants shut.

**Longer:** A fifth night is another train out.

**Shorter:** Three nights already holds a day trip.

**Idle:** some

**Note:** One or two slow days.

##### 5

**Why:** Too long for this group unless Kyoto is left out and Osaka becomes the Kansai base.

**Longer:** Nothing to add.

**Shorter:** Four nights is already long.

**Idle:** real

**Note:** Two idle days in the city itself.

### Places to visit (9)

- **Dotonbori and Namba at night** (food, short) — The canal of neon signs, takoyaki, okonomiyaki and kushikatsu; the big chains stay open right through New Year
  - Hours: Evenings; most chain restaurants open every day, New Year included
  - For Fernanda: low, Flat but packed shoulder to shoulder after 19:00; go early in the evening
  - Source: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e4001.html
- **Kuromon market** (market, short) — Osaka's kitchen: grilled scallops, wagyu skewers, fruit and fish counters under one long arcade
  - Hours: Mornings from about 9:00; each shop sets its own hours. Busiest 28–31 Dec, when about 150,000 people a day come for New Year food
  - Closed: {"start":"2027-01-01","end":"2027-01-04","note":"Many stalls shut; fully back about 5 Jan","source":"https://kuromon.com/"}
  - For Fernanda: moderate, Standing and crowded; much of the food is raw, so pick the grilled counters
  - Source: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e4031.html
- **Osaka Castle and its park** (sight, main) — The great keep on its stone walls, a museum inside, and a lift most of the way up; the moats and park around it are free
  - Hours: Tower about 9:00–17:00; closed 28 Dec–1 Jan, open again on 2 Jan. The park is always open
  - Closed: {"start":"2026-12-28","end":"2027-01-01","note":"Main tower closed for New Year; the park stays open","source":"https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e4000.html"}
  - For Fernanda: low, A lift to the 5th floor, then two flights of stairs; long lift queues at busy times
  - Booking: Tickets can be bought online ahead
  - Source: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e4000.html
- **Kaiyukan aquarium** (museum, main) — One of the world's great aquariums, spiralling down round a huge tank with a whale shark; warm, dry and seated in places
  - Hours: About 10:00–20:00; open over New Year, with a few maintenance days a year announced ahead
  - For Fernanda: low, Gentle downhill ramps all the way; benches by the big tank
  - Booking: Timed tickets online are worth it over the holidays
  - Source: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e4004.html
- **Shinsekai and Tsutenkaku** (food, short) — The old retro quarter under Tsutenkaku tower, famous for kushikatsu: skewers fried to order, so cooked through
  - Hours: Restaurants from late morning; many open over New Year
  - For Fernanda: low, Flat; noisy and lively rather than tiring
  - Source: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e4012.html
- **Umeda Sky Building Floating Garden** (viewpoint, short) — An open-air rooftop ring 170 m up between two towers, best at dusk over the city lights
  - Hours: About 9:30–22:30; it has run late-night countdowns on 31 Dec in past years. New Year hours to confirm
  - For Fernanda: low, Lifts and an escalator in a glass tube; the roof deck is windy and cold
  - Source: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e4002.html
- **Sumiyoshi Taisha** (sight, short) — One of Japan's oldest shrines, with a steep red arched bridge and halls in a style older than Buddhism in Japan
  - Hours: Grounds open daily. 1–3 Jan it takes over two million first visitors and is a crush; calm in late December and from 4 Jan
  - For Fernanda: low, Flat grounds; the drum bridge is very steep, and there is a flat path round it
  - Source: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e4007.html
- **Umeda department-store food halls** (food, short) — Hankyu and Hanshin's basements: bento, sweets, cooked New Year dishes; the first-sale lucky bags go on sale on 2 Jan
  - Hours: About 10:00–20:00; close early on 31 Dec and shut on 1 Jan. The New Year sale starts on 2 Jan and is very busy
  - Closed: {"start":"2027-01-01","end":"2027-01-01","note":"Department stores closed","source":"https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e2276.html"}
  - For Fernanda: low, Crowded and warm; nowhere to sit, so buy and take it back
  - Source: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e4009.html
- **Universal Studios Japan** (event, main) — The film theme park with Super Nintendo World and Harry Potter; a big paid countdown party on 31 Dec
  - Hours: Open every day; the 31 Dec countdown needs its own ticket and runs late. Long queues all holiday week
  - For Fernanda: high, Hours of standing in queues and a lot of walking; better for João and Carolina
  - Booking: Tickets and Express passes sell out over New Year; book ahead
  - Source: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e4021.html

## Matsumoto

**Fit:** A day out — A day out from Yudanaka, about 2 h each way

**Prefecture:** Nagano

### Tags

- snow
- museums

### Known for

- **The black castle**: One of five original keeps, with the Alps behind it.
- **Shirahone Onsen**: Milky-white baths up the valley.

### Window

- The keep's inner stairs are ladder-steep; the view is from the grounds.
- Only on the way if the snow monkeys go into the route.

**Feel:** A clean mountain city with one great building in it.

**Getting there:** On the Nagano–Takayama line of travel; an hour from Nagano.

**For Fernanda:** She views the castle from outside.

**Region:** alps

**Do line:** A black original castle below the Alps

### Aliases

- Matsumoto Castle
- Shirahone Onsen

### Verdict

**Headline:** A black original castle with the Alps behind it.

**Text:** One of Japan's original castle keeps stands black against the snowy Alps, in a clean mountain city with milky-white baths up the valley. It is about two hours from Yudanaka, and it is our plan B for Sat 26 Dec if nobody wants snow; the keep's inner stairs are ladder-steep, so Fernanda may prefer the view from the grounds.

**Route:** deep-snow-country

### How long to stay and what is on

**Best:** 1 night, on the way between Nagano and Takayama

#### Date notes

- 29–31 Dec: the castle is closed; 1–3 Jan it opens 10:00–15:30; normal hours from 4 Jan.
- 29 Dec–3 Jan and Mondays: the City Museum of Art, with the Kusama rooms, is closed.
- 1–3 Jan: many Nakamachi shops close.
- The bus to Takayama crosses a mountain pass on chains, about 2.5 hours, and snow can hold it; Kamikochi is closed all winter.
- A white day is maybe one chance in three; Matsumoto is on the dry side.

#### Nights

##### 1

**Why:** The black castle with the Alps behind it, Nakamachi's white storehouses and Nawate's frog street. The keep's inner stairs are ladder-steep, so Fernanda sees it from the grounds.

**Longer:** A second night buys Shirahone Onsen's milky baths in the snow, an hour by bus.

**Shorter:** A single night is the castle and the old streets; without it, the castle fits as a stop on the way.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** One real sight; a clean, easy city for a night.

##### 2

**Why:** The castle, Nakamachi and the Kusama rooms at the City Museum of Art one day; Shirahone or the Azumino countryside the next.

**Longer:** A third night adds little.

**Shorter:** One night covers the castle and the old streets.

**Idle:** some

**Note:** Day two is a half day out.

##### 3

**Why:** Too long for one great building and a few streets, unless it is a base for Shirahone and Nagano.

**Longer:** Nothing to add.

**Shorter:** Two nights already holds a day out.

**Idle:** real

**Note:** A quiet, cold extra day.

##### 4

**Why:** Not sensible: Matsumoto is a stop, not a stay.

**Longer:** Nothing to add.

**Shorter:** Three nights is already too many.

**Idle:** real

**Note:** Mostly idle.

##### 5

**Why:** Not sensible: Matsumoto is a stop, not a stay.

**Longer:** Nothing to add.

**Shorter:** Four nights is already too many.

**Idle:** real

**Note:** Mostly idle.

## Arima Onsen

**Fit:** A day out — A day out from Kyoto, about 1 h 30 each way

**Prefecture:** Hyogo

### Tags

- onsen
- food

### Known for

- **Gold and silver water**: Kin-no-yu's iron-red spring, hot and famous, and Gin-no-yu's clear, fizzy water, which is gentler.
- **Ryokan with private baths**: Hyoe Koyokaku, Arima Grand Hotel and Taketoritei Maruyama all have baths you can book for yourselves.
- **Irizome-shiki**: On 2 Jan, priests, inn-keepers and geisha carry the founders' statues through town and pour the year's first hot water.
- **Kobe and Himeji**: Kitano's old merchants' houses and Kobe beef 30 minutes away; Himeji Castle about an hour.

### Window

- Mild; snow is rare and light.
- Both public baths close on 1 Jan, and many shops too. That evening the ryokan bath is the bath.
- Ryokan rates run 50–100% higher on 31 Dec–2 Jan.

**Feel:** A steep little spa town in a fold of the Rokko hills: rust-red water, wooden lanes and old sweet shops. An hour from Osaka and a world away.

**Getting there:** About 75 minutes from Kyoto (train to Sannomiya, then the bus), an hour from Osaka by direct bus, and about three hours from Kinosaki.

**For Fernanda:** The lanes are steep, so take taxis between the inn and the baths. The gold spring runs hot; a private bath at the inn is the easy way. Saiseikai Hyogo Prefectural Hospital, a regional perinatal centre, is about 20 minutes by car.

**Region:** kansai

**Do line:** Gold and silver hot springs near Kobe

### Aliases

- Arima Onsen
- Kobe

### Verdict

**Headline:** A steep little spa town of gold and silver water.

**Text:** Rust-red “gold” water and clear, fizzy “silver” water, in a steep town of wooden lanes and old sweet shops an hour from Osaka, with Kobe 30 minutes away. From Kyoto it makes a day out with Kobe; a night there belongs to a route of onsen towns.

**Route:** western-onsen-trail

### How long to stay and what is on

**Best:** 2 nights (3–4 as a base for Kobe and Himeji)

#### Date notes

- 29–30 Dec: Himeji Castle is closed. It is open, and busy, on 31 Dec and 1–3 Jan.
- 31 Dec–2 Jan: most Kobe beef counters close.
- 1 Jan: both public baths, Kin-no-yu and Gin-no-yu, are shut and many shops close. The ryokan bath and dinner are the evening.
- 2 Jan: Irizome-shiki, the first-bath ceremony. Check the 2027 time with the tourist office.
- Kin-no-yu closes on the 2nd and 4th Tuesdays and Gin-no-yu on the 1st and 3rd, so Gin-no-yu is shut on Tue 5 Jan.
- New Year: ryokan rates are 50–100% higher on 31 Dec–2 Jan.

#### Nights

##### 1

**Why:** One bath, one dinner and a morning in the lanes. Arima is about 75 minutes from Kyoto and an hour from Osaka, so one night works on the way through.

**Longer:** A second night gives a whole day: the gold and silver baths, the lanes and the sweet shops.

**Shorter:** As a day trip from Kyoto or Osaka you miss the ryokan, which is the point.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** Short and complete.

##### 2

**Why:** A full day in town: the gold spring for the others, the cooler silver spring, the hill lanes and sweet shops, and two ryokan dinners. Fernanda can keep to the private baths.

**Longer:** A third night adds a Kobe day: Kitano's old houses, the harbour and beef.

**Shorter:** One night is a single evening.

**Idle:** some

**Note:** The town takes a morning; the inn fills the rest.

##### 3

**Why:** Arima, plus Kobe as a day out, 30 minutes away. Over New Year, add the first-bath ceremony on 2 Jan.

**Longer:** A fourth night adds Himeji Castle, about an hour away.

**Shorter:** Two nights loses Kobe.

**Idle:** some

**Note:** One full day out and one slow one.

##### 4

**Why:** Arima as a base for 1–5 Jan: a quiet arrival on the 1st when the public baths are shut, the ceremony on the 2nd, then Kobe and Himeji, and out after the return rush.

**Longer:** A fifth night is a second slow day.

**Shorter:** Leaving on 4 Jan puts the move on the last peak day.

**Idle:** some

**Note:** The arrival on 1 Jan is quiet, as expected.

##### 5

**Why:** Too long for a small hill town. Kobe and Himeji are the only big days out.

**Longer:** Nothing to add.

**Shorter:** Four nights is already generous.

**Idle:** real

**Note:** Two slow days.

### Places to visit (6)

- **Irizome-shiki first-bath ceremony** (onsen, short) — Priests, inn-keepers and geisha carry the statues of the spring's founders and pour the year's first hot water, on 2 Jan
  - Hours: Held every 2 January; check the 2027 time with the tourist office
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Source: https://www.ryuusenkaku.jp/english/annual.html
- **Gin-no-yu (silver spring)** (sight, main) — Clear carbonated water, gentler and cooler than the gold spring
  - Hours: Closed 1 Jan and the 1st and 3rd Tuesdays
  - For Fernanda: moderate
  - Source: https://arimaspa-kingin.jp/en-02.htm
- **Kin-no-yu (gold spring)** (onsen, main) — Iron-red water that is Arima's signature; hot, so for the others, with the free foot bath outside for her
  - Hours: Closed 1 Jan and the 2nd and 4th Tuesdays
  - For Fernanda: moderate
  - Source: https://arimaspa-kingin.jp/en-01.htm
- **Arima's hill lanes** (sight, main) — Old sweet shops, carbonated-spring crackers and tiny shrines on steep lanes
  - Hours: Many shops shut on 1 Jan
  - For Fernanda: moderate
  - Source: https://visit.arima-onsen.com/things-to-do/buddhism-heritage
- **Kobe day** (food, short) — Kitano's old foreign merchants' houses, the harbour and Kobe beef, 30 minutes away
  - Hours: Most beef counters closed 31 Dec–2 Jan
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Source: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kita-ku,_Kobe
- **Himeji Castle day** (sight, main) — Japan's finest original castle, about an hour via Shin-Kobe
  - Hours: Closed 29–30 Dec; open 31 Dec and 1–3 Jan, busy
  - For Fernanda: moderate
  - Source: https://www.hyogo-tourism.jp/world/spot/detail_1054.html

## Nagoya

**Fit:** A day out — A day out from Kyoto, about 45 min each way

**Prefecture:** Aichi

### Tags

- food
- museums

### Known for

- **Hitsumabushi**: Grilled eel on rice eaten three ways; Atsuta Houraiken has served it since 1873.
- **Red miso and fried wings**: Miso-katsu, miso-nikomi udon and tebasaki chicken wings, all cooked and all filling.
- **The Honmaru Palace**: Nagoya Castle's rebuilt palace of painted screens and gold, all on one level.
- **Atsuta Shrine**: Home of one of the imperial regalia, in a grove of great camphor trees.
- **SCMaglev and Railway Park**: JR Central's train museum, with old shinkansen, steam engines and the maglev. Closed on Tuesdays.

### Window

- Nagoya Castle closes 29 Dec–1 Jan; the wooden keep itself is shut for rebuilding, but the palace is open.
- Atsuta Shrine takes over two million visitors on 1–3 Jan; from about 5 Jan it is calm.
- Atsuta Houraiken's main shop closes on Wednesdays and the 2nd and 4th Thursdays; the department-store branches are the fallback.
- Dry, cold and usually sunny.

**Feel:** Japan's fourth city, flat and practical, with a big appetite: eel, red miso and fried wings, the Osu shopping arcades, and a castle palace in gold. A good place to break a long journey and eat well.

**Getting there:** On the Tokaido shinkansen: 1 h 40 from Tokyo, 35 minutes from Kyoto, about 3 h 20 from Hakata. The Kintetsu to Ise-Shima leaves from next door.

**For Fernanda:** Flat, with lifts, taxis and big hotels joined to the station. Everything above is cooked. Nagoya University Hospital and Nagoya City University Hospital are 15–20 minutes by taxi.

**Region:** alps

**Do line:** Eel, red miso and a golden palace

### Aliases

- Nagoya Castle
- Atsuta Shrine
- Hitsumabushi
- Aichi

### Verdict

**Headline:** Japan's fourth city, with a big appetite.

**Text:** Grilled eel eaten three ways, red miso and fried chicken wings, a rebuilt castle palace of gold screens, and a big train museum. It is 35 minutes from Kyoto by shinkansen, an easy day out for the food and the train museum.

**Route:** winter-food-trail

### How long to stay and what is on

**Best:** 2 nights

#### Date notes

- 29 Dec–1 Jan: Nagoya Castle and the Honmaru Palace are closed; the keep is shut for rebuilding all year.
- 1–3 Jan: Atsuta Shrine and Osu Kannon are a crush.
- Tuesdays: the SCMaglev and Railway Park is closed (Tue 5 Jan).
- Wednesdays and the 2nd and 4th Thursdays: Atsuta Houraiken's main shop is closed (Wed 6 Jan).
- From about 5 Jan: restaurants and museums are back to normal.

#### Nights

##### 1

**Why:** A stopping place: an evening of tebasaki and miso, then on. It splits a long ride, such as Hakata to Tokyo, into two easy halves.

**Longer:** A second night gives one full day: Atsuta, eel and the castle palace.

**Shorter:** A single night is the least it can be.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** No time for the city, and that is the point.

##### 2

**Why:** One full day: Atsuta Shrine in the morning, hitsumabushi for lunch, the Honmaru Palace in the afternoon, miso-nikomi udon at night.

**Longer:** A third night adds Inuyama's original castle or the railway museum.

**Shorter:** One night is a dinner stop.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** One busy day with little walking.

##### 3

**Why:** Two days: the city on one; on the other, Inuyama Castle, one of the twelve originals, 30 minutes by Meitetsu (steep inside, so Fernanda may wait below), or the Toyota museum and the railway park.

**Longer:** A fourth night only makes sense as a base for Ise, which is better slept in.

**Shorter:** Two nights loses the day out.

**Idle:** some

**Note:** Comfortable; Nagoya does not need three days.

##### 4

**Why:** Only as a base for day trips: Inuyama, Ise Jingu (about 1 h 40 by Kintetsu) and the Toyota sites. Sleeping in Ise-Shima is kinder than a long day trip.

**Longer:** Nothing to add.

**Shorter:** Three nights is enough.

**Idle:** some

**Note:** One quiet day.

##### 5

**Why:** Not sensible: a week of day trips from a city that is good for two days.

**Longer:** Nothing to add.

**Shorter:** Four nights is already long.

**Idle:** real

**Note:** Two idle days.

### Places to visit (10)

- **Hitsumabushi** (food, short) — Grilled eel on rice eaten three ways; Atsuta Houraiken claims to have invented it
  - Hours: The main shop closes on Wednesdays and the 2nd and 4th Thursdays; use a branch on 6 Jan
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Source: https://thegate12.com/spot/245
- **Miso-katsu, tebasaki and miso-nikomi udon** (sight, short) — Nagoya's red-miso and fried-wing classics, all cooked
  - Hours: Back to normal by 5 Jan
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Source: https://www.japan-Guide.com/e/e3315.html
- **Atsuta Shrine** (sight, short) — One of Japan's most important shrines, in a cedar grove; calm again after the New Year crowds
  - Hours: Very busy 1–3 Jan; fine from 5 Jan
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Source: https://www.japan-Guide.com/e/e3315.html
- **Nagoya Castle and the Honmaru Palace** (sight, short) — The rebuilt palace's painted screens, all on one level
  - Hours: Closed around New Year; open again by 5 Jan
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Source: https://www.japan-Guide.com/e/e3315.html

## Atami

**Fit:** A day out — A day out from Tokyo, about 1 h each way

**Prefecture:** Shizuoka (Izu)

### Tags

- onsen
- museums
- gentle

### Known for

- **Fireworks over the bay**: Twenty minutes from 20:20 on 25 Dec, the last of the year, watched from the flat beach promenade.
- **MOA Museum of Art**: Japanese and Chinese art and a golden tea room on the hilltop, reached by long escalators, with the sea below.
- **Kiunkaku**: A 1919 villa of gardens and Western rooms where Japan's great novelists stayed.
- **Capybaras in the bath**: At Izu Shaboten Zoo they soak in a yuzu-scented hot bath at 10:30 and 13:30 over the holidays.
- **The Jogasaki coast**: Black lava cliffs and a suspension bridge over the surf, near Izu-Kogen.

### Window

- 25 Dec: the last winter fireworks over the bay, 20:20–20:40.
- Kiunkaku closes on Wednesdays and over the year-end, about 26–30 Dec, so 25 Dec is the day for it.
- The MOA Museum closes on Thursdays; its New Year dates are not yet published.
- Mild, dry and sunny, about 12 °C by day; the plum garden and Kawazu's early cherries come after us.

**Feel:** A steep little resort tumbling down to a sandy bay: old shopping streets, steam from the hot springs and the sea in nearly every view. Livelier than Shuzenji and warmer than Tokyo.

**Getting there:** 45 minutes from Tokyo by Kodama or Hikari. From Lake Kawaguchi, about 2 h 45 by bus to Mishima and one stop on the shinkansen.

**For Fernanda:** The town is steep, but the seafront is flat, taxis are cheap and the MOA's escalators do the climbing. Choose a hotel with a private bath or quiet bath hours. IUHW Atami Hospital in town has obstetrics; Juntendo University Shizuoka Hospital, a major perinatal centre, is 35–40 minutes by car.

**Region:** tokyo-fuji

**Do line:** Seaside spa, art museum, winter fireworks

### Aliases

- Izu
- MOA Museum of Art

### Verdict

**Headline:** A steep, warm resort above a sandy bay.

**Text:** Old shopping streets, steam from the hot springs and the sea in nearly every view, with the MOA art museum on the hilltop and the year's last fireworks over the bay on 25 December. We are in Yudanaka that night, and Atami fits best on a route along the Pacific.

**Route:** fuji-pacific-shore

### How long to stay and what is on

**Best:** 2 nights (3 with Izu-Kogen)

#### Date notes

- 25 Dec, 20:20–20:40: the last winter fireworks over the bay.
- Wednesdays, and about 26–30 Dec: Kiunkaku is closed.
- Thursdays: the MOA Museum is closed; check its New Year dates.
- 22 Dec–11 Jan: capybaras in the yuzu bath at Izu Shaboten Zoo, 10:30 and 13:30.
- 31 Dec–3 Jan: the hotels are at their fullest and dearest, and Kinomiya shrine's giant camphor draws New Year crowds.
- Mid-January: the plum garden opens, after us.

#### Nights

##### 1

**Why:** An evening by the sea, with the fireworks if it is 25 Dec. Atami is 45 minutes from Tokyo, so one night works as a stop on the way into Izu.

**Longer:** A second night gives a day for the MOA Museum and Kiunkaku.

**Shorter:** A single night is a bath and a seafront walk.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** Easy and short.

##### 2

**Why:** One full day: the MOA Museum in the morning, Kiunkaku and the old shopping streets after lunch, a warm walk along the bay.

**Longer:** A third night adds Izu-Kogen: the capybaras and the Jogasaki cliffs.

**Shorter:** One night is an evening.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** Gentle and full.

##### 3

**Why:** Two full days: Atami's museums and seafront on one, Izu-Kogen's zoo and sea cliffs on the other, 25 minutes down the Ito line. With the fireworks on the 25th, the right length.

**Longer:** A fourth night adds Odawara Castle, or Hatsushima island if the sea is calm.

**Shorter:** Two nights loses Izu-Kogen.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** Two easy days.

##### 4

**Why:** Atami plus day trips: Odawara Castle, Hakone's museums, or the Mishima Skywalk with Fuji behind it. Fine as a slow base.

**Longer:** Nothing nearby that Shuzenji or Hakone do not do better.

**Shorter:** Three nights is the usual stay.

**Idle:** some

**Note:** One easy day.

##### 5

**Why:** Too long for a small resort unless the point is rest; over New Year it is busy and dear.

**Longer:** Nothing to add.

**Shorter:** Four nights is already long.

**Idle:** real

**Note:** One or two idle days.

### Places to visit (9)

- **Atami bay fireworks** (event, short) — Twenty minutes of fireworks over the bay on 25 Dec, from the flat beach promenade
  - Hours: 25 Dec, 20:20–20:40
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Source: https://www.nta.co.jp/kokunai/special/eventinfo/10612957.htm
- **MOA Museum of Art** (museum, short) — Japanese and East Asian art in a hilltop building reached by long escalators, with sea views
  - Hours: Usually closed Thursdays; New Year dates not yet published
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Source: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e6317.html

  - For Fernanda: low

  - Hours: Twice daily from 22 Dec to 11 Jan
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Source: https://www.haveagood-holiday.com/en/articles/izu-shaboten-zoo-capybara-outdoor-bath-30th-anniversary-2026

  - Hours: Always open

  - Hours: Always open
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Source: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e6317.html

  - Hours: Always open; crowded from midnight on 31 Dec and through 1–3 Jan
  - For Fernanda: low

  - Hours: About ten sailings a day; cancelled in rough seas. ¥2,900 return

## Takayama

**Fit:** Would mean a change — About 2 h 30 from Kanazawa: two nights here means two fewer somewhere else

**Prefecture:** Gifu

### Tags

- snow
- food
- museums
- classics

### Known for

- **The old town**: Edo-era merchant streets, sake breweries with cedar balls over the door, the morning market. · Place: Sanmachi sake breweries
- **Hida beef**: Yakiniku, shabu-shabu, steak on the teppan, skewers at the market. · Place: Maruaki
- **Hida Folk Village**: Farmhouses moved to a hillside, open every day of the year. · Place: Hida Folk Village
- **Takayama Jinya**: The only surviving provincial government house; closes 29 Dec. · Place: Takayama Jinya

### Window

- About 66 cm of snowfall in December; expect white streets.
- The Jinya closes 29 Dec–3 Jan, so it is an arrival-afternoon visit on the 28th.
- Kusakabe House closes Tuesdays (29 Dec); Yoshijima closes Monday to Thursday in winter.
- On Tue 29 Dec, Kyoya, Menya Shirakawa and Heianraku are shut; Maruaki is open every day but New Year's Day.

**Feel:** A mountain town that smells of cedar and grilled beef, bigger than it looks and quieter than its reputation in winter. One full day is right; a second buys the Alps.

**Getting there:** By Nohi bus from Kanazawa via Shirakawa-go (2.5 h total), or four hours from Tokyo via Nagoya. The Hida limited express runs down the valley to Gero in 45 minutes.

**For Fernanda:** Flat centre, icy pavements; cleats. The folk village is a hillside. A regional hospital in town.

**Region:** alps

**Do line:** Old town, morning market, sake and Hida beef

### Aliases

- Hida
- Hida Folk Village
- Hida-Furukawa
- Hida beef

### Verdict

**Headline:** An old mountain town of cedar, sake and beef.

**Text:** Takayama smells of cedar and grilled beef: Edo-era merchant streets, a morning market by the river with a New Year decorations market at the end of December, sake breweries with cedar balls over the door, and the Hida Folk Village on a hillside. The town fills about one good day, and the Jinya, the old government house, closes from 29 Dec. We dropped it to save a move; the 'with Takayama' route keeps two nights here, arriving via Shirakawa-go.

**Route:** kyoto-new-year-takayama

### How long to stay and what is on

**Best:** 2 nights (3 with an Okuhida day)

#### Date notes

- 19–25 Dec: Hida Folk Village has its Christmas light-up.
- 28–31 Dec: the Jinya-mae morning market turns into a New Year decorations market.
- 29 Dec–3 Jan: the Jinya is shut; arriving on the 28th, see it that afternoon.
- Tuesdays (22 and 29 Dec, 5 Jan): Kusakabe House, Kyoya, Menya Shirakawa and Heianraku are closed. Yoshijima House is shut Monday to Thursday all winter.
- 1 Jan: many Sanmachi shops and Maruaki close; about half reopen 2–3 Jan, and the markets run with fewer stalls.
- 29 Dec–3 Jan: Nohi buses to Shirakawa-go, Kanazawa and Okuhida run a holiday timetable; book seats.

#### Nights

##### 1

**Why:** One afternoon and one morning: the Jinya and the old town on arrival, the morning market and a brewery before leaving. Just possible, and the Jinya is shut 29 Dec–3 Jan.

**Longer:** A second night gives the full town day: market, breweries, Hida beef and the Folk Village.

**Shorter:** A single night is the old town at a trot.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** No idle time, and no slack if the bus from Shirakawa-go runs late in snow.

##### 2

**Why:** An arrival afternoon plus one full day: the Jinya, the morning markets, Sanmachi's breweries, the Folk Village and Hida beef at Maruaki. The right length unless you add the mountains.

**Longer:** A third night buys Okuhida: the Shinhotaka ropeway and a snow bath, a long cold day.

**Shorter:** One night means the Jinya and the old town in one afternoon, then leaving.

**Idle:** some

**Note:** If day two is Tue 29 Dec, the Jinya, Kusakabe House and three good restaurants are shut: a half day of things.

##### 3

**Why:** The town plus a day out: Okuhida's ropeway and snow baths, 90 minutes by bus, or Hida-Furukawa's canal town 15 minutes away for a gentler one. Over New Year it is a snowy, open place to sit out the busy travel days, with a ryokan dinner on the 31st.

**Longer:** A fourth night is Shirakawa-go as a day trip, or another slow day.

**Shorter:** Two nights keeps the town and drops the mountains.

**Idle:** some

**Note:** One easy day in three; Okuhida is long and cold for Fernanda, so she may prefer Furukawa or the inn.

##### 4

**Why:** Works only at a gentle pace with day trips: the town, Shirakawa-go, Okuhida and Hida-Furukawa, one a day, with New Year's Eve at a ryokan in the old town. Over 29 Dec–3 Jan the Jinya and some museums are shut.

**Longer:** A fifth night adds nothing new.

**Shorter:** Three nights loses one day trip.

**Idle:** real

**Note:** Two of the four days are day trips; without them it is too long.

##### 5

**Why:** Too long for a one-day town, even with every day trip; the fifth day is a second walk through the same streets. Only as a snowbound rest in a machiya with a kitchen.

**Longer:** Nothing to add.

**Shorter:** Four nights already covers every day trip.

**Idle:** real

**Note:** At least two idle days.

### Places to visit (14)

- **Hida Folk Village** (museum, main) — Open-air museum of thatched farmhouses
  - Hours: 8:30-17:00 every day
  - For Fernanda: moderate, Slopes, icy
  - Source: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e5901.html
  - **Famous for:** An open-air museum of 30 thatched farmhouses moved from Hida villages, set around a pond on a hillside.
  - **Expect:** You walk between the houses, step inside to see hearths, looms and tools, and watch craftspeople at work in a few of them. Ninety minutes to two hours. In snow it looks like a small Shirakawa-go with far fewer people. Entry 700 yen; open 8:30 to 17:00 every day of the year. The paths climb and get icy, so boots and care for her; the loop can be shortened. Ten minutes by bus from Takayama station.
  - **Reputation:** classic

Forty-five minutes to an hour; entry 440 yen. The floors are cold in winter, so thick socks. Flat throughout. Closed 29 December to 3 January, so see it on the afternoon of 28 December.
