## Gero Onsen

**Fit:** Would mean a change — About 2 h 30 from Kyoto: two nights here means two fewer somewhere else

**Prefecture:** Gifu

### Tags

- onsen
- gentle

### Known for

- **One of Japan's three great hot springs**: Smooth alkaline water, named among the three great springs by the scholar Hayashi Razan; three public baths and free footbaths along the river. · Place: Gero footbaths and Yumeguri pass
- **A ryokan New Year**: Kaiseki and toshikoshi soba on the 31st, osechi breakfast on the 1st. A quiet night: no public event in town, and no bell for visitors confirmed.
- **Gassho Village**: Ten thatched houses and folk crafts, open on 31 Dec and 1–2 Jan. · Place: Gero Onsen Gassho Village

### Window

- Open through New Year; the ryokan serve every meal while the town's shops close.
- The ¥1,300 bath pass covers three ryokan baths.
- Gero's winter fireworks start about 10 Jan, after we leave.
- About 2 °C milder than Takayama; snow is possible but not the point.

**Feel:** A classic spa town, not a scenic village: hotels along a river, yukata on the bridge, steam from the footbaths. The point is the inn: the bath, the dinner, the quiet.

**Getting there:** Forty-five minutes from Takayama by limited express. To Kyoto, about 2¼–2½ h via Nagoya; the one direct train takes about 3 h (check the 2027 timetable).

**For Fernanda:** Flat, lifts in the big inns, a private bath in the room. Medical advice would put these nights in a city with a perinatal centre; Gero has a prefectural hospital, and Kyoto is under two hours away.

**Region:** alps

**Do line:** Hot baths, footbaths and a ryokan New Year

### Aliases

- Gero Onsen

### Verdict

**Headline:** A quiet ryokan New Year in a famous spa town.

**Text:** The water really is special: Hayashi Razan, a 17th-century scholar, named Gero one of Japan's three great hot springs, with Arima and Kusatsu, and it feels smooth and silky. The town is a riverside resort of big 1970s hotels, not a pretty old village. There are free footbaths and the thatched Gassho Village, but no event on New Year's Eve and no bell for visitors that is confirmed (ask the ryokan). Its best part, the hot baths, is what Fernanda can enjoy least. The direct train on to Kyoto takes about 3 hours, or 2¼–2½ via Nagoya.

**Route:** gero-new-year

### How long to stay and what is on

**Best:** 2 nights, ideally 30 Dec–1 Jan for a ryokan New Year's Eve

#### Date notes

- 31 Dec–2 Jan: ryokan New Year plans with toshikoshi soba and an osechi breakfast, at a surcharge of often 50–100%; shops in town close but the inns serve every meal.
- 31 Dec–2 Jan: the Gassho Village opens 9:00–16:00.
- 31 Dec: ask the inn whether Onsen-ji lets guests ring the bell at midnight.
- Once a day a direct Hida train runs to Kyoto (around 16:18); check the 2027 timetable, otherwise change at Nagoya.
- Gero's winter fireworks start about 10 Jan, after we leave.

#### Nights

##### 1

**Why:** One evening at the inn: a bath, kaiseki and breakfast. If it is New Year's Eve, one night means moving on 31 Dec or 1 Jan with no quiet day either side, and 31 Dec is a peak travel day.

**Longer:** A second night gives a quiet day for the footbaths, the bath pass and a nap before dinner.

**Shorter:** A single night is the inn and nothing else: a stopover.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** No idle time, but most of it is arriving and leaving.

##### 2

**Why:** One quiet day between two inn evenings: the footbaths, three ryokan baths on the ¥1,300 pass, the Gassho Village and kaiseki. Made for New Year's Eve: arrive on the 30th, the bell at midnight, leave after the New Year breakfast.

**Longer:** A third night is a day at the inn; Gero is a spa town, not a sight.

**Shorter:** One night means a travel day either side and no quiet day.

**Idle:** some

**Note:** The full day is for the bath, a walk and a nap: pleasant, not beautiful.

##### 3

**Why:** Two quiet days: fine if the point is rest through the busiest week, with Takayama 45 minutes up the valley for a half day. Otherwise the town runs out.

**Longer:** A fourth night is another day of baths.

**Shorter:** Two nights keeps New Year's Eve and loses a lazy day.

**Idle:** real

**Note:** Be honest about this one: day three is the inn again.

##### 4

**Why:** Only as a deliberate retreat: four inn dinners in a row, with Takayama as a half day out. New Year surcharges make it dear.

**Longer:** Nothing to add.

**Shorter:** Three nights is already generous.

**Idle:** real

**Note:** Two or three idle days.

##### 5

**Why:** Too long for a town of hotels along a river; even the most restful week would want somewhere with more to look at.

**Longer:** Nothing to add.

**Shorter:** Four nights is already a lot of baths.

**Idle:** real

**Note:** Mostly idle; split it with Takayama or Kyoto.

### Places to visit (3)

- **Gero footbaths and Yumeguri pass** (onsen, short) — Three ryokan baths for ¥1,300
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Source: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e5990.html
  - **Famous for:** One of Japan's three great hot springs, with free footbaths around town and a pass to three ryokan baths.
  - **Expect:** Gero is a small spa town on a river. Buy the 1,300 yen Yumeguri Tegata wooden pass at any ryokan or the tourist office; it admits you to three participating baths and is valid for six months. In between, there are half a dozen free footbaths, including one by the station and one with a cafe. The water is clear, alkaline and smooth. For her, footbaths freely and short soaks at 40 degrees or below. Allow a lazy half day.
  - **Reputation:** classic
  - **Links:**
  -   - **Official site (Gero tourism)**: Url: https://www.gero-spa.com/
  -   - **japan-guide**: Url: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e5990.html
- **Gero Onsen Gassho Village** (museum, short) — Thatched houses; 9:00-16:00 on 31 Dec - 2 Jan
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Source: https://www.gero-gassho.jp/lg_en/
  - **Famous for:** Ten thatched farmhouses moved from Shirakawa-go to a hillside park above Gero, with crafts and a long slide.
  - **Expect:** A compact open-air museum you can see in an hour: houses with hearths, a folk museum, a pottery and paper-making workshop and a 175-metre roller slide that children love. Entry 800 yen. Open 9:00 to 16:00 on 31 December and 1 and 2 January, which is useful when much else is shut. Paths climb gently and may be snowy. Ten minutes on foot or a short bus ride from the station.
  - **Reputation:** real
  - **Links:**
  -   - **Official site**: Url: https://www.gero-gassho.jp/lg_en/
  -   - **japan-guide: Gero Onsen**: Url: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e5990.html
- **Onsen-ji temple** (sight, short) — The temple above the town that tells the legend of the white heron who found the spring; views over the roofs and steam
  - Hours: Free, until sunset
  - For Fernanda: high, 173 stone steps up from the town
  - Source: https://gltjp.com/en/directory/item/15959

## Okuhida Onsen

**Fit:** Would mean a change — About 4 h from Kanazawa: two nights here means two fewer somewhere else

**Prefecture:** Gifu

### Tags

- snow
- onsen

### Known for

- **Shinhotaka Ropeway**: A two-stage cable car to 2,156 m with the Northern Alps all round. · Place: Shinhotaka Ropeway
- **Snow rotenburo**: Open-air baths with snow on the rocks, in five small onsen hamlets.

### Window

- The ropeway runs 9:00–15:30 in winter; the summit deck is icy and high.
- Buses from Takayama are about 90 minutes each way.
- New Year's Day from here to Kyoto is a five-hour travel day.

**Feel:** Deep snow, steam, and the Alps close enough to touch. The most mountain-winter the trip can get.

**Getting there:** Nohi bus from Takayama, about 90 minutes; reserved seats 30 days out.

**For Fernanda:** The summit deck is cold, high and icy; ask her doctor. The baths are the easy part.

**Region:** alps

**Do line:** Snow baths and a ropeway into the Alps

### Aliases

- Shinhotaka Ropeway
- Shin-Hotaka
- Hirayu
- Japanese Alps
- Northern Alps

### Verdict

**Headline:** The deepest snow and outdoor baths we could reach.

**Text:** Small hot-spring villages high in the Alps, with open-air baths in the snow and a ropeway up to 2,156 metres. From our route it is about four hours by bus via Takayama, so it needs nights of its own; with Takayama back in the route it becomes a 90-minute day out. The top of the ropeway is high and icy, so Fernanda may prefer the baths.

### How long to stay and what is on

**Best:** 2 nights, or a day trip from Takayama

#### Date notes

- The Shinhotaka Ropeway runs 9:00–15:30 in winter and stops in strong wind; New Year hours need a call (0578-89-2252).
- Snow is near-certain in our window; the summit deck is icy and high, so Fernanda checks with her doctor.
- 29 Dec–3 Jan: Nohi buses run a holiday timetable; book seats.
- 31 Dec–2 Jan: ryokan New Year plans at a surcharge.
- 1 Jan: from here to Kyoto is about five hours, a long New Year's Day.

#### Nights

##### 1

**Why:** An afternoon arrival, an open-air bath in the snow (a private one for Fernanda), and the Shinhotaka ropeway next morning before the bus out. With the ropeway closing at 15:30, it is tight.

**Longer:** A second night gives an unhurried ropeway morning and a second bath evening.

**Shorter:** A single night is one bath evening and a quick ropeway; a day trip from Takayama does nearly as much without the move.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** Full and cold; the bus is 60–90 minutes each way.

##### 2

**Why:** One full day for the ropeway to 2,156 m and the Alps, then baths in deep snow at Hirayu or Shin-Hotaka. The most mountain-winter the trip can get.

**Longer:** A third night is a second bath day in a hamlet with nothing else.

**Shorter:** One night rushes the ropeway; a day trip from Takayama may be easier.

**Idle:** some

**Note:** Day two is the view and the baths. Beautiful, but that is all there is.

##### 3

**Why:** Two quiet days of baths and snow: one for the ropeway, one for Hirayu no Mori or the inn. For people who want to do nothing in deep snow.

**Longer:** A fourth night adds nothing.

**Shorter:** Two nights holds all the sights.

**Idle:** real

**Note:** A real retreat; restless for João and Carolina.

##### 4

**Why:** Not sensible: a scatter of hamlets with one ropeway, an hour or more from anything else.

**Longer:** Nothing to add.

**Shorter:** Three nights is already a long rest.

**Idle:** real

**Note:** Mostly idle.

##### 5

**Why:** Not sensible: there is a day's worth of sights and the rest is baths.

**Longer:** Nothing to add.

**Shorter:** Four nights is already far too many.

**Idle:** real

**Note:** Mostly idle; Takayama is a better long base.

### Places to visit (2)

- **Shinhotaka Ropeway** (nature, day_trip) — Cable car to 2,156 m with Alps views
  - Hours: 9:00-15:30 Dec-Mar
  - For Fernanda: moderate, Icy deck and altitude; ask her doctor
  - Source: https://okuhida.or.jp/en/p/sightseeing/%e6%96%b0%e7%a9%82%e9%ab%98%e3%83%ad%e3%83%bc%e3%83%97%e3%82%a6%e3%82%a7%e3%82%a4/
  - **Famous for:** Japan's only double-decker ropeway, rising to 2,156 metres for a close view of the Northern Alps.
  - **Expect:** Two cable cars climb from the valley to a summit station with an open deck facing the Hotaka peaks, under deep snow at around minus ten. Round trip about 3,800 yen; 90 minutes by bus from Takayama each way. The view depends entirely on the weather, so check the live camera before leaving. The deck is icy and the altitude noticeable; she should take it slowly and ask her doctor first. Open 9:00 to 15:30 in winter.
  - **Reputation:** classic
  - **Links:**
  -   - **Official site**: Url: https://shinhotaka-ropeway.jp/en/
  -   - **japan-guide**: Url: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e5943.html
  -   - **Okuhida tourism**: Url: https://okuhida.or.jp/en/
- **Hirayu no Mori** (onsen, short) — A big day-bath in the forest at Hirayu, with outdoor pools in the snow and private iron-kettle baths
  - Hours: 10:00-21:00; private baths 12:00-15:00, ¥3,000 for 45 minutes
  - For Fernanda: low, Use a private bath; outdoor stones are icy
  - Price: ¥700
  - Source: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e5942.html

## Kinosaki Onsen

**Fit:** Would mean a change — About 2 h 30 from Kyoto: two nights here means two fewer somewhere else

**Prefecture:** Hyogo

### Tags

- onsen
- food
- gentle

### Known for

- **Seven public baths**: You walk between them in yukata and geta along a willow-lined canal.
- **Snow crab**: November to March is crab season and the ryokan dinners are built around it.
- **Nishimuraya**: An 1860 inn with a modern sister hotel that has Western beds and a lift.

### Window

- Crab season plus New Year is Kinosaki's peak; two-night plans are common and the famous house is gone for 31 Dec.
- Only five of the seven baths are open at the moment.
- The way home is 5–6 hours to Tokyo.

**Feel:** A small town built around the ritual of bathing: lanterns, steam, wooden sandals on the bridge. Flat and sociable.

**Getting there:** About 2 h 50 from Kyoto on the Kinosaki limited express.

**For Fernanda:** Public baths run hot, so a ryokan with a private bath. Flat streets suit the parents.

**Region:** kansai

**Do line:** Hot baths, yukata strolls and snow crab

### Aliases

- Kinosaki Onsen

### Verdict

**Headline:** A canal town where you walk between hot baths.

**Text:** A small willow-lined canal town where you stroll between public baths in yukata and wooden sandals, and the ryokan dinners are built around snow crab. It is lovely, but it repeats Kanazawa's crab and Gero's baths, and the way home to Tokyo is 5–6 hours. The public baths run hot, so Fernanda may prefer a ryokan with a private bath.

**Route:** kinosaki

### How long to stay and what is on

**Best:** 2 nights

#### Date notes

- 25 Dec: the ropeway is closed; 31 Dec–3 Jan it runs short hours.
- 29 Dec–1 Jan: the public baths take turns to close: Ichi-no-yu and Mandara-yu on 31 Dec, Goshono-yu and Yanagi-yu on 1 Jan.
- 31 Dec: the last day of the small female crab; the big male crab runs to March.
- New Year: the town's busiest and dearest nights, booked months ahead.
- 29–30 Dec: Himeji Castle, the usual stop on the way back, is closed.

#### Nights

##### 1

**Why:** One evening walking the baths in yukata and one crab dinner, then a long way out: Kyoto is about 2.5–3 hours, Tokyo 5–6.

**Longer:** A second night gives a full day of baths, the ropeway to Onsen-ji, and a second evening.

**Shorter:** A single night is a long way to go for one dinner.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** Mostly train.

##### 2

**Why:** A day and two evenings: the public baths (not all seven are running), the ropeway and Onsen-ji, the flat willow-lined canal, and crab kaiseki in season. Fernanda keeps to the ryokan's private bath; the public ones run hot.

**Longer:** A third night is a third evening of baths, or Izushi's castle town and soba as a half day.

**Shorter:** One night is a long way to go for one dinner.

**Idle:** some

**Note:** Day two is pleasant and slow; like Gero, the town is the inn.

##### 3

**Why:** A slow onsen stay with Izushi's soba and clock tower or the Genbudo basalt cliffs as half days out. Good for the parents, quiet for João and Carolina.

**Longer:** A fourth night adds nothing new.

**Shorter:** Two nights is the usual stay.

**Idle:** some

**Note:** Two easy days.

##### 4

**Why:** Too long for a small town of seven baths, and the way home is 5–6 hours to Tokyo whatever you do.

**Longer:** Nothing to add.

**Shorter:** Three nights is already a slow stay.

**Idle:** real

**Note:** Two idle days.

##### 5

**Why:** Not sensible: the same bath walk and crab dinner five nights running.

**Longer:** Nothing to add.

**Shorter:** Four nights is already long.

**Idle:** real

**Note:** Mostly idle.

### Places to visit (7)

- **The seven public baths (sotoyu)** (onsen, main) — Walking between the town's public baths in yukata and geta is the point of Kinosaki; most inns give a free pass
  - Hours: Roughly 7:00–23:00 (some open mid-afternoon). Each bath has its own weekly rest day, and over New Year they take turns to close
  - For Fernanda: moderate, The water runs hot and the floors are wet; the inn's private bath is the easy choice for her
  - Source: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e3527.html
- **Ropeway and Onsen-ji temple** (viewpoint, short) — A short ropeway up Mt Daishi to the temple that founded the spa, with views over the town and the sea
  - Hours: About 9:10–17:10; closed the 2nd and 4th Thursdays (24 Dec in 2026); short hours 31 Dec–3 Jan
  - Closed: {"start":"2026-12-24","end":"2026-12-24","note":"Ropeway closed (4th Thursday)","source":"https://www.gltjp.com/en/directory/item/18558/"}
  - For Fernanda: low, The ropeway does the climb; the temple has a few steps from the middle station
  - Source: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e3529.html
- **The willow canal at night** (sight, short) — Stone bridges, willows and lanterns along the Otani river, full of families in yukata between baths
  - Hours: Always open; liveliest after dinner
  - For Fernanda: low, Flat; geta are slippery on wet stone, so shoes are fine
  - Source: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e3528.html
- **Snow-crab kaiseki at the inn** (food, meal) — November to March is crab season: crab boiled, grilled, in hot pot and in rice porridge
  - Hours: Dinner at the ryokan; booked with the room
  - For Fernanda: low, Ask for every crab dish cooked; skip the crab sashimi
  - Source: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e3525.html
- **Izushi castle town and sara soba** (sight, main) — A little castle town 40 minutes by bus: the Shinkoro clock tower, red torii up to the castle ruins, and soba on five small plates
  - Hours: Streets always open; about 50 soba shops, each with its own rest days. Busy for first visits 1–3 Jan
  - For Fernanda: low, Flat streets; the torii stairs to the ruins are steep and optional
  - Source: https://visitkinosaki.com/sample-itineraries/time-travel-in-izushi-castle-town/
- **Genbudo Park** (nature, short) — Cliffs of six-sided basalt columns formed by lava, 10 minutes from town, with a small rock and mineral museum
  - Hours: 9:00–17:00; some listings say closed 29 Dec–3 Jan, so check
  - Closed: {"start":"2026-12-29","end":"2027-01-03","note":"May be closed for New Year (sources differ)","source":"https://www.hyogo-tourism.jp/world/spot/detail_1060.html"}
  - For Fernanda: low, Short flat paths from the car park
  - Source: https://www.hyogo-tourism.jp/world/spot/detail_1060.html
- **Kinosaki Marine World** (museum, main) — An aquarium on the Sea of Japan coast with dolphin and sea-lion shows and fishing for your own lunch
  - Hours: About 9:30–16:30; irregular closing days, so check the calendar
  - For Fernanda: low, Mostly indoors and flat; benches at the shows
  - Price: About ¥2,800 adults
  - Source: https://visitkinosaki.com/things-to-do/marine-world/

## Ginzan Onsen

**Fit:** Would mean a change — About 3 h 30 from Tokyo: two nights here means two fewer somewhere else

**Prefecture:** Yamagata

### Tags

- snow
- onsen

### Known for

- **The street**: Wooden ryokan along a river under gas lamps and heavy snow: Japan's most photographed winter scene.

### Window

- Day visits are capped and need a reserved shuttle; the few inns book a year ahead.
- Three hours twenty from Tokyo plus a bus; five and a half from Kyoto.

**Feel:** Perfect, tiny, and full. Better seen in a photograph than squeezed into this trip.

**Getting there:** Yamagata shinkansen to Oishida, then a 35-minute bus with a reservation.

**For Fernanda:** Icy lanes and stairs in every inn.

**Region:** north

**Do line:** A snowy street of wooden inns

### Aliases

- Ginzan Onsen
- Yamagata

### Verdict

**Headline:** Japan's most photographed winter street, tiny and full.

**Text:** Wooden ryokan line a river under gas lamps and heavy snow, perhaps the best-known winter scene in Japan. It is tiny: day visits are capped, the few inns book a year ahead, and it is five and a half hours from Kyoto. The lanes are icy and every inn has stairs.

### How long to stay and what is on

**Best:** 1–2 nights between 20 and 27 Dec, only if rooms exist

#### Date notes

- From 20 Dec: winter day-visit rules. Daytime by park-and-ride shuttle; 17:00–20:00 capped at 100 people an hour with a booked ticket; after 20:00 inn guests only.
- New Year: sold out a year ahead; 20–27 Dec is the only realistic window.
- Late December: Zao's snow monsters are only half grown; the ropeway's night light-up starts around 27 Dec.
- 29 Dec–3 Jan: Yamagata shinkansen seats are full, worst on 30 Dec and 3 Jan.

#### Nights

##### 1

**Why:** The one way to see the gas-lit wooden inns under snow without the caps on day visitors. Rooms hold four at most, so seven means two or three rooms, and the lanes and inn stairs are icy.

**Longer:** A second night buys Yamadera for the climbers, or the Zao ropeway's night light-up from Yamagata.

**Shorter:** A single night is the photograph come to life; without it, a day visit means the shuttle and a booked evening slot.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** A dreamlike evening after a long journey: over three hours from Tokyo plus a bus.

##### 2

**Why:** One evening and one full day: the river lanes by daylight, a bath, and Yamadera (1,015 icy steps, for the climbers) or Zao's ropeway in the snow.

**Longer:** A third night is another evening in a street of a dozen inns.

**Shorter:** One night is the photograph.

**Idle:** some

**Note:** Lovely and small; the second day is quiet.

##### 3

**Why:** Too long for one short street of inns; the extra day is spent getting to Yamagata and back.

**Longer:** Nothing to add.

**Shorter:** Two nights holds the village and one day out.

**Idle:** real

**Note:** A quiet, icy day with little to do.

##### 4

**Why:** Not sensible: no inn here sells four winter nights to a group of seven, and there is nothing to fill them.

**Longer:** Nothing to add.

**Shorter:** Three nights is already too many.

**Idle:** real

**Note:** Mostly idle.

##### 5

**Why:** Not sensible: base in Yamagata or Sendai for a longer Tohoku stay instead.

**Longer:** Nothing to add.

**Shorter:** Four nights is already too many.

**Idle:** real

**Note:** Mostly idle.

## Shuzenji (Izu)

**Fit:** Would mean a change — About 2 h 15 from Tokyo: two nights here means two fewer somewhere else

**Prefecture:** Shizuoka (Izu)

### Tags

- onsen
- gentle

### Known for

- **A bamboo-grove onsen town**: Quiet, mild, a red bridge over a river, a temple at the top of the street.
- **Atami**: The seaside spa an hour from Tokyo, with winter fireworks on 6 and 25 Dec.

### Window

- Mild and dry; no snow, no museums.
- About two hours from Tokyo on the Odoriko; three from Kyoto via Mishima.

**Feel:** The gentlest onsen option within reach: warm, flat, uncrowded.

**Getting there:** Odoriko limited express from Tokyo, then the Izu-Hakone railway.

**For Fernanda:** Gentle and flat; the calm Hakone alternative without the ropeway or the gas.

**Region:** tokyo-fuji

**Do line:** A quiet bamboo-grove onsen town

### Aliases

- Izu
- Shuzenji Onsen

### Verdict

**Headline:** The gentlest hot-spring town within reach, warm and quiet.

**Text:** A red bridge over a river, a bamboo grove and a temple at the top of the street, in a mild, uncrowded onsen town. It could take the Fuji nights as a calmer stop, but it has no snow, no museums and none of the lake's view of Fuji.

### How long to stay and what is on

**Best:** 2 nights

#### Date notes

- 25 Dec, 20:20–20:40: Atami's winter fireworks over the bay.
- Thursdays: the MOA Museum in Atami is closed.
- 31 Dec–2 Jan: ryokan New Year plans at a surcharge; Shuzen-ji's own hatsumode is small and calm.
- Kawazu's early cherries are in February and Atami's plum garden opens mid-January, both after us.

#### Nights

##### 1

**Why:** An evening in a quiet onsen town with a private bath, and the bamboo path in the morning. Mild and dry, about two hours from Tokyo.

**Longer:** A second night gives an unhurried day: the temple, the bamboo grove, a soba lunch.

**Shorter:** A single night is a bath and a dinner.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** Easy, short and calm.

##### 2

**Why:** One gentle day: Shuzen-ji temple, the bamboo path and the riverside footbath, a ryokan dinner, and nothing steep. The calmest onsen town in reach, with no snow and no museums.

**Longer:** A third night buys Atami's MOA Museum and warm seafront, or Fuji from the Mishima Skywalk.

**Shorter:** One night is a bath and a dinner.

**Idle:** some

**Note:** Day two is a short walk and the bath; restful by design.

##### 3

**Why:** Add Atami for the MOA Museum and the seafront, with fireworks over the bay if it is 25 Dec, or the Joren waterfall in the Izu hills.

**Longer:** A fourth night is another bath day.

**Shorter:** Two nights is Shuzenji alone.

**Idle:** some

**Note:** Gentle; João and Carolina may find it slow.

##### 4

**Why:** Too long for a small town of a few lanes; it only works as a rest stop with Atami and Mishima as half days.

**Longer:** Nothing to add.

**Shorter:** Three nights is already generous.

**Idle:** real

**Note:** Two quiet days.

##### 5

**Why:** Not sensible: Izu in winter is pleasant rather than wow, and a dead end for onward travel.

**Longer:** Nothing to add.

**Shorter:** Four nights is already long.

**Idle:** real

**Note:** Mostly idle.

### Places to visit (9)

- **Shuzen-ji temple** (sight, short) — The Zen temple founded by Kobo Daishi that gave the town its name, at the top of the main street
  - Hours: Open daily; visitors may strike the bell on New Year's Eve from about 23:30 (tickets in past years)
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Source: https://tabi-mag.jp/syuzenji/
- **Chikurin-no-komichi bamboo path** (nature, short) — A short, flat path through a bamboo grove beside the Katsura river, with a round bamboo bench in the middle
  - Hours: Always open
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Source: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e6311.html
- **Tokko-no-yu foot bath** (onsen, short) — The town's oldest hot spring, in the riverbed below the temple; today a foot bath only
  - Hours: Always open; free
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Source: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e6311.html
- **Izu Panorama Park ropeway** (nature, main) — A seven-minute ropeway up Mt Katsuragi to a flat terrace of sofas facing Fuji across Suruga Bay
  - Hours: Winter 9:00–17:00 (last up 16:30); open over New Year, from 5:00 on 1 Jan for the first sunrise
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Price: About ¥3,500 return
  - Source: https://banzokubiology.com/?p=11425
- **Strawberry picking at Ema** (food, short) — Thirty minutes of eat-all-you-can strawberries in warm greenhouses, ten minutes from Izu-Nagaoka
  - Hours: From about mid-December; 10:00–15:00 (9:00 at weekends); check New Year days
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Price: About ¥2,500
  - Source: https://www.walkerplus.com/event/ar0622e328690/
- **Mishima Skywalk** (nature, short) — Japan's longest pedestrian suspension bridge, 400 m across a valley, with Fuji at one end
  - Hours: 9:00–17:00; open over New Year
  - For Fernanda: moderate, Flat but windy and a little bouncy
  - Price: About ¥1,100
  - Source: https://banzokubiology.com/?p=13899
- **Eel in Mishima (Sakuraya)** (food, meal) — Mishima's spring water is said to clean the eel's flavour; Sakuraya has grilled it since the Meiji era
  - Hours: Usually closed Wednesdays and one Tuesday a month; open over New Year by one listing
  - Closed weekdays: Wed
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Source: https://www.mapple.net/original/411957/
- **Nirayama reverberatory furnace** (sight, short) — Twin brick chimneys of an 1850s iron furnace, a World Heritage site, 15 minutes from Izu-Nagaoka
  - Hours: Winter 9:00–16:30; closed the 3rd Wednesday of the month, 31 Dec and 1 Jan
  - Closed: {"start":"2026-12-31","end":"2027-01-01","note":"Closed","source":"https://www.at-s.com/facilities/article/view/place/138539.html"}
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Source: https://www.at-s.com/facilities/article/view/place/138539.html
- **Shuzenji Rainbow Village (Niji-no-sato)** (garden, main) — Gardens in English, Canadian and Japanese styles, joined by a little steam railway, with evening lights this winter
  - Hours: 10:00–16:00 in winter; closed Tuesdays and some days in mid-December and mid-January; open over New Year
  - Closed weekdays: Tue
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Source: https://www.at-s.com/spot/article/138190

## Kusatsu Onsen

**Fit:** Would mean a change — About 3 h from Yudanaka: two nights here means two fewer somewhere else

**Prefecture:** Gunma

### Tags

- snow
- onsen

### Known for

- **The yubatake**: A steaming hot-water field in the middle of town, lit at night.
- **Yumomi**: The water-cooling show with paddles and song.

### Window

- Open through New Year; a bus from Naganohara is the last leg.
- The water is 50 °C and acidic at source.

**Feel:** Steam over snow and the smell of sulphur: the most dramatic onsen town in reach of Tokyo.

**Getting there:** Limited express to Naganohara, then a 25-minute bus; about 3.5 hours from Tokyo and far from Kyoto.

**For Fernanda:** Too hot and acidic for her; a foot bath at most.

**Region:** alps

**Do line:** A steaming hot-water field in the snow

### Aliases

- Kusatsu Onsen
- Yubatake
- Gunma

### Verdict

**Headline:** The most dramatic onsen town within reach of Tokyo.

**Text:** Steam rises over snow from a hot-water field in the middle of town, lit at night, and bath-keepers cool the water with paddles and song. The water is 50 °C and acidic at the source, so Fernanda may prefer a footbath at most, and it is far from Kyoto.

### How long to stay and what is on

**Best:** 2 nights (for the bathers; little for Fernanda)

#### Date notes

- Through our window: the Yubatake is lit every evening.
- The yumomi water-stirring shows run six times a day, New Year included.
- 29 Dec–3 Jan: the town fills with Tokyo families and ryokan prices double.
- The bus to Karuizawa runs with snow chains and can stop in bad weather.

#### Nights

##### 1

**Why:** The steaming Yubatake under lights at dusk and a yumomi show, then out. It is about 3.5 hours from Tokyo by train and bus, and a long way from Kyoto.

**Longer:** A second night gives Sainokawara's huge open-air bath and an unhurried evening.

**Shorter:** A single night is the Yubatake and a dinner.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** A lot of travel for one evening.

##### 2

**Why:** The Yubatake by day and lit at night, the seated yumomi shows, the Sainokawara park bath in the snow, and a ryokan with a private bath. The water is about 50 °C and acidic at source, too hot for Fernanda beyond a footbath.

**Longer:** A third night buys a ski morning for João and Carolina or Shima Onsen's 1691 bathhouse.

**Shorter:** One night is the Yubatake and a dinner.

**Idle:** some

**Note:** A great town for bathers; Fernanda will have the quietest time.

##### 3

**Why:** Add a ski morning at the small resort above town, Shima Onsen's old bathhouse, or the bus to Karuizawa, about 80 minutes when the road is clear.

**Longer:** A fourth night adds nothing.

**Shorter:** Two nights covers the town.

**Idle:** some

**Note:** One quiet day for the non-skiers.

##### 4

**Why:** Too long: one hot-water field and a ring of public baths, with no rail line out.

**Longer:** Nothing to add.

**Shorter:** Three nights is already generous.

**Idle:** real

**Note:** Two idle days, longest for Fernanda.

##### 5

**Why:** Not sensible for this group: the baths are the point, and she can barely use them.

**Longer:** Nothing to add.

**Shorter:** Four nights is already too many.

**Idle:** real

**Note:** Mostly idle.

## Aomori and Hirosaki

**Fit:** Would mean a change — About 4 h from Tokyo: two nights here means two fewer somewhere else

**Prefecture:** Aomori

### Tags

- snow
- museums
- food

### Known for

- **Aomori Museum of Art**: Nara Yoshitomo's eight-metre white dog and Chagall's stage backdrops, next to the Sannai-Maruyama Jomon site.
- **Hirosaki Castle park**: Moats, gates and an original keep under deep snow, 40 minutes away, with apple-pie cafés in town.
- **Nebuta Museum Wa Rasse**: The giant lantern floats of the summer festival, indoors by the station.
- **The Hakkoda ropeway**: Ten minutes up into frost-covered trees, an hour from town; it stops in strong wind.
- **Scallops and nokke-don**: Build-your-own rice bowls at the fish market, and grilled scallops and kaiyaki miso for Fernanda.

### Window

- Snow is near-certain: Aomori is one of the snowiest cities in the world.
- The Nebuta museum closes 31 Dec–1 Jan; the art museum has reopened on 2 Jan in past years.
- Hakkoda's ropeway runs about 9:00–16:00 in winter; the frost trees fill out from January.
- Three hours from Tokyo by Hayabusa, full on 3 Jan; Hirosaki's snow-lantern festival is in February, after us.

**Feel:** A snowbound port at the top of Honshu, with a castle town and apple orchards behind it and a mountain range of frozen trees an hour away. Big skies, deep snow and very few foreign visitors.

**Getting there:** Hayabusa from Tokyo to Shin-Aomori, about 3 h 15, then a short taxi or train into town. Hirosaki is about 40 minutes by local train.

**For Fernanda:** Deep snow and icy pavements: cleats, taxis and short walks, with the museums for the coldest days. Aomori Prefectural Central Hospital, with a perinatal centre, is in the city.

**Region:** north

**Do line:** Snowy castle town, art and frozen trees

### Aliases

- Aomori
- Hirosaki
- Hakkoda
- Oirase
- Nebuta

### Verdict

**Headline:** The snowy top of Honshu, with very few visitors.

**Text:** A snowbound port with a castle town and apple orchards behind it: Hirosaki's original keep under deep snow, Nara Yoshitomo's giant white dog at the art museum, and a ropeway up into frost-covered trees. It is three hours north of Tokyo, the other way from Kyoto, so it belongs to a different route.

**Route:** tohoku-snow-samurai

### How long to stay and what is on

**Best:** 3 nights (4 over New Year)

#### Date notes

- 31 Dec–1 Jan: the Nebuta museum is closed, and most of the city shuts on 1 Jan.
- Around New Year: the Aomori Museum of Art and the Sannai-Maruyama site close; both have reopened on 2 Jan in past years, but check.
- Winter: Hirosaki Castle's keep is closed; the park is free and open.
- Every day the weather allows: the Hakkoda ropeway, about 9:00–16:00, every 15–20 minutes, stopped by winds of 25 m/s or more.
- 3 Jan: the Tohoku shinkansen south is at its fullest; book seats the day they go on sale.

#### Nights

##### 1

**Why:** One evening and a morning at the Nebuta museum before the train back. Three hours from Tokyo is a long way for that.

**Longer:** A second night gives the art museum or Hirosaki.

**Shorter:** A single night barely repays the trip.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** Mostly train.

##### 2

**Why:** One full day: the art museum and Sannai-Maruyama, or Hirosaki's castle park and apple-pie cafés.

**Longer:** A third night gives both, or Hakkoda.

**Shorter:** One night is the station and the museum.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** Full.

##### 3

**Why:** Two full days: Hirosaki on one; the art museum, the Jomon site and the Nebuta museum on the other.

**Longer:** A fourth night adds Hakkoda's ropeway, weather permitting, or an easy day.

**Shorter:** Two nights loses Hirosaki or the museums.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** Good days, all short hops.

##### 4

**Why:** The New Year shape: arrive on the quiet 1 Jan with the city shut, an easy 2 Jan at the museums, Hirosaki on the 3rd, and Hakkoda on the 4th if the wind allows, with the Nebuta museum as the bad-weather plan.

**Longer:** A fifth night adds an afternoon at Asamushi Onsen, 20 minutes away, or a slow day.

**Shorter:** Three nights means riding south on a peak day, or losing Hakkoda.

**Idle:** some

**Note:** 1 Jan is a quiet arrival with the city closed.

##### 5

**Why:** Aomori plus Asamushi Onsen or a second go at Hakkoda; a long time in deep cold.

**Longer:** Nothing to add.

**Shorter:** Four nights is plenty.

**Idle:** real

**Note:** One idle day, more in a blizzard.

### Places to visit (11)

- **Aomori Museum of Art** (museum, main) — A white museum dug into the ground, with Nara Yoshitomo's 8.5 m Aomori-ken dog and Chagall's huge stage backdrops

  - Hours: 9:00–18:00 in winter; closed 31 Dec–1 Jan
  - Closed: {"start":"2026-12-31","end":"2027-01-01","note":"Closed","source":"https://iko-yo.net/facilities/25025"}

  - Hours: Park open all hours and free in winter; the keep is closed in winter
  - For Fernanda: moderate, Snowy paths; cleats
  - Source: https://banzokubiology.com/?p=12579
- **Hirosaki's apple-pie cafés** (food, meal) — The apple capital has dozens of cafés baking apple pie, and old Western houses to have it in
  - Hours: Most close 1 Jan; some reopen on 2 Jan

  - For Fernanda: moderate, Very cold at the top; the lift and the warm station make it possible
  - Price: ¥2,200 return
  - Source: https://hakkoda-ropeway.jp/en/faq/
- **Sukayu Onsen** (onsen, short) — A great wooden bathhouse in the snow; the mixed Sennin-buro has a women-only hour, 8:00–9:00, and smaller separate baths
  - Hours: Day baths 7:00–18:00 (Tamanoyu 9:00–17:00); about ¥1,000
  - For Fernanda: moderate, Very hot, acidic water; Tamanoyu is the gentler choice
  - Price: About ¥1,000
  - Source: https://www.mapple.net/article/34185/
- **Nokke-don at the Aomori fish market** (food, meal) — Buy a bowl of rice and tickets, then pick toppings stall by stall; grilled scallops and cooked things for Fernanda
  - Hours: Mornings; closed Tuesdays; New Year days change, so check
  - Closed weekdays: Tue
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Source: https://www.nap-camp.com/mag/111131
- **A-Factory** (market, short) — A glass market hall by the station with a cider workshop, apple sweets and a food court
  - Hours: About 10:00–19:00; hours vary by shop
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Source: https://www.mapple.net/article/33670/
- **Asamushi Onsen and aquarium** (onsen, short) — A seaside onsen town 20 minutes from Aomori by train, with a foot bath by the station and an aquarium of local sea life
  - Hours: Aquarium 9:00–17:00, open all year; New Year days unconfirmed
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Source: https://sp.jorudan.co.jp/zoo_aqua/spot_0310.html
- **Tsugaru Railway stove train** (sight, day_trip) — An old train heated by coal-burning stoves, on which you can grill dried squid, across the snowy Tsugaru plain from Goshogawara
  - Hours: December to March; 2026–27 timetable to be published
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Booking: A stove-train ticket on top of the fare
  - Source: https://tabi-mag.jp/tsugaru-stovetrain/

## Sendai and Matsushima

**Fit:** Would mean a change — About 2 h 15 from Tokyo: two nights here means two fewer somewhere else

**Prefecture:** Miyagi

### Tags

- food
- classics
- gentle

### Known for

- **Matsushima**: Pine-covered islands in a bay, one of Japan's three great views; a seated 50-minute cruise runs every day, New Year included.
- **Gyutan**: Grilled beef tongue with barley rice and oxtail soup: Sendai's own dinner.
- **The Pageant of Starlight**: The zelkova avenue of Jozenji-dori lit end to end in December.
- **Zuihoden**: Date Masamune's gilded, painted mausoleum among tall cedars, up a flight of stone steps.
- **Sendai's New Year sale**: On 2 Jan the shops open with gifts for customers, a custom kept since the Edo period.

### Window

- The Pageant of Starlight's 2026 dates are not announced; last year it ran 5–28 Dec, and one guide expects 6–25 Dec.
- The Matsushima cruise and Zuigan-ji run every day; the city's museums close roughly 28 Dec–4 Jan, and the oyster huts about 31 Dec–3 Jan.
- Cold and mostly dry, with the odd snowfall; Zao's frozen trees are only half formed before mid-January.

**Feel:** Tohoku's big, green, easy city of tree-lined avenues and covered arcades, with the pine islands of Matsushima 40 minutes away. Comfortable and well fed rather than dramatic.

**Getting there:** Hayabusa from Tokyo, about 1 h 30; Matsushima is 40 minutes by the Senseki line.

**For Fernanda:** Flat avenues, covered arcades and station hotels with lifts; the Matsushima cruise is seated. Oysters grilled rather than raw. Tohoku University Hospital, a comprehensive perinatal centre, is about 15 minutes by taxi.

**Region:** north

**Do line:** Pine-island bay, beef tongue and city lights

### Aliases

- Sendai
- Matsushima
- Zao
- Yamadera
- Miyagi

### Verdict

**Headline:** Tohoku's easy city, with Matsushima's pine islands nearby.

**Text:** Sendai is a big, green city of tree-lined avenues and covered arcades, known for grilled beef tongue, with the pine-covered islands of Matsushima 40 minutes away and a seated cruise every day. It is comfortable rather than dramatic, and it lies north of Tokyo, away from Kyoto, so it fits only a route of its own.

**Route:** tohoku-snow-samurai

### How long to stay and what is on

**Best:** 3 nights

#### Date notes

- December, to about 25–28 Dec: the Pageant of Starlight on Jozenji-dori; the 2026 dates are still to come.
- From about 27 Dec, at weekends and over New Year: the Zao ropeway's frozen-tree light-up has run in past seasons, about 1 h 30 away, with the trees still young.
- Every day, New Year included: the Matsushima cruise and Zuigan-ji; 1 Jan adds a sunrise cruise.
- About 28 Dec–4 Jan: the city's museums are closed; the oyster huts close about 31 Dec–3 Jan.
- 2 Jan: Sendai's New Year sale, with gifts for customers.

#### Nights

##### 1

**Why:** An evening of gyutan, and the lights if they are on. Sendai is 90 minutes from Tokyo, so one night works as a stop on the way north.

**Longer:** A second night gives Matsushima.

**Shorter:** A single night is dinner and bed.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** Short.

##### 2

**Why:** One full day: Matsushima's cruise and Zuigan-ji in the morning, back for the lights and beef tongue at night.

**Longer:** A third night adds Zuihoden and the city, or Yamadera for the keen climbers.

**Shorter:** One night is a dinner.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** Full and easy.

##### 3

**Why:** Two full days: Matsushima on one; Zuihoden, the arcades and a café morning on the other, with Yamadera's thousand steps for João and Carolina only. It also lets the long flight wear off before the snow.

**Longer:** A fourth night adds Akiu Onsen or an evening at the Zao ropeway.

**Shorter:** Two nights loses the city day.

**Idle:** some

**Note:** The second day is flexible.

##### 4

**Why:** Sendai plus a day in the mountains: Zao's frozen trees by ropeway at night, or Ginzan Onsen's snowy street by day, about 2 h 30 each way.

**Longer:** A fifth night adds Akiu Onsen.

**Shorter:** Three nights is the usual stay.

**Idle:** some

**Note:** One easy day.

##### 5

**Why:** Too long for a city that is pleasant rather than packed with sights; over New Year it is quiet.

**Longer:** Nothing to add.

**Shorter:** Four nights is already generous.

**Idle:** real

**Note:** One or two quiet days.

### Places to visit (10)

- **Matsushima bay cruise** (nature, main) — A seated 50-minute cruise among the pine-covered islands of one of Japan's three great views, 40 minutes from Sendai by the Senseki line
  - Hours: Hourly about 10:00–15:00 in winter; runs every day except in storms; a sunrise cruise has run on 1 Jan
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Price: About ¥1,500
  - Source: https://rurubu.jp/andmore/spot/80003790
- **Zuigan-ji** (sight, short) — Date Masamune's Zen temple by the bay, with gold-screened rooms and cave shrines along the cedar approach
  - Hours: 8:00 or 8:30 to about 15:30 in winter; from 8:30 on 1 Jan; the New Year's Eve bell from 21:00
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Price: About ¥1,000
  - Source: https://rurubu.jp/andmore/spot/80003789
- **Grilled oysters in Matsushima** (food, meal) — Winter is oyster season: all-you-can-eat oyster huts and grilled oysters on the waterfront
  - Hours: The oyster huts close about 31 Dec–3 Jan
  - Closed: {"start":"2026-12-31","end":"2027-01-03","note":"Oyster huts closed (approximate)","source":"https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e5100.html"}
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Source: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e5100.html
- **Zuihoden** (sight, short) — Date Masamune's gilded, painted mausoleum among tall cedars, up a flight of stone steps
  - Hours: 9:00–16:20 in December and January; closed 31 Dec; on 1 Jan only the main hall opens, free
  - Closed: {"start":"2026-12-31","end":"2026-12-31","note":"Closed","source":"https://www.zuihoden.com/cms/wp-content/uploads/9cc50af006e01c3282c11cc789adfc34.pdf"}
  - For Fernanda: moderate, Stone steps up to the mausoleum
  - Price: ¥570
  - Source: https://www.zuihoden.com/cms/wp-content/uploads/9cc50af006e01c3282c11cc789adfc34.pdf
- **Yamadera (Risshaku-ji)** (sight, main) — A mountain temple of halls clinging to cliffs, up 1,015 stone steps, an hour from Sendai on the Senzan line
  - Hours: Open in winter, about 8:30–15:00; the steps ice over, so cleats
  - For Fernanda: high, 1,015 icy steps; probably one for the others
  - Source: https://www.visityamagata.jp/en/spot-yamagata-yamadera-en/
- **The Pageant of Starlight** (event, short) — The zelkova avenue of Jozenji-dori lit end to end with LEDs in December
  - Hours: Evenings in December; 2026 dates not yet announced (2025 ran 5–28 Dec; one guide expects 6–25 Dec)
  - Closed: {"start":"2026-12-26","end":"2027-01-09","note":"Probably over by 26 Dec; the end date is unconfirmed","source":"https://en.japantravel.com/miyagi/sendai-pageant-of-starlight/9040"}
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Source: https://en.japantravel.com/miyagi/sendai-pageant-of-starlight/9040
- **Gyutan (grilled beef tongue)** (food, meal) — Sendai's own dinner: charcoal-grilled beef tongue with barley rice and oxtail soup; a whole 'beef tongue street' in the station
  - Hours: Station restaurants open late and most of New Year
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Source: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e5100.html
- **Sendai Umino-Mori Aquarium** (sight, short) — A big modern aquarium by the port with a Sanriku sea tank, sea lions and penguins; New Year shows in past years
  - Hours: 9:00–17:30; open every day including New Year
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Source: https://report.iko-yo.net/articles/29684
- **Sendai's New Year sale (Hatsuuri)** (event, short) — On 2 Jan the arcades open early and give customers gifts with what they buy, a custom from the Date domain more than 200 years old
  - Hours: 2 Jan from early morning; busiest first thing
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Source: https://bestcalendar.jp/dates/kinenbi/767
- **Zao snow monsters by ropeway** (nature, day_trip) — Trees frozen into white shapes at the top of the Zao ropeway, lit up on set evenings; about 1 h 30 from Sendai by bus
  - Hours: Light-up on set dates from about 27 Dec in past seasons, 17:00–21:00; 2026–27 dates not yet published. The trees are only half-formed before mid-January
  - For Fernanda: high, About –10 °C at the top, crowded cars at night
  - Price: About ¥4,400 return
  - Source: https://tabi-mag.jp/zao-lightup/

## Koyasan

**Fit:** Would mean a change — About 2 h 30 from Kyoto: two nights here means two fewer somewhere else

**Prefecture:** Wakayama

### Tags

- snow

### Known for

- **A night in a temple**: Shukubo lodging, vegetarian dinner, the 6 am fire ritual.
- **Okunoin**: A two-kilometre walk through a cedar-forest cemetery to Kobo Daishi's mausoleum.

### Window

- Group rooms by association request at least 14 working days ahead; Fukuchi-in is the one with an onsen.
- Cold corridors, floor futons, shared facilities, icy stone paths.

**Feel:** Silent, cold and moving; one night is right, two is a lot.

**Getting there:** Nankai railway from Osaka, then a cable car; about 2.5 hours from Kyoto.

**For Fernanda:** Floor beds and icy paths: not this time.

**Region:** kansai

**Do line:** A night in a temple among cedars

### Aliases

- Mount Koya
- Koya-san
- Okunoin

### Verdict

**Headline:** A silent temple town in the mountains.

**Text:** You sleep in a temple, eat a vegetarian dinner, join the dawn fire ritual and walk two kilometres through a cedar-forest cemetery. In winter the corridors are cold, the beds are futons on the floor and the stone paths are icy, so Fernanda may prefer to save it for a warmer season.

## Kumano Kodo and Nachi

**Fit:** Would mean a change — About 4 h 30 from Kyoto: two nights here means two fewer somewhere else

**Prefecture:** Wakayama

### Known for

- **The Kumano Kodo**: Pilgrim trails to three grand shrines and the Nachi waterfall.

### Window

- Long buses and stone stairways; a slow region in any season.

**Feel:** Forest, moss, stone steps, and the tallest waterfall in Japan at the end.

**Getting there:** Limited express down the coast from Osaka, then buses.

**For Fernanda:** Stairs and buses: not this time.

**Region:** kansai

**Do line:** Pilgrim trails, grand shrines and a waterfall

### Aliases

- Kumano Kodo
- Nachi Falls
- Nachi

### Verdict

**Headline:** Mossy pilgrim trails to Japan's tallest waterfall.

**Text:** Pilgrim trails of forest, moss and stone steps lead to three grand shrines and the Nachi waterfall, the tallest in Japan. It is a slow region of long bus rides and stairways, best walked in spring or autumn.

## Miyajima

**Fit:** Would mean a change — About 2 h 45 from Kyoto: two nights here means two fewer somewhere else

**Prefecture:** Hiroshima

### Tags

- classics
- food
- gentle

### Known for

- **The floating torii**: Itsukushima's shrine stands on stilts over the sea. Walk out to the great gate at low tide and watch it float at high tide.
- **Chinka-sai**: On New Year's Eve about a thousand torches are lit from the shrine's sacred fire on Mikasa-hama beach.
- **The island after dark**: Once the day visitors take the ferry back, the lanterns, the deer and the quiet waterfront are yours.
- **Grilled oysters**: Winter is oyster season. They come grilled or fried and cooked through, with momiji-manju cakes along Omotesando.

### Window

- Mild and calm; snow is rare.
- 31 Dec is the island's big night: Chinka-sai at 18:00, then the shrine opens at midnight, with queues of up to two hours for the first visit.
- The inns that serve dinner fill first for 30–31 Dec. Book them before anything else.

**Feel:** A sacred island of deer, red lacquer and pines, ten minutes across the water from Hiroshima. Busy by day and hushed at night.

**Getting there:** About 45 minutes from Hiroshima: JR to Miyajimaguchi, then a 10-minute ferry. About 2 h 30 from Kyoto, four hours from Tokyo.

**For Fernanda:** Flat around the shrine and the town. The top of Mt Misen is a steep trail, so the view from the ropeway is plenty. The ferry is short and sheltered. JA Hiroshima General Hospital in Hatsukaichi, which has obstetrics, is 15–20 minutes from the mainland pier.

**Region:** west

**Do line:** Floating torii, deer and New Year torches

### Aliases

- Itsukushima
- Floating torii

### Verdict

**Headline:** A sacred island of deer, red lacquer and pines.

**Text:** Itsukushima's shrine stands on stilts over the sea, the great gate floats at high tide, and on New Year's Eve about a thousand torches are lit on the beach. It is about two and a half hours past Kyoto, so it belongs to a route that ends the year in the west.

**Route:** golden-route-winter

### How long to stay and what is on

**Best:** 2 nights (1 if Hiroshima is the base)

#### Date notes

- 30–31 Dec: Hiroshima's Peace Memorial Museum is closed. The island stays calm until the evening of the 31st.
- 31 Dec, 18:00–18:30: Chinka-sai, the torch festival on Mikasa-hama beach. It is crowded near the fire, so watch from the back.
- 31 Dec, from about 21:00: people queue at Daisho-in, where the first 108 in line ring the temple bell.
- 1 Jan: the shrine opens at midnight, with queues of up to two hours for the first visit. The ropeway runs from 05:00 for sunrise on Mt Misen, at about 07:15.
- 1–3 Jan: day crowds come over from Hiroshima, and the island empties after dark.
- Every day: the torii floats at high tide, and you can walk out to it at low tide. Check the tide table.
- Visitors pay a ¥100 island tax with the ferry fare.

#### Nights

##### 1

**Why:** An afternoon at the shrine, an evening on the waterfront after the day visitors leave, and a quiet morning before they come back. Enough if Hiroshima is the base for the Peace Park.

**Longer:** A second night gives a slow day: Momijidani, Daisho-in and the torii at both tides.

**Shorter:** No night means a day trip from Hiroshima, about 45 minutes each way, and you miss the quiet evening.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** Short and lovely; the evening is the point.

##### 2

**Why:** A calm day on the island and two evenings. On our dates it holds New Year's Eve: a walk, a bath, Chinka-sai at 18:00 and dinner at the inn. Fernanda can see the shrine in daylight on the 30th or 31st and skip the midnight queue; João, Carolina and Tatiana can join the bell queue at Daisho-in.

**Longer:** A third night means staying into 1–2 Jan, when the crowds arrive and the trains are full.

**Shorter:** One night, arriving on 31 Dec, puts the move on a busy morning.

**Idle:** some

**Note:** 31 Dec is slow on purpose.

##### 3

**Why:** Time for Mt Misen by ropeway for Carlos, Nadir and the fit ones, Iwakuni's wooden bridge by train, and an easy afternoon. Good before 29 Dec; over New Year the third day is mostly crowds.

**Longer:** A fourth night adds nothing the island has not already shown you.

**Shorter:** Two nights covers the shrine, the town and a quiet evening.

**Idle:** some

**Note:** One easy day.

##### 4

**Why:** Too long for a small island, unless it is a quiet base for Hiroshima and Iwakuni as day trips.

**Longer:** Nothing to add.

**Shorter:** Three nights is already a slow stay.

**Idle:** real

**Note:** Two slow days.

##### 5

**Why:** Not sensible: the same shrine and the same lane five days running, with Hiroshima better as a base.

**Longer:** Nothing to add.

**Shorter:** Four nights is already long.

**Idle:** real

**Note:** Mostly idle.

### Places to visit (6)

- **Itsukushima Shrine and the floating torii** (nature, short) — The shrine on stilts over the sea; walk out to the torii at low tide, see it float at high tide
  - Hours: 31 Dec 06:30–17:00; 1 Jan from midnight to 18:30; 2–3 Jan 06:30–18:30. Queues up to 2 h on 1 Jan
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Source: https://gethiroshima.com/features/all-you-need-to-know-about-miyajima-at-new-year/
- **Chinka-sai fire festival** (event, main) — About 1,000 torches lit from the shrine's sacred fire on the beach on New Year's Eve
  - Hours: 31 Dec, 18:00–18:30 at Mikasa-hama; heated pushing near the fire, so watch from the back
  - For Fernanda: moderate
  - Source: https://miyajima.or.jp/english/event/event_chinka.html
- **Daisho-in temple** (event, main) — Hillside temple with hundreds of small statues; the public can ring its bell on New Year's Eve
  - Hours: Open from midnight on 1 Jan; the first 108 people in line (from about 21:00 on 31 Dec) ring the bell
  - For Fernanda: moderate
  - Source: https://gethiroshima.com/features/all-you-need-to-know-about-miyajima-at-new-year/
- **Mt Misen by ropeway** (viewpoint, main) — Views over the Inland Sea islands; a special sunrise run on New Year's Day
  - Hours: Ropeway from 05:00 on 1 Jan, from 09:00 on 2 Jan; the last stretch to the summit is a steep, unlit trail
  - For Fernanda: high
  - Source: https://gethiroshima.com/features/all-you-need-to-know-about-miyajima-at-new-year/
- **Omotesando street and grilled oysters** (food, short) — Winter is oyster season; grilled and fried oysters are cooked through, momiji-manju cakes
  - Hours: Shops and food stalls open over New Year
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Source: https://gethiroshima.com/features/all-you-need-to-know-about-miyajima-at-new-year/
- **Momijidani park** (garden, short) — A gentle valley walk with streams and tame deer, quiet in winter
  - Hours: Public park, always open
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Source: https://miyajima.or.jp/english/event/calendar04.html

## Shirahama

**Fit:** Would mean a change — About 3 h 30 from Kyoto: two nights here means two fewer somewhere else

**Prefecture:** Wakayama

### Tags

- onsen
- food
- gentle

### Known for

- **Sakinoyu**: One of Japan's oldest baths, open to the sky on the rocks, with the waves breaking just below.
- **The white beach and the cliffs**: Shirarahama's pale sand, the sea cave under Sandanbeki by lift, and Senjojiki's wide rock shelves at sunset.
- **Toretore Ichiba**: A huge fish hall with tuna cutting, grilled fish and cooked set meals.
- **Kumano**: A day inland to Hongu Taisha, the great torii at Oyunohara and the river bath at Kawayu.

### Window

- The mildest coast on mainland Japan in winter. Snow is rare and the days are often sunny.
- A favourite New Year escape from Osaka, so hotel dinner plans for 31 Dec are scarce and dearer.
- Adventure World is open every day from 28 Dec to 4 Jan.
- Three hours each way from Kyoto; five or more from Tokyo.

**Feel:** A seaside spa town of big hotels on a white bay: warm sea air and steam rising from the rocks. Easy, sunny and a little old-fashioned.

**Getting there:** About 3 h from Kyoto and 2 h 20 from Shin-Osaka on the Kuroshio limited express, which runs along the coast.

**For Fernanda:** A big hotel with a lift and a private or in-room bath is the easy choice. The seaside bath and the rock shelves are wet and uneven, so hold someone's arm. Kinan Hospital in Tanabe, a regional perinatal centre, is 20–25 minutes by taxi.

**Region:** kansai

**Do line:** Seaside baths, white sand, winter sun

### Aliases

- Shirahama Onsen
- Wakayama

### Verdict

**Headline:** A sunny seaside spa on a white-sand bay.

**Text:** One of Japan's oldest baths sits open to the sky on the rocks, with waves breaking below, beside a white beach on the mildest coast of mainland Japan. It is three hours from Kyoto and five or more from Tokyo, so it fits only a route built around it.

**Route:** kii-coast-classic

### How long to stay and what is on

**Best:** 3 nights (4 with a Kumano day)

#### Date notes

- 28 Dec–4 Jan: Adventure World is open every day, 10:00–17:00.
- Late Dec to 3 Jan: Toretore Ichiba is at its busiest. Go when it opens.
- December to February: Sennin-buro, the giant river bath at Kawayu, is dug each winter. The 2026–27 dates are not out yet.
- 1–3 Jan: first-visit crowds at Kumano Hongu Taisha. A weekday in late December is calmer.
- Engetsu island: the sun sets through the arch only around the equinoxes. In winter it drops to one side, which is still a fine sunset.
- Sakinoyu's hours change with the season and it can close for upkeep. Check before you go.
- The way out: about 2 h 20 to Shin-Osaka and 3 h to Kyoto. Tokyo is five hours or more.

#### Nights

##### 1

**Why:** A sunset and a bath, then a long train back. Shirahama is three hours from Kyoto, so one night is mostly travel.

**Longer:** A second night gives a whole day for the cliffs, the market and Sakinoyu.

**Shorter:** It is too far for a day trip from Kyoto.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** Mostly train.

##### 2

**Why:** One full day on the coast: Toretore market when it opens, Sandanbeki's cave by lift, Senjojiki's rock shelves, the beach and an afternoon soak. Two hotel dinners.

**Longer:** A third night adds Adventure World or a slow day.

**Shorter:** One night is a long way to go for one evening.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** A full day and two good evenings.

##### 3

**Why:** The coast, an easy day (Adventure World, the beach, the hotel's baths) and room to stretch out. Good for Carlos and Nadir; quieter for João and Carolina.

**Longer:** A fourth night brings in Kumano: Hongu Taisha, the great torii at Oyunohara and the river bath at Kawayu.

**Shorter:** Two nights keeps the coast and loses the easy day.

**Idle:** some

**Note:** One slow day.

##### 4

**Why:** The coast, a Kumano day by hired van (about three hours on the road in all) and a slow day. Across 28 Dec–1 Jan it also keeps everyone off the trains on the peak days. Fernanda may prefer the beach and the hotel bath on the Kumano day; Hongu has 158 stone steps.

**Longer:** A fifth night is another beach day.

**Shorter:** Three nights drops Kumano or the slow day.

**Idle:** some

**Note:** If it spans New Year's Eve, the 31st is slow on purpose.

##### 5

**Why:** Too long unless the point is rest. The town is small and quiet in winter.

**Longer:** Nothing to add.

**Shorter:** Four nights already holds the coast and Kumano.

**Idle:** real

**Note:** Two idle days.

### Places to visit (8)

- **Sakinoyu seaside bath** (onsen, main) — An ancient open-air bath on the rocks with waves breaking just below; one of Japan's oldest
  - Hours: Winter 10:00–15:00, closed Wednesdays (¥420); New Year hours to confirm. Rocks are wet, so a hand to hold
  - For Fernanda: moderate
  - Source: https://en.wikivoyage.org/wiki/Shirahama
- **Engetsu island at sunset** (nature, short) — A rock arch offshore; on some winter evenings the sun sets through the hole
  - Hours: Open-air viewpoint, always open
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Source: https://en.wikivoyage.org/wiki/Shirahama
- **Sandanbeki cliffs and cave** (nature, short) — 50 m sea cliffs, with a lift down to a sea cave where the waves boom
  - Hours: Paid entry (about ¥1,300); New Year hours to confirm
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Source: https://en.wikivoyage.org/wiki/Shirahama
- **Senjojiki rock terraces** (event, main) — Wide flat sandstone shelves over the sea, best in late afternoon light
  - Hours: Free and always open; uneven rock, keep back from the edge
  - For Fernanda: moderate
  - Source: https://en.wikivoyage.org/wiki/Shirahama
- **Toretore Ichiba fish market** (market, short) — A huge seafood hall with tuna cutting, grilled fish and cooked set meals
  - Hours: Usually open with extended hours over the holidays; busiest from the last week of December to 3 Jan
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Source: https://banzokubiology.com/toretore-nenmatsu-konzatsu/
- **Adventure World** (garden, short) — A zoo, safari and marine park, popular with families (the pandas returned to China in 2025)
  - Hours: Closing days vary by season; check the official calendar for 31 Dec – 1 Jan
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Source: https://www.aws-s.com/en/guide/opening_hours/
- **Kumano Hongu Taisha and Oyunohara** (sight, main) — The head shrine of the Kumano pilgrimage and the 34 m great torii on the river flat where the shrine once stood
  - Hours: Shrine open; 158 stone steps to the main hall, so she may prefer Oyunohara. About 2.5 h by bus, or about 1.5 h by hired van
  - For Fernanda: moderate
  - Source: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e4953.html
- **Sennin-buro at Kawayu Onsen** (onsen, main) — A giant open-air bath dug into the river each winter, where hot water rises through the riverbed; swimsuits, free
  - Hours: Runs December to February, 06:30–22:00 (2026–27 dates not yet published)
  - For Fernanda: moderate
  - Source: https://visitwakayama.jp/en/attractions/detail_534.html

## Matsue and Izumo

**Fit:** Would mean a change — About 4 h from Kyoto: two nights here means two fewer somewhere else

**Prefecture:** Shimane

### Tags

- onsen
- museums
- gentle

### Known for

- **Matsue Castle**: One of the twelve original keeps, black-timbered, with Lake Shinji from the top floor. Open every day of the year.
- **The Adachi garden**: Ranked Japan's finest garden for over twenty years, seen through the museum's windows like paintings, with Yokoyama Taikan's works inside.
- **Izumo Taisha**: One of Japan's oldest and greatest shrines, with its vast straw rope, an hour away on the little Ichibata line.
- **Lake Shinji at sunset**: One of the country's celebrated sunsets, from the flat promenade by the prefectural art museum.
- **Kotatsu boats on the moat**: A slow ride round the castle moats at a heated table, ducking under low bridges.

### Window

- The castle is open every day, New Year included, and the Adachi Museum says the same; the history museum and the Lafcadio Hearn house close for New Year.
- Izumo Taisha takes its big crowds on 1–3 Jan, so 30 Dec is the calm day to go.
- Winter here is grey and damp more often than snowy; snow on the Chugoku mountains can delay the Yakumo.
- About 2 h 30 from Okayama, so about four hours from Kyoto or Hiroshima.

**Feel:** A small castle town between a lake and a lagoon: willow-lined moats, samurai lanes and a sunset people travel for. Quiet, unshowy, and the right size for a slow New Year.

**Getting there:** The Yakumo limited express from Okayama, about 2 h 30 over the mountains. Okayama is 45 minutes from Shin-Osaka and 35 from Hiroshima by shinkansen.

**For Fernanda:** Flat lakeside and moat walks, a heated boat, and lakeside onsen inns with private baths. The castle keep has steep wooden stairs, so the grounds may be enough for Fernanda. Matsue Red Cross Hospital, with a perinatal centre that answers at night and over New Year, is about ten minutes away by taxi.

**Region:** west

**Do line:** Castle, great garden, grand shrine, lake sunsets

### Aliases

- Izumo
- Izumo Taisha
- Adachi Museum
- Shimane

### Verdict

**Headline:** A quiet castle town between a lake and a lagoon.

**Text:** Willow-lined moats you ride in a heated boat, an original black-timbered castle keep, the Adachi garden framed by the museum's windows, and Izumo Taisha, one of Japan's oldest shrines, an hour away. It is about four hours from Kyoto across the mountains, so it fits only a western route.

**Route:** west-castles-inland-sea

### How long to stay and what is on

**Best:** 3 nights (4 over New Year)

#### Date notes

- Every day: Matsue Castle and the Adachi Museum of Art are open, New Year included.
- 29 Dec–1 Jan: the Matsue History Museum is closed; the Hearn house and museum close for New Year too.
- 30 Dec: the last calm day at Izumo Taisha before the New Year crowds.
- 31 Dec: New Year's Eve dinner at the inn; most restaurants in town close.
- 1–3 Jan: Izumo Taisha is at its busiest; go early or not at all.
- Winter: the Horikawa kotatsu boats run, but check 31 Dec and 1 Jan; they stop in strong wind or high water.

#### Nights

##### 1

**Why:** An evening by the lake and one morning: the castle, or the sunset if the train is on time. The Yakumo takes 2 h 30 each way, so one night barely repays the trip.

**Longer:** A second night gives a full day for the Adachi garden or Izumo Taisha.

**Shorter:** A single night is a long way to go for a sunset.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** Mostly train.

##### 2

**Why:** One full day: the castle, Shiomi Nawate and a kotatsu boat in the morning, the Adachi garden in the afternoon, Lake Shinji at sunset. Izumo Taisha has to wait.

**Longer:** A third night adds Izumo Taisha and its warigo soba, an hour away on the Ichibata line.

**Shorter:** One night is a sunset and a train.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** Full; the journeys in and out are long.

##### 3

**Why:** Two full days: the Adachi garden on one, Izumo Taisha on the other, the castle and the moats in between. The right length outside New Year.

**Longer:** A fourth night gives an easy day after the long arrival, or an afternoon at Tamatsukuri Onsen, 20 minutes away.

**Shorter:** Two nights loses Izumo Taisha or the Adachi garden.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** Two good days with the long arrival behind you.

##### 4

**Why:** The New Year shape: an easy day after the four-hour arrival, the Adachi garden, Izumo Taisha on 30 Dec before the crowds, and a slow 31 Dec of castle, boat and bath before the inn's dinner. Fernanda gets a quiet day either side of the long trains.

**Longer:** A fifth night would be a true rest day with little new to see.

**Shorter:** Three nights means leaving on a peak day, or losing the Adachi garden or Izumo Taisha.

**Idle:** some

**Note:** 31 Dec is light by design.

##### 5

**Why:** Too long for a small town in winter unless the point is rest; the extra day is a bath day at Tamatsukuri Onsen or a second look at the lake.

**Longer:** Nothing to add.

**Shorter:** Four nights is already generous.

**Idle:** real

**Note:** One idle day, two in bad weather.

  - Hours: Very crowded 1–3 Jan; go on 30 Dec
  - For Fernanda: low

  - Hours: Always open
  - For Fernanda: low

  - Hours: The street is always open; the house and museum close for New Year

## Fukuoka (Hakata)

**Fit:** Would mean a change — About 3 h from Kyoto: two nights here means two fewer somewhere else

**Prefecture:** Fukuoka

### Tags

- food
- gentle

### Known for

- **The yatai**: About a hundred open-air stalls in Nakasu and Tenjin, eight or ten seats each, for ramen, oden and yakitori.
- **Tonkotsu ramen**: Hakata's pork-bone ramen, at the counters where the style began or the Ramen Stadium in Canal City.
- **Mizutaki and motsunabe**: The city's two hot pots, chicken and offal, both cooked through at the table.
- **Dazaifu Tenmangu**: The scholars' shrine among plum trees, 30 minutes away, with umegae-mochi baked at the gate.
- **Yanagawa**: A punted boat with a kotatsu through a castle town's canals, then eel steamed in a bamboo box.

### Window

- Almost every yatai is shut on 31 Dec and 1 Jan; about half are back on 2–3 Jan and most by 4 Jan.
- Dazaifu takes about two million people over 1–3 Jan; from 4 Jan it is calm again.
- Mild and dry, about 10 °C by day. The city's museums close for New Year, roughly 28 Dec–4 Jan.
- Hakata Hankyu has opened its New Year sale on 1 Jan in past years; most department stores reopen on the 2nd.

**Feel:** A big, flat, easy city by the sea where food is the point: steam from the stalls along the river, ramen at midnight, hot pot at lunch. Relaxed and mild, and a long way from the rest of the trip.

**Getting there:** Nozomi from Shin-Osaka about 2 h 30, from Kyoto about 2 h 45. From Tokyo it is five hours by train, or a flight.

**For Fernanda:** Flat, mild and compact, with taxis everywhere, and mizutaki is about the ideal meal. She may want to skip the raw squid and sashimi at the stalls. Kyushu University Hospital has obstetrics, and Fukuoka University Hospital's perinatal centre takes emergencies around the clock.

**Region:** south

**Do line:** Street stalls, ramen and hot pot

### Aliases

- Hakata
- Dazaifu
- Yatai

### Verdict

**Headline:** Kyushu's easy food city, mild in winter.

**Text:** A big, flat city by the sea where food is the point: open-air stalls along the river, Hakata pork-bone ramen, and chicken hot pot cooked at the table. It is five hours from Tokyo by train, a long way from the rest of our trip.

**Route:** winter-food-trail

### How long to stay and what is on

**Best:** 3 nights (4 over New Year)

#### Date notes

- Until 30 Dec: the yatai are out most evenings and Yanagibashi market is busy with New Year shopping.
- 31 Dec–1 Jan: almost every yatai and most independent restaurants are shut; Canal City and the station chains stay open.
- 1–3 Jan: Dazaifu Tenmangu takes about two million people; go from 4 Jan, early.
- 2 Jan: department-store sales and food halls reopen; Hakata Hankyu has opened on 1 Jan in past years.
- 3 Jan: Tamaseseri at Hakozaki shrine, men in loincloths tussling for a wooden ball; watch from the edge.
- About 28 Dec–4 Jan: the city's museums are closed.

#### Nights

##### 1

**Why:** A stopover: one evening of ramen or the stalls, then the train out. Fukuoka is 2 h 30 from Osaka and five hours from Tokyo, so one night barely repays the trip.

**Longer:** A second night gives one full food day.

**Shorter:** A single night is a long way to go for a bowl of ramen.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** Mostly train.

##### 2

**Why:** One full day: a market morning, Ohori Park or Canal City, mizutaki for lunch, the yatai at night. Dazaifu or Yanagawa has to wait.

**Longer:** A third night adds Dazaifu or Yanagawa's kotatsu boats.

**Shorter:** One night is dinner and the train.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** Full, and easy on the legs.

##### 3

**Why:** Two full days: the city's food on one, Dazaifu or Yanagawa on the other. In December everything is open; over 1–3 Jan the stalls and many restaurants are shut.

**Longer:** A fourth night lets the city wake up after New Year, or adds an easy day.

**Shorter:** Two nights loses the day out.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** Comfortable.

##### 4

**Why:** The New Year shape: arrive on the quiet 1 Jan and stay through the busy 2–4 Jan while the city reopens. An easy 2 Jan of food halls and Ohori Park, Yanagawa on the 3rd, and on the 4th the yatai back and Dazaifu calm.

**Longer:** A fifth night buys Nagasaki or Karatsu once the trains calm down.

**Shorter:** Three nights means moving on 4 Jan, a peak day.

**Idle:** some

**Note:** 2 Jan is slow; the city is still half asleep.

##### 5

**Why:** Fukuoka plus a day out to Nagasaki, about 1 h 30 with one change, or the pottery town of Karatsu. Good for Fernanda; João and Carolina may want more.

**Longer:** A sixth night is too long so far from the rest of the route.

**Shorter:** Four nights drops the day out.

**Idle:** some

**Note:** One easy day.

## Ise-Shima (Toba)

**Fit:** Would mean a change — About 2 h 30 from Kyoto: two nights here means two fewer somewhere else

**Prefecture:** Mie

### Tags

- food
- classics
- gentle

### Known for

- **Ise Jingu**: The Outer and Inner shrines in ancient cedar forest, rebuilt every twenty years: Japan's most sacred place.
- **Okage Yokocho**: The old pilgrims' street by the Inner Shrine, and akafuku rice cakes under red bean paste.
- **Mikimoto Pearl Island**: Where cultured pearls began, with ama women divers in white.
- **Spiny lobster**: Ise-ebi is in season from October to April, grilled or in a hot pot, with Matoya oysters alongside.
- **Ago Bay**: Pearl rafts and pine islands from the flat deck at Yokoyama.

### Window

- 1–3 Jan: Ise Jingu takes hundreds of thousands, with waits of up to two hours on New Year's morning; company groups follow on the first working days, so dawn is the time.
- Many restaurants close 1–3 Jan; hotel restaurants carry on, and the lobster is in season.
- Mild and sunny, rarely below freezing, no snow.
- About 1 h 40 from Nagoya by Kintetsu limited express; the reserved seats sell out on holidays.

**Feel:** Japan's most sacred shrine in a cedar forest, then a coast of quiet bays, pearl rafts and fishing towns. Calm, warm and unhurried, with some of the best seafood of the trip.

**Getting there:** Kintetsu limited express from Nagoya to Toba, about 1 h 40, or about two hours from Kyoto or Osaka.

**For Fernanda:** The shrines are long gravel walks under trees, flat but tiring; a taxi between the Outer and Inner shrines helps. Lobster and oysters cooked rather than raw. Ise Red Cross Hospital, a regional perinatal centre, is about 20 minutes from Toba.

**Region:** kansai

**Do line:** Japan's most sacred shrine, pearls, lobster

### Aliases

- Ise Jingu
- Ise Shrine
- Toba
- Mikimoto Pearl Island

### Verdict

**Headline:** Japan's most sacred shrine, then quiet bays of pearls.

**Text:** Ise Jingu stands in ancient cedar forest, and beyond it lie quiet bays of pearl rafts and pine islands, with spiny lobster in season. It is calm and warm, but it sits well south of our line of trains, so it fits a route of its own.

**Route:** fuji-pacific-shore

### How long to stay and what is on

**Best:** 3 nights (4 over New Year)

#### Date notes

- 31 Dec–1 Jan: Ise Jingu stays open through New Year's Eve night; waits of up to two hours on New Year's morning.
- 1–3 Jan: the shrines and Okage Yokocho at their most crowded; on 4–5 Jan company groups come, so go at dawn.
- 1–3 Jan: many restaurants close; hotel restaurants and the Okage Yokocho stalls carry on.
- Every day: Toba Aquarium is open, but very busy 1–3 Jan.
- Mikimoto Pearl Island has not published its New Year hours.
- October to April: spiny lobster season.

#### Nights

##### 1

**Why:** One evening and one morning: Ise Jingu at opening, akafuku, and away. A long way to come for a shrine visit.

**Longer:** A second night gives the coast: Toba's pearls and aquarium.

**Shorter:** A single night is the shrine and nothing else.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** Mostly train.

##### 2

**Why:** One full day: both shrines and Okage Yokocho in the morning, Pearl Island or the aquarium in the afternoon, lobster for dinner.

**Longer:** A third night adds Ago Bay and Kashikojima.

**Shorter:** One night is the shrine alone.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** Full.

##### 3

**Why:** Two full days: Ise Jingu and Okage Yokocho on one; Toba, Ago Bay and the Yokoyama lookout on the other. The right length outside New Year.

**Longer:** A fourth night buys an easy day by the sea, or Futami's wedded rocks.

**Shorter:** Two nights rushes the coast.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** Two good days, all mild.

##### 4

**Why:** The New Year shape: arrive on the quiet 1 Jan, keep 2–3 Jan for Toba and Ago Bay while the shrine is crowded, and see Ise Jingu at dawn on 4 Jan, the day before you leave.

**Longer:** A fifth night is a long, quiet day: pleasant, but little new.

**Shorter:** Three nights means moving on a peak day, or seeing the shrine in the crush.

**Idle:** some

**Note:** 2 Jan is an easy day after the long move.

##### 5

**Why:** Too long for this coast in winter unless the point is a seaside rest.

**Longer:** Nothing to add.

**Shorter:** Four nights is already generous.

**Idle:** real

**Note:** One or two idle days.
