## Morioka

**Fit:** Would mean a change — About 2 h 30 from Tokyo: two nights here means two fewer somewhere else

**Prefecture:** Iwate

### Tags

- snow
- food
- museums

### Known for

- **Wanko soba**: Bite-sized bowls of soba refilled until you put the lid on: a cheerful group meal.
- **Two more noodles**: Jajamen with miso paste, and chewy cold reimen; Morioka is proud of all three.
- **Kakunodate**: Black samurai fences holding the snow, 45 minutes away by the Komachi.
- **Chuson-ji**: The gold-leafed Konjikido hall of 1124 in a snowy cedar forest at Hiraizumi, about an hour south.
- **The old centre**: The castle-ruin park, the red-brick Iwate Bank of 1911, and craft shops by the river.

### Window

- Snow is likely by Christmas and the pavements ice over, so cleats and taxis.
- In Kakunodate the Aoyagi house stays open in winter; the Kawarada house shuts 28 Dec–4 Jan.
- Chuson-ji is open all year, 8:30–16:30 in winter, at the top of a long slope.
- Shops close on 1 Jan, and Morioka Hachimangu draws the whole city on 1–3 Jan.

**Feel:** A small, low, walkable city of rivers and bridges with Mt Iwate behind it, red-brick banks and old craft shops. Friendly, cheap and quiet, and an easy hub for the snowy towns around it.

**Getting there:** Hayabusa from Tokyo, about 2 h 15; 40 minutes from Sendai, about an hour from Shin-Aomori.

**For Fernanda:** Flat streets, but ice from late December: cleats, slow steps and taxis. Station hotels with lifts. Iwate Medical University Hospital, with a perinatal centre, is at Yahaba, about 25 minutes by taxi.

**Region:** north

**Do line:** Wanko soba, samurai houses, a golden hall

### Aliases

- Kakunodate
- Hiraizumi
- Chuson-ji
- Iwate

### Verdict

**Headline:** A friendly little castle town in snowy Tohoku.

**Text:** A low, walkable city of rivers and bridges with Mt Iwate behind it, and wanko soba: bite-sized bowls of noodles refilled until you put the lid on. The samurai houses of Kakunodate and the golden hall of Chuson-ji are about an hour away. It is over two hours north of Tokyo, so it belongs to a different route.

**Route:** tohoku-snow-samurai

### How long to stay and what is on

**Best:** 3 nights (4 with an easy day)

#### Date notes

- 28 Dec–4 Jan: Kakunodate's Kawarada house is shut; the Aoyagi house stays open.
- 29 Dec–3 Jan: museums and many restaurants close; most wanko soba houses stay open, but check.
- 1 Jan: shops close, and Morioka Hachimangu fills with the city's first shrine visit.
- Every day: Chuson-ji at Hiraizumi, 8:30–16:30 in winter.
- 30 Dec and 3 Jan: the busiest days on the Tohoku shinkansen; book the Komachi and Hayabusa early.

#### Nights

##### 1

**Why:** A stopover on the way north: wanko soba and a walk by the river. Morioka's charm is slow, and one night does not show it.

**Longer:** A second night gives one day trip: Kakunodate or Hiraizumi.

**Shorter:** A single night is a dinner.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** Short.

##### 2

**Why:** One full day: the old centre and a wanko soba lunch, or a day out to Kakunodate's samurai lanes.

**Longer:** A third night adds Hiraizumi's golden hall.

**Shorter:** One night is a meal.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** Full.

##### 3

**Why:** Two days out with the evenings in Morioka: Kakunodate on one, Hiraizumi on the other.

**Longer:** A fourth night gives an easy day in between, which the ice and cold make welcome.

**Shorter:** Two nights loses Hiraizumi or Kakunodate.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** Busy; the cold tires everyone.

##### 4

**Why:** Kakunodate, Hiraizumi and an easy Morioka day of noodles, the red-brick bank and the craft shops: the right pace in deep cold, with a rest before New Year.

**Longer:** A fifth night adds Koiwai Farm in the snow, or a day at Nyuto's milky baths for João and Carolina.

**Shorter:** Three nights drops the easy day.

**Idle:** some

**Note:** One easy day by design.

##### 5

**Why:** Morioka plus a third day out: Nyuto Onsen's outdoor baths for the keen bathers, or Koiwai Farm. Five nights is a lot of one small city.

**Longer:** Nothing to add.

**Shorter:** Four nights is plenty.

**Idle:** some

**Note:** One idle day, perhaps two.

### Places to visit (10)

- **Wanko soba** (food, short) — Bite-sized bowls of soba refilled until you put the lid on; a cheerful group meal

  - Source: https://www.japan-guide.com/e/e3602.html

  - Hours: Open all year 8:30–16:30 in winter; a one-kilometre uphill path
  - For Fernanda: moderate
  - Source: https://thegate12.com/spot/1035
- **Morioka's old centre** (garden, short) — The castle-ruin park, the red-brick Iwate Bank building and craft shops by the river

  - Hours: 8:30–16:30 from 5 Nov to 4 Mar; open every day

- **Geibikei gorge kotatsu boat** (nature, main) — A flat-bottomed boat poled up a limestone gorge while you sit at a heated kotatsu table; the boatman sings on the way back

## Tsunagi Onsen

**Fit:** Would mean a change — About 3 h from Tokyo: two nights here means two fewer somewhere else

**Prefecture:** Iwate

### Tags

- snow
- onsen
- gentle

### Known for

- **Mt Iwate over Lake Gosho**: The volcano white across the water from the lake-side rooms.
- **Rooms with their own bath**: Open-air baths in the room at Taikan and Bessou Kakei: short, private soaks in the snow light.
- **A New Year's Eve kaiseki**: Iwate beef, Sanriku fish and toshikoshi soba without leaving the hotel.
- **Morioka on the doorstep**: The city is 25 minutes away by shuttle for anyone restless.

### Window

- Snow is likely from late December; the village is small and is mostly hotels.
- New Year plans run dinner on 31 Dec and osechi on 1 Jan; the rooms with private baths go first.
- The hotel shuttles from Morioka station must be booked; a taxi takes about 25 minutes.

**Feel:** A handful of big onsen hotels on the shore of a lake, with Mt Iwate standing white across the water. Nothing to do but bathe, eat and look at the mountain, which is the idea.

**Getting there:** About 25 minutes from Morioka station by hotel shuttle or taxi; Morioka is 2 h 15 from Tokyo.

**For Fernanda:** Everything happens indoors, so the ice matters less. A room with its own bath lets her keep soaks short and cool. The nearest obstetric centre, Iwate Medical University Hospital, is 35–45 minutes by taxi, further than from Morioka.

**Region:** north

**Do line:** Lakeside onsen with Mt Iwate across the water

### Aliases

- Tsunagi Onsen
- Lake Gosho

### Verdict

**Headline:** Onsen hotels on a lake, facing a white volcano.

**Text:** A handful of onsen hotels on Lake Gosho, with Mt Iwate standing white across the water and baths in some of the rooms. There is nothing to do but bathe, eat and look at the mountain, which is the idea, and it sits on a Tohoku route, not ours.

**Route:** tohoku-snow-samurai

### How long to stay and what is on

**Best:** 2 nights

#### Date notes

- 30 Dec: a peak day on the shinkansen, but the hop from Morioka is 25 minutes by road.
- 31 Dec: New Year's Eve dinner and toshikoshi soba at the hotel; nothing else nearby is open.
- 1 Jan: osechi for breakfast, and a quiet Hayabusa from Morioka.
- 2–3 Jan: the hotels stay full and dear until the holiday ends.

#### Nights

##### 1

**Why:** One bath, one dinner, and the mountain at breakfast. Close enough to Morioka to work as a single night.

**Longer:** A second night gives a whole slow day, which is the point of the place.

**Shorter:** A single night is a bath and a dinner.

**Idle:** none

**Note:** Easy and short.

##### 2

**Why:** A whole day with nothing to do but soak, read and watch the light on Mt Iwate, and dinner in the hotel. Restful for Fernanda and the parents.

**Longer:** A third night is another day of the same; over New Year it pushes the move onto the busy 2 Jan.

**Shorter:** One night is the bath and the dinner only.

**Idle:** some

**Note:** Day two is slow by design.

##### 3

**Why:** A deep rest, with Morioka as an afternoon out. João and Carolina will be restless.

**Longer:** A fourth night adds nothing.

**Shorter:** Two nights is the usual stay.

**Idle:** some

**Note:** Two slow days.

##### 4

**Why:** Too long for a village of hotels; better to sleep in Morioka and come out for one or two nights.

**Longer:** Nothing to add.

**Shorter:** Three nights is already long.

**Idle:** real

**Note:** Two idle days.

##### 5

**Why:** Not sensible: the same bath and the same view five days running.

**Longer:** Nothing to add.

**Shorter:** Four nights is already long.

**Idle:** real

**Note:** Mostly idle.

### Places to visit (5)

- **Rooms with their own open-air bath** (onsen, short) — Short, private soaks in snow-light without walking on ice
  - Hours: Hotels run New Year plans
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Source: https://selected-ryokan.com/ryokan/nanbu-yumori-no-yado-taikan_tsunagi-oshuku_iwate_tohoku.html
- **Mt Iwate over Lake Gosho** (nature, short) — The volcano white across the water from the hotel windows
  - Hours: Always
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Source: https://selected-ryokan.com/ryokan/bessou-kakei_tsunagi_iwate_tohoku.html
- **New Year's Eve kaiseki** (food, short) — Iwate beef, Sanriku fish and toshikoshi soba without leaving the hotel
  - Hours: 31 Dec
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Source: https://selected-ryokan.com/ryokan/shikitei_tsunagi-onsen_iwate_tohoku.html
- **Morioka on the shuttle** (sight, short) — The city is a 25-minute ride for anyone restless on 31 Dec
  - Hours: Shops close on 1 Jan
  - For Fernanda: low
  - Source: https://selected-ryokan.com/ryokan/nanbu-yumori-no-yado-taikan_tsunagi-oshuku_iwate_tohoku.html
- **Koiwai Farm in the snow** (nature, short) — A 130-year-old dairy farm on the plain below Mt Iwate, about 20 minutes by taxi: snowy pastures, a restaurant and soft ice cream
  - Hours: Parts close in winter and its museum shuts 31 Dec–1 Jan; check what is open

## Hokkaido (Sapporo, Otaru, Biei)

**Fit:** Another trip — Another trip: a flight away

**Prefecture:** Hokkaido

### Tags

- snow
- onsen
- food

### Known for

- **Guaranteed snow**: Sapporo averages 48 cm on the ground by late December; Niseko and Furano open from late November.
- **Seafood**: Crab, uni, scallops at Nijo market; soup curry; Sapporo beer.
- **Otaru**: A canal town of stone warehouses and gaslights, an hour away.

### Window

- The Sapporo and Otaru snow festivals, the Jozankei candles and the Lake Shikotsu ice are all late January to February.
- Sapporo's Christmas market ends 25 Dec; after that it is a cold city with lit streets.
- Two flights and about a day and a half of transit; Niseko's New Year chalets for seven are the most expensive lodging in Japan.
- Polished ice on every pavement: the one hazard that matters most at 30 weeks.

**Feel:** Real winter, Siberian cold, white everywhere; the festivals that make it famous come a month later.

**Getting there:** Fly Haneda–Chitose, 1 h 35, sixty flights a day; the rail route is eight hours.

**For Fernanda:** Ice underfoot in a city at minus five; strap-on spikes and short walks only.

**Region:** north

**Do line:** Deep snow, seafood and a canal town

### Aliases

- Sapporo
- Niseko
- Otaru
- Furano
- Biei

### Verdict

**Headline:** Japan's real winter, best in February.

**Text:** The far north has the surest snow, crab, sea urchin and scallops, and Otaru's canal of stone warehouses and gas lamps. Its famous snow festivals come in late January and February, after we leave, it needs a flight each way, and Fernanda may prefer to skip the icy pavements this time.

## Kyushu (Beppu, Yufuin, Kurokawa)

**Fit:** Another trip — Another trip: a flight away

**Prefecture:** Kyushu

### Tags

- onsen
- food

### Known for

- **Kurokawa Onsen**: Lantern-lit lanes and a pass to three outdoor baths.
- **Fukuoka's yatai**: Street stalls, Hakata ramen, a flight home from the city.
- **Takachiho**: The gorge and the all-night kagura dances, by car.
- **Beppu's hells**: Boiling pools in every colour, open every day.

### Window

- The best things are car-or-bus country with one to three buses a day; two rental cars is the realistic answer.
- The Aso crater is closed; Ibusuki's sand baths refuse pregnant guests; no big festival in the window.
- Mild weather and an easy flight back to Haneda.

**Feel:** Warmer, greener, more rural; steam everywhere and the best ramen in the country.

**Getting there:** Five hours by shinkansen from Kyoto or a flight; a flight back from Fukuoka.

**For Fernanda:** The buses are the weak link; the rest is gentle. A trip of its own.

**Region:** south

**Do line:** Hot-spring towns, ramen and a gorge

### Aliases

- Beppu
- Yufuin
- Kurokawa Onsen
- Takachiho
- Nagasaki

### Verdict

**Headline:** Warmer, greener Japan, with steam everywhere.

**Text:** Kyushu is warmer and more rural: Beppu's boiling pools in every colour, the lantern-lit lanes of Kurokawa Onsen, and the best ramen in the country. The best of it is reached by car or by buses that run one to three times a day, and it is five hours from Kyoto, so it deserves a trip of its own.

## Shikoku (Iya, Dogo)

**Fit:** Another trip — Another trip: a flight away

**Prefecture:** Shikoku

### Tags

- onsen

### Known for

- **Dogo Onsen**: The oldest bathhouse in Japan, the model for the Spirited Away bathhouse.
- **Ritsurin**: A stroll garden with pines trained for three centuries.
- **The Iya valley**: Vine bridges over a gorge, closed December to March.

### Window

- The vine bridges are closed in winter; Kotohira's 785 steps are for the others.

**Feel:** Slow, rural, and far: the island Japanese visitors save for later.

**Getting there:** Shinkansen to Okayama, then the Marine Liner over the bridge.

**For Fernanda:** Stairs at the famous sights.

**Region:** west

**Do line:** An old bathhouse, a great garden, vine bridges

### Aliases

- Dogo Onsen
- Matsuyama
- Iya Valley
- Ritsurin
- Kotohira

### Verdict

**Headline:** The slow, rural island Japanese visitors save for later.

**Text:** Dogo Onsen is Japan's oldest bathhouse and the model for the bathhouse in Spirited Away, and Ritsurin is a stroll garden of pines trained for three centuries. The Iya valley's vine bridges are closed from December to March, and the famous sights have many stairs.

## Okinawa

**Fit:** Another trip — Another trip: a flight away

**Prefecture:** Okinawa

### Known for

- **The subtropics**: Twenty degrees in January, coral sea, a different culture.

### Window

- Flights each way; boats are not advised for her.

**Feel:** Another country within the country, best with a week of its own.

**Getting there:** A flight from Haneda, 2.5 hours.

**For Fernanda:** Flights and boats: not this time.

**Region:** south

**Do line:** Subtropical beaches and a coral sea

### Aliases

- Naha

### Verdict

**Headline:** Another country within the country, warm in January.

**Text:** Okinawa is subtropical, with a coral sea, twenty degrees in January and a culture of its own. It needs a flight each way and is best with a week to itself, and Fernanda may prefer to skip the boats this time.

## Names people have heard of, and where they are

- **Tokyo Disney**: Not on our list; tell us if you'd like it.
- **Hakuba**: Not on our list; tell us if you'd like it.
- **Universal Studios Japan**: In Osaka, a short ride from Kyoto: a long day of queues, better suited to João and Carolina than to Fernanda, and tickets sell out over New Year. · Area: osaka
- **teamLab**: Digital art in Tokyo. teamLab Borderless is dry and easier for Fernanda; at teamLab Planets you walk barefoot through water, and teamLab does not allow pregnant visitors in some areas. · Area: tokyo
- **Ghibli Museum**: Studio Ghibli's museum in Mitaka, Tokyo. It is closed on Tuesdays, and tickets go on sale on the 10th of the month before. · Area: tokyo
- **Himeji Castle**: Japan's finest original castle, about 50 minutes from Kyoto by shinkansen and closed 29–30 Dec. It is part of the art-and-gardens route; a day from Kyoto would take one of our Kyoto days. · Area: okayama
- **Beppu**: Boiling pools in every colour, in Kyushu, which deserves a trip of its own. · Area: kyushu
- **Nagasaki**: In Kyushu, and its lantern festival is in February; better as part of a Kyushu trip. · Area: kyushu
- **Kobe**: Kitano's old merchants' houses and Kobe beef, 30 minutes from Arima. Kobe beef is a theme of the winter food route. · Area: arima
- **Yokohama**: Sankeien garden, Chinatown and the harbour lights. It is a day out in the Kamakura route, not in ours; tell us if you'd like it. · Area: kamakura
- **Tsumago and Magome**: Old post towns of the Kiso Valley, at their best in autumn. In winter they repeat what Takayama and Shirakawa-go give us.
- **Zao snow monsters**: Trees frozen into white shapes near Sendai, only half formed before mid-January; they are at their best in February. · Area: south-tohoku
- **Mt Takao**: A hill near Tokyo with Fuji views by cable car. An extra Tokyo night could buy it, and Fernanda may prefer to ask her doctor before the walk. · Area: tokyo
- **Sumo**: The January tournament starts the day after we fly home. Morning practice at a stable is possible on 21–23 Dec, but it means 60–90 minutes sitting on the floor. · Area: tokyo
- **Kabuki**: Single acts at Kabuki-za in Tokyo last about an hour, seated, on 20–23 Dec or 7–8 Jan. · Area: tokyo
